## Share package hygiene (found at merge, 2026-09-03) - LANE 7

- **RM-337 SHIPPED 2026-09-03 (LEDGER 1323; filed and fixed the same day, merger session; LANE 7, Tier-1) - 13 tests in the SHIPPED `Share/` package fail on a clean copy because they locate files by a CWD-RELATIVE path, and `Share/README.md` claimed the package passed clean.** MEASURED 2026-09-03 three ways, all agreeing at 13: in-place `cd Share && python -m pytest src -q` on main gives **13 failed / 8361 passed / 24 skipped / 11256 subtests**; the same command in a detached worktree at the PRE-RUN commit `17b83912d` gives **13 failed / 8273 passed**; and a genuine clean copy of `Share/` into a temp dir OUTSIDE the repo gives **13 failed / 8361 passed**. The third run is the one that matters - it rules out the parent `conftest.py` / repo-root confound, which was the first hypothesis and was WRONG. **PRE-EXISTING, NOT CAUSED BY THE 1.280.0 BATCH:** a set-difference of the failure names between `17b83912d` and this run returns exactly ONE new entry, `test_roster_key_id_only_contract_rm330.py`, which was fixed in the same session by adding it to `_HOST_DEPENDENT_TESTS` (`tools/ds_share_sync.py`); the other 13 are identical across both. **ROOT CAUSE, SOURCE-READ not inferred:** `test_rune_procs_per_attack.py:99` opens the literal relative path `agents/daemon_slayer/rune_procs.py`. In the generating tree the engine sits at the repository root so the CWD-relative open resolves; in the package the engine sits under `src/`, so it raises `FileNotFoundError` BEFORE any assertion runs. This is why the failures are invisible from the main tree, where the whole DS suite is green at 10845 passed / 0 failed. **THE 13, in two clusters, neither of which scores anything:** ASCII-hygiene file checks (`test_rune_procs_per_attack.py` x2, `test_bruiser_mitigation_item262.py`) and registry-census counts (`test_conditional_block_index_s229/s230/s231/s232` `test_registry_still_125_champions`, `test_form_index_sweep_s226`, `test_max_priority_sweep_s227` x2, `test_nested_hp_parser_s223`, `test_post_parser_sweep_s225`, `test_unit_variants_s224`). **THE DOC HALF IS ALREADY FIXED and the code half is not:** `Share/README.md` read "Measured 2026-09-01 on a clean copy at engine 1.279.0: 8286 passed, 0 failed" and "This package now passes clean". That is false for the shipped artifact and the README's own stated purpose is to tell a recipient what they will see, so it was corrected in place to the measured 13 with the cause named. **DO NOT FIX THIS BY EXCLUDING THE 13 FROM THE MIRROR.** `_HOST_DEPENDENT_TESTS` is for tests whose SUBJECT is the host (they import `core.daemon_slayer_client`, or read a `dashboard/` contract). These have no host subject at all - they check the engine's own files and its own registries, they SHOULD run in the package, and they would pass there if they resolved paths correctly. Excluding them would delete real coverage from the shipped artifact to make a number look better. **ACCEPTANCE:** each of the 13 locates its target relative to its own `__file__` rather than the process CWD; a clean copy of `Share/` outside the repo then reports 0 failed; and a guard asserts no test under `agents/daemon_slayer/tests/` opens a path that starts with `agents/`. The guard is the load-bearing half - without it the next such test reintroduces the class silently, exactly as these did. **Tier-1: test-side path resolution only, no engine change, no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump.** **CAUTION:** the fix must be verified from a clean copy OUTSIDE the repo, never from `cd Share` inside it, and never from the main tree, where all 13 pass for the wrong reason. **RESOLVED 2026-09-03, verified the way this row demanded.** A clean copy of `Share/` in a temp dir OUTSIDE the repo now reports **8376 passed / 0 failed / 24 skipped / 11256 subtests**, against the recorded baseline of **13 failed / 8361 passed / 24 skipped** taken the same way; 8361 + 13 + 2 new guard tests = 8376, so nothing was lost or skipped to reach zero. Sixteen literal sites across 14 files were anchored on `_DS_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]` - the 13 the row names, plus two the row did NOT name and that a sibling sweep found: `test_akshan_passive_carry_rm42.py:323` opens `server.py` the same broken way but is excluded from the mirror by `_HOST_DEPENDENT_TESTS`, so it never showed up in the Share count while being the identical defect in the main tree, and `test_geometry_item232.py` was already correct (it joined `_REPO_ROOT`) but still passed an `agents/`-prefixed literal, which the guard forbids outright so that it can carry ZERO exemptions. **THE GUARD, the load-bearing half:** `agents/daemon_slayer/tests/test_no_cwd_relative_paths.py` parses every `.py` under that directory with `ast` and fails on any `str` Constant beginning with `agents/` or `agents\`. It is AST-based rather than a text grep so that a `#` comment naming such a path stays legal, and docstrings are exempted STRUCTURALLY (the leading `ast.Expr` of a module/class/function body, skipped by node identity) rather than by a name allowlist - the guard's own docstring spells the banned path out three times and is the live proof that exemption works. It carries NO per-file skip list. Its forbidden prefixes are ASSEMBLED by concatenation from `_TOP_PACKAGE = "agents"`, because written as plain literals they would be the guard's own first two violations; do not "simplify" that back. **PROVEN NON-VACUOUS FOUR WAYS, not asserted:** it goes RED naming the file when a violation is planted and GREEN when removed, in the repo AND inside the shipped package copy (where it resolves its scan root to the temp dir, so it is not repo-root-dependent); it asserts it scanned more than 50 files (it finds 438) so a broken glob cannot read as green; it asserts it finds ITSELF in its own scan set; and it reports a `SyntaxError` as a failure rather than swallowing an unparsable file into a silent pass. **TWO RESIDUALS, deliberately not fixed here and worth knowing before this row is cited as total coverage.** (1) The guard keys on the `agents/` prefix because that is what the acceptance specified, so a CWD-relative open with no prefix, e.g. `open("champion_block_index.json")`, would still slip through; none exists today. (2) A path assembled by concatenation, `"agents" + "/x.json"`, splits the constant and evades the prefix test - the guard's own `_TOP_PACKAGE` line demonstrates that bypass. Both are narrower than the class actually observed and neither is reachable by accident, but neither is closed. **`Share/README.md` was corrected a SECOND time in the same pass** - the previous session had already rewritten it from "passes clean" to "expect 13", which this fix made stale in the opposite direction. Three further drift defects were found in that same section while rewriting it and fixed: a garbled duplicated clause ("told you to expect two, and this section told you to expect two failures"), a "The 21 skips are deliberate" line contradicting the same document's own header line of 24, and a mirrored-test recital of "356 of 429, drops 73" against a measured 362 of 437, drops 75. **HARDENED THE SAME DAY AFTER AN ADVERSARIAL PASS, which found four bypasses and one false claim.** A verifier ran a 33-case placement matrix: the guard caught all 21 real placements (nested function, class body, dict key AND value, list, tuple, set, f-string prefix, decorator arg, kwarg default, lambda default, comprehension, walrus, annotated attr, implicit concat, raw string, direct `open()`) and correctly stayed silent on comments and all four docstring kinds. But it slipped on four shapes, now all closed by a `_is_repo_root_relative` normaliser: a **`bytes` literal** (`b"agents/x"` - the worst of the four, because `open()` accepts a bytes path so it is a WORKING CWD-relative open, and `isinstance(value, str)` skipped it entirely), a leading `./`, any number of `../`, and a case variant (`Agents/x`, which resolves on this case-insensitive filesystem). **AND THE GUARD'S OWN DOCSTRING CARRIED AN UNBACKED CLAIM OF EXACTLY THE KIND THIS REPO KEEPS PAYING FOR.** It read "This module's own docstring is the live proof: it spells the banned path out three times above and the guard stays green." It is not proof: the test is `startswith` and the docstring's VALUE begins "RM-337 guard", so it never reaches the exemption. The verifier disabled the exemption entirely and re-scanned both trees - still zero hits - proving the exemption was load-bearing NOWHERE. The merger had repeated that same false reading in chat before the verifier caught it. Sentence replaced with the measured truth, and the exemption is now pinned by nine direct `GuardBehaviourTests` that scan fixture SOURCE rather than relying on whatever happens to be on disk - including the one case no repo file exercises, a docstring whose value actually STARTS with the banned prefix. Guard is 11 tests; reverting the normaliser to the naive `startswith` turns 6 of them red, so the hardening is not vacuous. **RESIDUALS NOW TWO, DOWN FROM SIX:** an unprefixed relative open (`open("champion_block_index.json")`) and a concatenated `"agents" + "/x.json"` which splits the constant. **ONE MORE VERIFIER CORRECTION worth keeping:** "the only remaining `agents/` hits are the guard's docstring" is FALSE as stated - there are 57 textual hits in the repo tests dir and 54 in the mirror, nearly all prose and `#` comments such as `parents[1]   # agents/daemon_slayer`. The defensible claim is the AST one: zero string CONSTANTS begin with the prefix, docstrings included. A textual grep and an AST sweep are different questions and the sloppy phrasing conflated them.

## Daemon Slayer refill (lane 5 research, 2026-09-02) - LANE 6

_Six rows, each PROBED live or in-process this run and then put through an independent refutation pass. Two were cut down by that pass and one was killed outright - the killed one is kept below as RM-332 so it is never re-filed as a defect. Read the REFUTED blocks before working any row here._

- **RM-329 SHIPPED 2026-09-03 at ENGINE 1.280.0 (LEDGER 1322; filed 2026-09-02, lane 5 research refill; LANE 6, Tier-1) - the EHP-family rankers carry NO mode-provenance note at all, so an unrecognised `mode` silently returns an unfiltered pool with nothing in `notes` to say so.** PROBED LIVE, `/rank-tank` Leona L11 top3: `mode="ARAM"` returns ids `['3143','3083','3084']` with `notes` `['enemy mix: AD 50% / AP 50% / true 0%', 'ARAM aramDamageTaken=1.050 folded into all EHP values']`; `mode="ARAMM"` returns `['223069','3143','3083']` with `notes` `['enemy mix: AD 50% / AP 50% / true 0%']` - the pool changed and nothing says why. Item `223069` "Void Immolation" carries DDragon `maps` `{'11': False, '12': True, '22': False, '30': True}` and is absent from the SR and ARAM top-5 while present under ARENA and NEXUSBLITZ, so it is a mode-legal item leaking into a mode-illegal answer. Same shape PROBED on `/rank-bruiser` (`['2503','3143','3083']` -> `['2503','223069','444637']`), `/rank-assassin` (`['3078','3100','3508']` -> `['446632','3078','3100']`), `/rank-onhit` and `/rank-mage`; `/rank-enchanter` returns identical ids for both spellings. `/hps` Soraka L11 moves its ability heal throughput 23.49 (ARAM) / 20.09 (ARAMM) / 8.84 (SR) and annotates none of it. The contrast routes DO annotate: `/rank` returns `['mode=ARAMM not in MODE_MAP_ID - no per-mode item filter applied']` and `/ehp` returns `['mode=ARAMM - modifier table not plugged in for this mode']`. **THE FILED FRAMING WAS CORRECTED BY THE REFUTATION PASS - do not restore it.** This was first written as "the archetype routes DROP the fallback note". They do not: `agents/daemon_slayer/ehp.py` contains **zero** occurrences of `MODE_MAP_ID`, and its note block at `ehp.py:4095` never emits a mode note for ANY mode, valid or invalid. Nothing is dropped on the unknown-mode branch specifically - the EHP-family rankers have no mode-provenance note lane in the first place. The channel is structurally present (`EhpRankResult.notes` exists at `ehp.py:3114` and is populated), so this is an addition, not a repair. **SEVERITY IS CAPPED AND THE CAP IS MEASURED:** no live caller can currently reach the unfiltered pool - `core/defensive_picks.py:390` passes `my_mode` defaulting to `"SR"`, and `dashboard/routes_build_plan.py:296` upper-cases a queue-id-derived value from the fixed set at `web/js/panels/champ_select.js:1762-1767`. This is internal-consistency hardening, NOT a live coaching regression. **WHY NOTHING PINS IT:** `agents/daemon_slayer/tests/test_rank_mode_legality_p1l23.py:423` `test_rank_items_garbage_mode_returns_result_with_note` asserts exactly this contract - and a grep of that file for `rank_tank|rank_bruiser|rank_mage|rank_assassin|rank_enchanter|rank_onhit|compute_hps` returns **0**. The name promises the garbage-mode-note contract; the body covers one of eight scorers. Classic `reference_guard_domain_narrower_than_its_name`. **A COUNTER-ARGUMENT EXISTS AND MUST BE ANSWERED, NOT IGNORED:** `agents/daemon_slayer/tests/test_mode_case_scorer_parity_rm325.py:286` class `ModeNoteProvenanceTests` argues in its own docstring that "A false provenance line is worse than none." That argues against a WRONG note, not for silence - but whoever works this row should make the added note say only what it can prove. **ACCEPTANCE:** parametrized over the 7 uncovered routes, `POST {"champion":<c>,"level":11,"mode":"NOT_A_REAL_MODE"}` asserts `any("not in MODE_MAP_ID" in n or "modifier table not plugged in" in n for n in body["notes"])`. Red at HEAD on all 7, already green on `/rank` and `/ehp`. **Tier-1 if the note is additive only and no pool changes; it does NOT bump `ENGINE_VERSION` for a notes-only addition, but `rank.py`/`hps.py`/`server.py` ship in the DS package so the Share mirror still syncs.** Re-tier to Tier-2 if you also decide to REJECT an unknown mode, which is the separate deferred decision below.

- **RM-330 SHIPPED 2026-09-03 at ENGINE 1.280.0 (LEDGER 1322; filed 2026-09-02, lane 5 research refill; LANE 6, Tier-2 if hardened, DECIDE-THEN-ACT) - the DS HTTP surface resolves `champion` through a tolerant resolver but takes ROSTER keys (`enemies`, `ally_grant_champions`, `granter_resists`) as exact canonical ids, so a Riot DISPLAY name silently contributes nothing.** PROBED LIVE and independently re-probed, `/ehp` Jinx L11 SR `items ["3031","3006","3094","3072"]`: `enemies ["Chogath"]` returns `cc_blended_ehp` **2260.9939064678956**; `["Cho'Gath"]` returns **3014.6585419571943**, which is the no-enemy identity - the enemy vanished. `["Sion"]` **2449.41006534022** against `["sion"]` **3014.6585419571943**; `["MonkeyKing"]` **2763.4369967940947** against `["Wukong"]` **3014.6585419571943**. The normalizing site is `agents/daemon_slayer/server.py:398` `_resolve_champion_id`, called at 30 sites for `champion`/`champ_a`/`champ_b`; the roster keys bypass it. **THIS ROW IS DELIBERATELY NOT FILED AS A DEFECT, BECAUSE TWO INDEPENDENT CHECKS SAY THE SILENT SKIP IS INTENDED.** (1) The contract is WRITTEN DOWN and it names this row's own example: `dashboard/routes_cc_blended_ehp_threat.py:33-38` documents `ally` as "comma-separated canonical DDragon ids (\"Annie\", \"Garen\", \"MonkeyKing\")" and states "unknown champions are silently skipped (mirrors compute_ehp's fail-soft contract on enemy_champions and compute_cc_pressure)". (2) Two canonicalizers already exist for exactly this bridge - `core/archetype_picks.py:400` `canonical_champion_id`, written "to bridge Live Client display names to the DS engine's canonical-id registries", and `core/daemon_slayer_client.py:2103` `_canon_champ_key` (RM-95) - and the champ-select path uses them (`web/data/champions_index.json` maps `byId["62"]` to `MonkeyKing` and `byId["31"]` to `Chogath`, so `resolveChampNames` at `web/js/panels/cc_blended_ehp_threat.js:47` already emits canonical ids). **THE LIVE-DEFECT CLAIM WAS REFUTED TWICE - do NOT re-file it as one.** The originally cited chain (`dashboard/_liveclient.py:211` display names -> `enemy_comp` -> `core/cc_blended_ehp_context.py` -> a DS roster key) is FALSE at the last hop: `core/cc_blended_ehp_context.py` contains ZERO occurrences of `enemies=`, `ally_grant_champions`, `daemon_slayer_client` or `:8860` - it calls `compute_cc_pressure` in-process and returns a prompt string. The fallback chain proposed by the refutation pass was then also measured and does NOT bite: `dashboard/routes_state.py:1006` `_resolve_enemy_champions` really does return raw `p.get("championName")` display names while the same file canonicalizes `champion` at `:844` and `:1255`, but its two consumers at `:954` and `:975` are spelling-INSENSITIVE - `build_capability_gap('Jinx', ['Chogath','Sion'], 'SR')` and the same call with `["Cho'Gath",'Sion']` serialize BYTE-IDENTICAL (`applies=True`, `top_gap='anti_tank'`, same verdict), and `['MonkeyKing']` vs `['Wukong']` likewise. **So: a real asymmetry on the engine's HTTP contract, with NO demonstrated live consumer.** **THE DECISION, which is why this is DECIDE-THEN-ACT:** either (A) fold a canonical-id resolver over the roster keys the way RM-325 folded `canonical_mode`, making the engine tolerant and the documented "canonical ids only" line obsolete - Tier-2, bumps `ENGINE_VERSION`, syncs the Share mirror, and needs the RM-325 discipline of asserting the two source values DIFFER before the fix; or (B) keep the id-only contract, propagate that documented sentence to the routes that lack it, and add a guard asserting every roster key is documented id-only. **B is the cheaper and better-evidenced default** - one contract is already written and two client-side canonicalizers already exist, so option A would make a third normalizer. Do not silently pick A because it looks like a bug fix. **ACCEPTANCE for (A):** `compute_ehp(..., enemies=["Cho'Gath"]).cc_blended_ehp == compute_ehp(..., enemies=["Chogath"]).cc_blended_ehp`, red at HEAD, plus the same over `ally_grant_champions` and `granter_resists` keys. **ACCEPTANCE for (B):** a test asserting each roster-key parse site has the id-only sentence in its route docstring, plus one asserting an unknown roster name is skipped rather than raising.

- **RM-331 SHIPPED 2026-09-03 at ENGINE 1.280.0 (LEDGER 1322; filed 2026-09-02, lane 5 research refill; LANE 6, Tier-2) - the Arena augment lookup is exact-match on `apiName`, so a DISPLAY name is silently dropped and scores identically to passing no augment at all.** PROBED LIVE and independently re-probed, one Jinx L13 body (`items ["3031","3006","3094"]`, `mode "ARENA"`, `target_armor 80`, `target_mr 50`), five spellings of one augment: `["TheBrutalizer"]` returns `weighted_dps` **64.5571076388889**; `[82]` (the numeric id) returns **64.5571076388889**; `["The Brutalizer"]` returns **58.457107638888886**; `["thebrutalizer"]` returns **58.457107638888886**; and the empty control `augments: []` returns **58.457107638888886**. The display spelling is byte-identical to sending no augment - a 10.4 pct DPS difference that presents as a correct answer. **Mechanism, SOURCE-READ:** `agents/daemon_slayer/data_loader.py:568` does `self.arena_augments_by_api.get(key)` with no normalization, and the miss is swallowed at `agents/daemon_slayer/augments.py:227` `except (KeyError, AttributeError): continue`, so nothing is logged and no note is emitted. **The asymmetry is what makes this different from RM-330's documented id-only contract:** the numeric-id path is ALREADY spelling-tolerant (`_coerce_str_list` stringifies, `data_loader.py:568` re-parses digits back to int), so the same parameter accepts `82`, `"82"` and the exact apiName but rejects the one spelling a human would type - and unlike the roster keys, no route docstring anywhere states an apiName-only contract. **RC ITSELF NORMALIZES TWICE ON THE CLIENT SIDE:** `coaches/arena_coach.py:264-272` builds a `name.lower().replace(" ","")` to apiName map, and `core/augment_external_source.py:445` `_norm_name` does the same. **WHY NOTHING PINS IT:** `agents/daemon_slayer/tests/test_arena_augment_e2e_p1l26.py:73-75` pins `_AD_AUG = "TheBrutalizer"` and siblings - exact apiNames only; worse, `:245-259` asserts an unregistered augment leaves the baseline byte-identical, which is exactly what a misspelled REAL augment produces, so that test cannot distinguish the two cases. **ACCEPTANCE:** assert `compute_dps(..., mode="ARENA", augments=["The Brutalizer"]).weighted_dps == compute_dps(..., augments=["TheBrutalizer"]).weighted_dps`, AND assert both differ from the `augments=[]` baseline - the second half is load-bearing, because without it the test passes when BOTH arms silently drop the augment. Red at HEAD. **Tier-2: bumps `ENGINE_VERSION` and touches the Share mirror.** Fix belongs in `DataSnapshot.arena_augment` as an alnum-lowercase alias index over `apiName` AND `name`, mirroring the existing `_norm_name`. **REFUTED IF** the operator rules apiName-only is the intended engine contract - in which case this downgrades to "delete one of the two RC normalizers, do not add a third", and should be recorded the way RM-330's contract is.

- **RM-332 SHIPPED 2026-09-03 at ENGINE 1.280.0 (LEDGER 1322; filed 2026-09-02, lane 5 research refill; LANE 6, Tier-0 NOTE WORDING ONLY) - both Protoplasm Harness rows describe a "Lifeline shield" the item does not grant; the item grants max-Health plus a heal.** `agents/daemon_slayer/_effects_data.py:2997` reads "Protoplasm Harness: Lifeline - triggered shield when dropping below 30% HP" and `:3988` reads "Protoplasm Harness (Arena 222525): Lifeline shield". PROBED against the pinned authority `data/daemon_slayer/16.15.1/items.json`, id `2525`: "Taking damage that would reduce your Health below 30% causes you to gain **maximum Health** for 5 seconds, then **heal** Health over the duration." Contrast id `6673` Immortal Shieldbow in the same file: "grants a **Shield** for 3 seconds." **THE DEFECT THIS ROW WAS ORIGINALLY FILED AS IS REFUTED - DO NOT RE-FILE IT.** It was filed as "2 of the 12 `unique_passive_key == 'lifeline'` members carry `shield=None` while their notes assert a shield, so the family roster is wrong". The roster is CORRECT and `shield=None` is CORRECT. `unique_passive_key` is a DEDUP family, not a shield family: `agents/daemon_slayer/effects.py:256-260` de-duplicates items sharing a non-empty key first-seen-wins while the duplicate still contributes its stat block, and `agents/daemon_slayer/ehp.py:2966-2972` enumerates the family in prose - "the lifeline family (Sterak's, Maw, Shieldbow, Verdant Barrier, Hexdrinker, **Protoplasm Harness**, Seraph's, Lifeline component) is a real conflict source" - so 2525's membership is the entire point. `agents/daemon_slayer/tests/test_effects_expansion.py:2842` confirms a "non-DPS piece is still deduped via `unique_passive_key='lifeline'`". Membership never implied a shield. **The guard that looked wrong is also correct:** `agents/daemon_slayer/tests/test_lifeline_target_shield_r59.py:54` hardcodes `{"6673","3053","3156"}` because `agents/daemon_slayer/_lifeline_target_shield.py:34` `ASSUME_LIFELINE_TARGET_ITEM_ID = "6673"` is a TARGET-SIDE assumption set - one canonical representative a modeled target is assumed to hold - not a census of the family, and `target_lifeline_shield` returns 0.0 for any shield-less id, so the literal cannot mis-score anything. **DO NOT "fix" that literal into a derived census** - deriving it from `ITEM_EFFECTS` would pull shield-less members into a target-shield assumption and invent EHP that does not exist. **ACCEPTANCE:** the two note strings describe a max-Health grant and a heal rather than a shield; optionally assert no `ITEM_EFFECTS` note contains the word "shield" while its row carries `shield=None` AND the item's DDragon description lacks "Shield" - that generalizes, but keep it advisory, because a note may legitimately describe a mechanic the engine models on another path. **Tier-0: comment text only, no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump.** `_effects_data.py` ships in the DS package, so the Share mirror still syncs. **Stale-comment tail worth fixing in the same pass:** `_lifeline_target_shield.py:31` says "The three ... items, all carrying an ItemShield" - the family is 12 today; the sentence is arithmetically inert but wrong.

- **RM-333 SHIPPED 2026-09-03 at ENGINE 1.280.0 (LEDGER 1322; filed 2026-09-02, lane 5 research refill; LANE 6, Tier-1 as a guard widening) - `apply_canonical_cast_rate_keys` is unreachable from the HTTP boundary, and the stranded-seam guard is structurally blind to it because the guard keys on `default is False` while this seam defaults to `None`.** PROBED: `grep -c "ult_rates\|apply_canonical_cast_rate_keys" agents/daemon_slayer/server.py` returns **0** - the route layer never imports the module and never names the flag, so no request body can arm it. In-process, flipping `ult_rates.APPLY_CANONICAL_CAST_RATE_KEYS` on `/ability-dps` at L13 with `["3115","3089","3135","6655"]` moves Kha'Zix 26.9991 to 56.1392 (**+107.9 pct**), MonkeyKing 29.9916 to 42.7546, KogMaw 59.1785 to 52.1189, while the control Ahri (DDragon id == display name) is byte-identical at 88.1013 both ways - so the seam is arithmetically real and gated exactly on the 21 champions whose id differs from their display name. **WHY BOTH GUARDS ARE GREEN - two independent invisibilities, and the second is the durable lesson:** (1) `ult_rates` functions are not in `_route_facing_entry_points()`, and (2) `_engine_seams()` filters on `p.default is False`, so a kwarg defaulting to `None` is skipped even if it were route-facing - measured, `"apply_canonical_cast_rate_keys" in _engine_seams()` is `False`. **This EXTENDS RM-118 rather than duplicating it.** RM-118's own RM-207 durable is "a seam under a different NAME is invisible to a name-keyed checker"; this is the same failure along a new axis - **a seam under a different DEFAULT TYPE is invisible to a default-keyed checker**. RM-118's wireable debt is still literally zero (re-measured this run: `STRANDED_TODAY` 5, `STRANDED_DEPTH1` 0) precisely because this seam is invisible to the thing that counts it. **ACCEPTANCE:** widen `_engine_seams()` to admit `p.default is None` for `apply_` / `assume_` / `use_` prefixed names, add `ult_rates` to the entry-point universe, then assert `apply_canonical_cast_rate_keys` is either parsed-and-passed by `server.py` or listed in `STRANDED_TODAY` with a written reason. Both branches are RED at HEAD. **Tier-1 for the guard widening alone - bumps NOTHING.** Wiring the seam is a separate Tier-2 decision that bumps `ENGINE_VERSION` and syncs the Share mirror. **DO NOT FLIP THE DEFAULT AS PART OF THIS ROW.** The module docstring records the reweight is not byte-identical (13 of 21 reorder on the ability scorer, 3 of 21 on carry), and per `feedback_default_flip_weakens_tests` a flip would turn existing measured-rate assertions into tautologies; keeping them honest means pinning the 21 affected champions' per-spell vectors on BOTH sides of the flag BEFORE any default moves. **REFUTED IF** a non-HTTP production caller (an RC coach, the build-table generator) already sets the module global, in which case the seam is reachable in production and only the route is dark - that narrows the row, it does not kill it.

- **RM-334 SHIPPED 2026-09-03 at ENGINE 1.280.0 (LEDGER 1322; filed 2026-09-02, lane 5 research refill; LANE 6, Tier-2) - `/ehp` exposes `compute_ehp` directly, carries the `enemies` CC transport that makes tenacity bite, and is the ONE route that never parses `apply_build_tenacity`, so the seam is arithmetically inert there while live on its siblings.** SOURCE-MAPPED with `ast` rather than inferred from a docstring - the three parse sites resolve to `_route_rank_tank` (`server.py:968`), `_route_hybrid` (`:1255`) and `_route_rank_bruiser` (`:1397`); `_route_ehp` is absent from that list. `inspect.signature(compute_ehp)` confirms the parameter exists with `default=False`, so the compute owner accepts it and only the route drops it. PROBED LIVE, Braum L13 `items ["3068","3075","3143","3111"]` (Mercury's Treads) `enemies ["Leona","Morgana"]`: `/ehp` returns `cc_blended_ehp` **4251.072939411764** under BOTH `apply_build_tenacity: false` and `: true`. **THE CONTROL CARRIES A PROBE TRAP THAT COST THE MERGER A FALSE READING - do not repeat it.** The first control ran `/rank-tank` with the same body and ALSO returned identical arms, which reads as "the flag is inert everywhere". It is not: `score_by` defaults to `"blended"`, and tenacity only moves the `cc_blended` axis. Re-run with `score_by: "cc_blended"` and `/rank-tank` separates cleanly - false gives `[('3053', 1819.809), ('3083', 1582.442)]`, true gives `[('3053', 3198.01), ('2525', 2154.627)]`, which both rescales AND reorders the second slot. Any acceptance test for this row must set `score_by="cc_blended"` or it passes vacuously. **A second saturation caveat, also measured:** at 3 or more hard-CC enemies `cc_pressure_fraction` clamps to 1.0 and the flag stops moving the number even in-process, so the test must sit at 1 or 2 CC enemies. Both traps are recorded in memory `reference_ds_probe_flag_needs_its_scoring_axis`. **RELATION TO RM-118: confirming-with-fresh-measurement, and an INVERSION worth recording** - RM-118's historical cases were flag-present / transport-missing; this is transport-present / flag-missing. `STRANDED_DEPTH1 = 0` stays literally true because the guard asks only whether a seam is wired on SOME route, never whether it is wired on EVERY route whose compute owner accepts it. **ACCEPTANCE:** a per-route assertion - for each route whose compute owner accepts seam S, either the route parses S or it is listed with a written reason; concretely, `/ehp` Braum + Merc Treads + `enemies=["Leona","Morgana"]` must return a DIFFERENT `cc_blended_ehp` under `apply_build_tenacity: true` than under `false`. Red at HEAD. **Tier-2: adds a route parameter and moves `/ehp` arithmetic, so it bumps `ENGINE_VERSION` and syncs the Share mirror.** **REFUTED IF** `/ehp` is deliberately the raw single-build primitive with tenacity deferred to the rankers and that intent is recorded somewhere - no such note exists in `_route_ehp`'s docstring, checked this run.

## Tooling + doc drift (lane 5 research, 2026-09-02) - LANE 7

- **RM-335 SHIPPED 2026-09-03 at ENGINE 1.280.0 (LEDGER 1322; filed 2026-09-02, lane 5 research refill; LANE 7, Tier-0) - the Share-sync gate prints a bundle path that does not resolve from the repo root, so the reader it is talking to cannot `ls` the file it names.** `tools/ds_share_sync.py:1162` prints `lolmath_ingest/{_INGEST_BUNDLE_REL}`, i.e. `lolmath_ingest/dist/daemon_slayer_bundle.json`, but `_SHARE = _REPO / "Share"` (`:67`) and `_INGEST = _SHARE / "lolmath_ingest"` (`:69`), so the real path is `Share/lolmath_ingest/dist/daemon_slayer_bundle.json`. The same omission is at `:1117`. Sibling drift messages at `:831`, `:833`, `:835` print a bare `{rel}` and invent no prefix, so this is an inconsistency inside one file rather than house style. **MEASURED COST, this run:** the printed path sent the merger to two `ls` probes that both returned "No such file or directory", from which it briefly and wrongly concluded the artifact was absent from every tree; it is present in both (main 14142305 bytes dated Sep 1 16:48, the research worktree 14076017 bytes dated Aug 14 21:13). **Second half, same line:** the remedy sentence says to "run `python tools/ds_share_sync.py` and commit Share/", but this artifact is gitignored (`.gitignore:197` `dist/`) and tracked nowhere (`git ls-files | grep -c daemon_slayer_bundle.json` returns 0), so the commit half is unsatisfiable for this specific path even though the rebuild half is correct. **DO NOT "FIX" THE DRIFT BEHAVIOUR ITSELF - it is intended and documented.** `tools/ds_share_sync.py:1149-1157` states the contract in its own docstring: an absent file is a SKIP (the clean-checkout / CI case) and a present-but-stale file is drift, cleared by write mode (`_rebuild_ingest_bundle`, `:1140-1146`). CI is unaffected, and that is OBSERVED, not merely derived: across the six most recent `ci` runs whose `check` job actually executed (33562934635, 33548150469, 33534709604, 33532329087, 33526771871, 33512825969) the step **"DS Share package in sync"** - which is the `python tools/ds_share_sync.py --check` invocation at `.github/workflows/ci.yml:308` - has conclusion `success` in EVERY one. The mechanism agrees: the bundle is tracked nowhere in git, and the only cache in that job is `~/.cache/ms-playwright`, outside the workspace (`ci.yml:267-270`), so a CI checkout cannot contain the file and `_check_ingest_bundle` returns 0 at its `if not target.exists()` guard (`:1159`). **A TRAP WORTH CARRYING: do NOT evidence this from the newest `ci` run.** The most recent one on `main` (33630963203, `ed13dd6f0`) reports `success`, but its `check` job is **skipped** - it is the scheduled nightly, which runs only `nightly-full-suite`, and that job contains no Share-sync step at all. A green run list is not proof the step you care about ran; open the job's steps. A blanket skip would delete a real signal. **Worth knowing for every lane:** this is why `--check` exits 1 in a lane worktree and 0 on main - the worktree's gitignored bundle is stale by construction and no merge fixes it; running write mode locally clears it. **ACCEPTANCE:** assert the printed text resolves - construct the message string in the drift branch of a unit test and assert `Path(printed).exists()`, or minimally assert it starts with `Share/`. **Tier-0: a message string, no behaviour change, no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump.** `tools/` is NOT part of the Share package sync, so no mirror work.

- **RM-336 SHIPPED 2026-09-03 at ENGINE 1.280.0 (LEDGER 1322; filed 2026-09-02, lane 5 research refill; LANE 7, Tier-0) - two route attributions in the `CLAUDE.md` Daemon Slayer deep-reference line are wrong, and that line is auto-loaded into every session, so it misleads by default.** Both re-derived with an `ast` route-owner map over `agents/daemon_slayer/server.py` this run, not read from a docstring. **(1)** CLAUDE.md describes `ds.hybrid apply_ad_axis_ability_damage`; the flag is parsed at `server.py:613` (`_route_rank`) and `:1444` (`_route_rank_bruiser`) and **`_route_hybrid` does not parse it at all**. The MODULE attribution is right - `hybrid.py:413` defines it - but the route implication is wrong. **(2)** CLAUDE.md describes Term A ally-granted EHP as `ds.ehp score_by=team_blended`; `score_by` is parsed at `server.py:961` (`_route_rank_tank`) and `:1394` (`_route_rank_bruiser`) and **`_route_ehp` does not parse it**, so Term A is a ranker-only axis, not an `/ehp` one. **This is the RM-118 "ownership prose lies in BOTH directions" durable recurring in the doc layer** - the same class that in a 2026-07-30 run had the rune lanes reaching MORE routes than stated and the vamp lanes fewer. Neither is a code defect; both will mislead the next agent that greps CLAUDE.md instead of running the signature sweep, which is what CLAUDE.md's own DS rules tell agents to do. **ACCEPTANCE:** correct the two attributions in place, and prefer naming the MODULE plus the parsing ROUTES rather than a bare `ds.<module> <flag>` token that reads as a route. Optional and stronger: a guard that re-derives, for each flag named in that line, the set of routes parsing it, and fails when the prose disagrees. **Tier-0, doc-only, no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump, no Share mirror.** **This row deliberately does NOT edit `CLAUDE.md` itself** - the file is CI size-budgeted under 60KB and fenced to rule / frozen-list / Settled changes, so the correction is filed for a lane that owns doc work rather than applied by the research lane. Byte delta is negative either way.

(98 percent)" is now 31097/31811 = **97.8 pct**; `:55` "three uncovered trees (715 tests)" is now **714**. **Stale cite:** `:33` says the enumerating side is `tests/test_skip_condition_hygiene.py:59-60`, but `_TEST_TREES` is at **`:72-73`** today. **Conflation, and this one is a correctness bug not just drift:** `:55` calls all three trees "uncovered", but `benchmarks` IS run in CI - `.github/workflows/codspeed.yml:51` runs `pytest benchmarks/ --codspeed` on push and PR. ## TFT PBE engine residuals (lane 8 cycle 47)

The four unbounded sites are `modes/shared_vision.py:389`, `tft/tft_vision_reader.py:186`, `vision_server/_inference.py:246` and `vision_server/_inference.py:291`. **This is a VERIFIED count, not a regex tally:** the same sweep produced three apparent hits that are DOCSTRING TEXT and not call sites at all - `core/anthropic_client.py:14` (usage example), `core/anthropic_client.py:60` (parameter description) and `coaches/_base_coach.py:735` ("Subclasses call this immediately after `resp = self._client.messages.create(...)`") - each opened and read by hand before exclusion. `tft/tft_live_analysis.py:391` and `:457` are the same defect and are ALREADY FILED as RM-302; they are excluded here rather than double-counted. **ACCEPTANCE:** an explicit per-call timeout at each of the four, each chosen against that site's observed latency rather than guessed, plus a red-first test per site asserting the configured value reaches the call; mutation-test by deleting the argument. **DO NOT** add a blanket default inside `core/anthropic_client.tracked_anthropic` as a drive-by: that shim exists for cost telemetry, it deliberately sets no transport policy, and the four sites differ in what latency is even acceptable (a vision inference is not a champ-select tick). **DO NOT** assume the SDK default is harmless because a call "usually returns" - the cost of the missing bound is paid only in the failure case, which is exactly when a coach is most visibly stuck. **COUNT CORRECTED 2026-09-01 (lane 5 research refill, LEDGER 1315; independently CONFIRMED by an adversarial verifier that reached 17 by its own AST method before seeing my number). The constructor census above is UNDERSTATED BY FIVE: the population is 17, not 12.** The five this row omits are `agents/agent7_context/warm_session.py:125`, `coach_integration/_coach.py:53`, `tft/tft_live_analysis.py:219`, `tft/tft_vision_reader.py:91` and `vision_server/_config.py:75`. Each is a real `ast.Call`, not docstring or comment text; each is tracked, production-reachable, and inside this row's own stated scope. **The repo already said 17 and a GREEN GUARD SAYS SO:** `tests/test_anthropic_base_url_pin.py:23-40` enumerates `CONSTRUCT_FILES` as exactly those 17 modules and `test_construction_files_list_is_exhaustive` (`:71`) fails on any net-new site - so this row's 12 contradicts a passing test in the tree it was measured against. **The deliberate-exclusion defence fails:** RM-302 defers only `tft/tft_live_analysis.py:391` and `:457`, the per-CALL half, and says nothing about the constructor at `:219`; and this row names `tft/tft_vision_reader.py:186`, `vision_server/_inference.py:246` and `:291` as unbounded call sites while dropping their own constructors (`tft/tft_vision_reader.py:91`, `vision_server/_config.py:75`) from the 12. That is an enumeration miss, not scoping. **THE SUBSTANTIVE CLAIM IS UNAFFECTED AND TRUE:** none of the 17 passes `timeout=`; all 17 pass exactly `api_key` + `base_url`; there is no `with_options(`, `default_timeout`, or post-construction `.timeout =` on any production anthropic client. The three docstring false-positives this row excluded are accurate - they belong to the `messages.create` sweep, not the constructor sweep - do not attack them. **Separately measured the same run:** production `messages.create` sites total 20, of which 14 carry `timeout` and 6 do not; the 6 are exactly this row's 4 plus RM-302's 2, so RM-314 + RM-302 together are EXHAUSTIVE over the call-site half. **ACCEPTANCE ADDITION:** whatever fix shape is chosen, close it against 17 constructors, and re-run `tests/test_anthropic_base_url_pin.py` - if that file's list and this row's list still disagree afterwards, one of them is wrong and it is not the guard.

Each was correct when written and sits inside the historical row that wrote it, so none is a defect on its own - the defect is that a reader grepping for the pointer gets eleven answers with nothing marking which is current. the `docs/DS_SWEEP_TRACKER.md` pointer-history records this lane nearly minting a duplicate for exactly this reason; THIS cycle read `ROADMAP.md:65` ("Next free id = RM-300"), found RM-300 through RM-313 already consumed in `BACKLOG.md`, and had to re-derive the true value by scanning every id registry. **ACCEPTANCE:** one authoritative pointer at a fixed, named location, plus a guard test asserting either that exactly one such sentence exists in the file or that the single pointer equals `max(filed id) + 1` computed from `ROADMAP.md` + `BACKLOG.md` + `docs/LEDGER.md`. - **RM-316 OPEN (filed 2026-08-31, lane 8 cycle 47; LANE 8, Tier-1) - the TFT PBE degraded-mode writer swallows its own failure with no log line at any level, so the one path that exists to tell the user "coaching paused" cannot report that it failed to say so.** `tft/tft_pbe_engine.py:562` ends `_write_status` with a bare `except Exception: pass`. That method is not incidental: `_run_safe:411` calls it with "Coaching paused - retrying" as the sole user-visible signal when a model call raises. If the write fails - a `PermissionError` from `os.replace` while the overlay holds the destination open is the realistic case on Windows - the panel keeps rendering the PREVIOUS round's advice and nothing anywhere records why. Its sibling `_write_fields:530` gets this right (`logger.error("Failed to write TFT PBE coaching data: %s", exc)`); only the degraded path is silent. Note both writers also share one scratch name, `self._data_file.with_suffix(".tmp")`. **FENCE:** RM-286 fenced `_write_status` as "already atomic, do not touch" and that fence is about its ATOMICITY (the RM-211 record) - this row is a different defect in the same method and does not license changing the write mechanics. **ACCEPTANCE:** log at WARNING with the exception, keeping the fail-soft return; a red-first test that forces the replace to raise and asserts the failure is VISIBLE in the log, mutation-tested by restoring the bare `pass`. **DO NOT** convert it to a raise: it runs inside `_run_safe`'s own handler, and an exception escaping there would replace a silent degradation with a dead worker thread.

(The verifier REFUTED an earlier wording of this row that claimed it is referenced nowhere including tests: that was true of HEAD, but false of the tree that ships this row, whose `tests/test_metrics_cache_lane8_cycle40.py` calls it at lines 380, 388, 393 and 397. The claim that survives is the one that matters: no PRODUCTION consumer.) Verified by import inspection rather than grep alone: `dashboard/routes_metrics.py:17` builds `/metrics` from `core.prom_metrics` plus `dashboard._context.read_json` and never imports this module; a live `/metrics` (200, 3689 bytes) and `/api/state` (200, 1733 bytes) contain none of `supervisor_state`, `ladder_idx`, `last_5_incidents`, `awaiting_first_heartbeat` or `policy_source_status`. So a daemon thread does file I/O on six artifacts every 5s to populate a summary with no consumer. `config/FEATURE_POLICY.md:57` asserted an "OPS tab FEATURE POLICY section" consumer that does not exist; that line was corrected in cycle 40 and now points here. **ACCEPTANCE:** an explicit decision, recorded either way - wire one consumer (the OPS tab the doc imagined is the obvious candidate, and `to_dict()` is now complete enough to serve it) or retire the module and its `main.py` wiring. Whichever is chosen, assert it in a test so the next audit reads a decision rather than an omission. **DO NOT** delete it as a drive-by: the wiring lives in `main.py`, which is on the CLAUDE.md frozen list, so removal needs the frozen-file adjudication and is lane 7's call on tree shape, not lane 8's. **DO NOT** treat cycle 40's hardening as having made it live - the module is now correct and still unread, which is exactly the `feedback_verified_claim_vs_measured_downstream` shape.

- **RM-298b OPEN (filed 2026-08-31, lane 8 cycle 40; LANE 8, Tier-1)** - **`_read_last_coaching_ts` picks the newest timestamp by LEXICOGRAPHIC string compare, and that is safe only by accident of there being one writer.** The code keeps `ts_str > latest` over the five per-mode artifacts. It is correct TODAY, verified rather than assumed: `core/coaching_timestamps.py:64-72` is the sole writer, all five call sites funnel through it (`coaches/aram_coach.py:1266`, `coaches/arena_coach.py:903`, `coaches/brawl_coach.py:566`, `coach_integration/_coach.py:719`, `tft/tft_coach_engine.py:882`), no production caller overrides `runtime_dir`, and `git log -p` on that file shows `datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat()` throughout and never `strftime` or a `Z` suffix. The sharpest edge still holds - `isoformat()` omits `.ffffff` at `microsecond == 0`, but `ord('+') 43 < ord('.') 46`, so the whole-second form correctly sorts before the sub-second one. **The invariant is contingent, not structural:** a single future writer emitting a `Z` suffix inverts the order for every same-second comparison (`ord('Z')` 90 sorts after every `+00:00`), and nothing tests or guards single-writer-ness. `str(ts)` at the comparison also coerces a non-string `ts` into the ordering rather than rejecting it. **ACCEPTANCE:** parse to an aware `datetime` and compare instants, or add a guard test that fails if any writer of `coaching_ts_*.json` emits a format other than the one `core/coaching_timestamps.py` produces. Red-first: seed two artifacts, one `Z`-suffixed and one `+00:00`, same second, and assert the genuinely-later one wins. **DO NOT** "fix" it by sorting on the raw value without the parse - that is the current behaviour with extra steps.

- **RM-298c OPEN (filed 2026-08-31, lane 8 cycle 40; LANE 7, Tier-0)** - **`MetricsSummary.stable_ticks` is a declared field that can never hold a value.** It occupies a `__slots__` entry, is documented as `int | None`, and `_read_status` assigns it `None` unconditionally with the comment "stable_ticks is NOT in status.json - would need a new supervisor field". It has been permanently None since Phase 1 Step 2. Now that `to_dict()` is driven off `__slots__` (cycle 40), it also serialises as a guaranteed-null key. **ACCEPTANCE:** either add the supervisor field that would populate it - `ops/rc_supervisor.py` is FROZEN, so that route needs adjudication - or drop the field from `__slots__`, `__init__` and the class docstring together. **DO NOT** drop it without checking `tools/run_phase2_perf.py:217`, which constructs a bare `MetricsSummary`, and note that removing a `__slots__` entry changes the `to_dict()` key set that RM-298a's future consumer would read.

Measured directly with no objective events (nobody has taken Baron): a `baron` row is present at t=1200 and t=1230 and ABSENT at 1260, 1400, 1500 and 1800; `herald` is absent at t=900 though Herald spawns at 840. So Baron is surfaced for a 30-second slice of a 20-plus-minute availability window, and the module's own purpose statement ("surface ... baron 20:00") is unmet for nearly all of the time Baron matters. `_dynamic_epic_callouts` rescues Baron only AFTER a BaronKill event exists - i.e. the first Baron, the one worth pre-warning, is the one never called. **ACCEPTANCE:** a persistent objective stays surfaced as active until a take event retires it, with a red-first test asserting a baron row at t=1500 with no events; mutation-test by restoring the drop. **DO NOT** simply widen `_OBJ_ACTIVE_WINDOW_S` - that also widens the drake active window, which is correct as it stands.

- **RM-306 OPEN (filed 2026-08-31, lane 8 cycle 44; LANE 8, Tier-1)** - **"Elder UP now - group for it" fires in games where Elder cannot exist.** Elder Dragon spawns only after a team secures Soul. `core/event_callouts.py` treats `elder` as a flat one-shot at a nominal 2100s and `_dynamic_epic_callouts` claims only `dragon` and `baron`, never `elder`. Measured: `next_callouts("sr", 2110.0, 16, 3, objective_events=[])` returns `{'tag': 'elder', 'line': 'Elder UP now - group for it', 'eta_s': -10.0}` with ZERO drakes taken by anybody. The discriminator is already in the module and already used one line away - `_SOUL_SECURED_STACKS = 4` and `_elemental_drake_counts` suppress the DRAKE row when Soul is locked but are never consulted for the elder row. **ACCEPTANCE:** gate the elder row on a soul-secured count, red-first test asserting no elder row at t=2110 with zero drakes AND an elder row once four same-element drakes are present; mutation-test by removing the gate. **INTERACTS WITH RM-307:** if `killer_team` is permanently "unknown" in production then `_elemental_drake_counts` never increments and this gate would suppress elder ALWAYS - resolve RM-307 first, or the fix trades a false positive for a false negative.

- **RM-307 OPEN (filed 2026-08-31, lane 8 cycle 44; LANE 8 + LIVE-GATED, Tier-1)** - **two RC modules read the same untrusted Live Client `KillerName` field against two different rosters, and at most one can be right.** `dashboard/_liveclient.py` builds its ally/enemy sets from `p.get("championName")` and classifies `ev.get("KillerName")` against those; `core/decision_detector.py` resolves the SAME field with `(p.get("summonerName") or p.get("riotIdGameName")) == name`. If the summoner-name reading is the correct one, then `killer_team` is permanently `"unknown"` in production and three features are unconditionally silent: `epic_buff_callouts` (drops any unsided buff by design), `dragon_soul_callout` (returns None - verified: four drakes with `killer_team="unknown"` yields None) and the soul suppression in `_dynamic_epic_callouts`. **The existing suite CANNOT settle this:** `tests/test_liveclient_objective_events.py` `_player()` sets `"summonerName": name` and `"championName": name` to the SAME string, so the fixture is parallel by construction and all its tests pass under either reading (`feedback_fixture_parallel_by_construction`). Even under the champion-name reading there is a confirmed failure mode: a drake killed by a turret or a minion execute carries a non-champion `KillerName`, so `killer_team="unknown"`, and four such drakes then produce "Drake UP now - contest or trade" when the pit actually spawns Elder. **ACCEPTANCE (live-gated half):** one real `:2999` `events.Events` capture during a game with an objective kill, recorded in `docs/LIVE_GAME_GATED_SYNC.md`; nothing in the repo carries one today. **ACCEPTANCE (headless half):** de-parallelize the fixture so summonerName and championName differ, which makes the suite able to fail under the wrong reading regardless of which reading turns out correct.

- **RM-308 OPEN (filed 2026-08-31, lane 8 cycle 44; LANE 8, Tier-1)** - **two smaller correctness defects in the same module, both measured, neither fixed in-slice because each changes panel CONTENT rather than robustness.** **(a) Duplicate inhibitor rows with an identical tag AND identical text.** `inhibitor_callouts` keys the tag on lane only (`f"inhib_{lane or 'lane'}"`) and `_inhib_lane` deliberately ignores the `T1`/`T2` team token, but BOTH teams have a top inhibitor. Measured: `Barracks_T1_L1` down at 1000 plus `Barracks_T2_L1` down at 1010, read at 1020, returns two rows both tagged `inhib_top` with the identical line and etas 280.0 and 290.0. In a base race that spends two of three panel slots on the same sentence, and nothing in `next_callouts` de-duplicates by tag. **(b) The static dragon cadence states a falsehood whenever no take has been observed.** With `objective_events` empty - which is also what an extraction failure looks like - `next_callouts("sr", 1000.0, ...)` returns "Drake spawns 5:00 - set up vision" at eta 200.0, though the drake has in fact been standing since 300s. The module cannot distinguish "no drake taken" from "no event data reached me" and resolves that ambiguity toward a positive false claim; contrast `_inhib_lane`, which resolves the identical ambiguity toward silence and says so in its own comment ("a generic callout is correct, a wrong lane is not"). **ACCEPTANCE:** (a) de-duplicate by tag keeping the soonest eta, or make the tag team-aware, red-first test on the two-inhibitor case; (b) decide explicitly whether an empty event list means "no takes" or "no data" and make the drake row honest about which, with a test per branch.

- **RM-301 OPEN (filed 2026-08-31, lane 8 cycle 43; LANE 8, Tier-1)** - **`force_scan()` read-modify-writes the polled data file with NO lock held, while every other writer in the module runs under `self._lock`.** `tft/tft_live_analysis.py` `force_scan` loads `tft_live_data.json`, clears four augment keys and writes it back; it is invoked from the hotkey / right-click path, so it runs on a DIFFERENT thread from `_loop` -> `_run_cycle`, which holds `self._lock` across the whole vision + model + publish sequence. The two interleave and the loser's fields are lost. Cycle 43 routed the write through `core.polled_json.atomic_write_json` and gave its silent `except: pass` a log line. **It did NOT make the write atomic** - the verifier caught that overclaim: HEAD already did tmp+replace, under `.json.tmp`, a name distinct from the `.tmp` the other writers share, so this call site never tore the destination and never collided. What it gains is a per-writer name, a bounded WinError-5 retry and a visible failure. The LOCK is the open half, and it was deliberately not taken: taking `self._lock` would block the hotkey thread behind an in-flight model call, which is a worse user-facing trade than a rare lost update. **ACCEPTANCE:** either a bounded `acquire(timeout=...)` with a documented give-up path, or a single-writer queue that both callers post to; a red-first test must drive the two writers concurrently and assert no field is lost, mutation-tested by removing the serialization. **DO NOT** simply wrap `force_scan` in `with self._lock:` - measure how long `_run_cycle` actually holds the lock first (it spans a vision read AND a model call), because a hotkey that blocks for the length of an API call is the defect this row is trying to avoid.

- **RM-302 SHIPPED 2026-09-04 (LEDGER 1331; body below retained as the historical record - NOTE the p99 source it names has n=0 and a substitute population was used; filed 2026-08-31, lane 8 cycle 43; LANE 8, Tier-1)** - **neither model call in `tft/tft_live_analysis.py` carries a request timeout, so both inherit the Anthropic SDK default of 600 s inside a loop that ticks every 1.5 s.** The two `self._client.messages.create(...)` sites (the `_run_analysis` fallback and the `_run_augment_select` fallback) pass only `model`, `max_tokens` and `messages`. The symptom is NOT a thread storm - `_run_cycle` opens with `self._lock.acquire(blocking=False)` and returns immediately when held, so ticks are dropped rather than queued. The symptom is silence: TFT vision coaching can stop for up to ten minutes with no scan, and pre-cycle-43 the panel showed the previous round's advice throughout with nothing saying why. Cycle 43 added a degraded marker on the FAILURE path, but a call that never returns never reaches it. Note the primary path is already bounded - `core/moon_proxy.py:28` sets `TIMEOUT_S = 8` - so only the local fallback is exposed. **ACCEPTANCE:** an explicit per-request timeout at both sites, chosen against the observed p99 of the logged `Live analysis in %dms` line rather than guessed, plus a test that a timed-out call publishes the degraded marker; mutation-test by removing the timeout argument. **DO NOT** reuse `moon_proxy`'s 8 s blindly: that bounds a LAN round-trip to a local relay, and this is a direct call to api.anthropic.com with a 500-token completion.

- **RM-303 OPEN (filed 2026-08-31, lane 8 cycle 43; LANE 8, Tier-1)** - **validation is ASYMMETRIC across the three unit lists that the same payload publishes.** In `tft/tft_live_analysis.py` `_write`, the 60-entry trait-name scrub and the `_TFT_CHAMPIONS` whitelist run over `out['shop_units']` ONLY. `board_units` and `bench_units` are copied straight from the vision read into the payload, and `board_units` additionally feeds the Haiku prompt via `_clean_units`, which applies the much weaker `_is_valid_unit` heuristic (a length and case check) rather than the whitelist. So the exact OCR failure the shop filter exists to catch - a trait name read as a champion name - is scrubbed in one field and published in the other two, to a panel and to a model prompt. The module's own prompt text asserts the invariant in prose ('These are ALL TRAIT NAMES, never champion names') for all three lists. Same block: the HP clamp is gated on `isinstance(_ohp, (int, float))`, so a vision read that returns the STRING '999' bypasses it entirely and `ops/rc_state_validator.py:117` then range-flags `tft_live_data.hp` for a value this module could have clamped at the source. **ACCEPTANCE:** one shared sanitizer applied to all three lists, plus a numeric coercion on hp before the clamp, with a red-first test per field asserting a trait name and a string hp are both rejected; mutation-test each by reverting that field to the raw value. **DO NOT** widen the whitelist to board/bench without checking the false-negative cost first: `_TFT_CHAMPIONS` is a hand-maintained Set 17 roster and a legitimate champion missing from it would be silently rewritten to 'unknown' on the BOARD, which is worse than the current asymmetry. Measure the roster against the live CommunityDragon set before tightening.

- **RM-300 OPEN (filed 2026-08-31, lane 8 cycle 42; LANE 7, Tier-1)** - **the repo has two rules enforcing CLAUDE.md's "7-bit ASCII authored content" and NEITHER can find an existing violator.** `tools/precommit_gate.py:_glyph_hits` was WIDENED to a non-ASCII catch-all on 2026-07-28, and its own docstring records why: two rules enforcing one hard rule disagreed, and the looser one ran first. But the gate scans ADDED (+) diff lines, so a file committed before the widening is invisible to it forever. The other rule, `tests/test_u2500_hygiene.py`, walks the `_ASSERTED_CLEAN` frozenset - an ALLOWLIST, which by construction cannot discover a file nobody enrolled. MEASURED 2026-08-31 over `git ls-files '*.py'` (2815 tracked, excluding `docs/_archive/` and `Share/`): **28 files, 640 non-ASCII codepoints, 34 distinct** - U+00B7 x300, U+2550 x144, U+2192 x105, U+2605 x34, U+00D7 x6, plus CJK (U+6211, U+4F9D, U+6771) and emoji (U+1F525). By directory: `coaches/` 5, `ops/` 5, `scripts/` 5, `agents/` 4, `tests/` 3, `dashboard/` 2, `tft/` 2, `core/` 1, plus `composition_advisor.py`. NONE are exempt under `precommit_gate._ascii_exempt` (which covers only `logs/`, `docs/_archive/`, `__pycache__/`, `.git/`, `data/` and a binary suffix list). Lane 8 cycle 42 fixed exactly ONE of the 28 (`core/obs_publisher.py`, 4x U+00B7) because that was the file under audit; sweeping the tree is lane 7's unit of work, not lane 8's. **ACCEPTANCE:** replace the allowlist walk in `tests/test_u2500_hygiene.py` with a census over tracked files that honours the SAME exemption predicate the gate uses, so the two rules cannot drift again; report the count it finds and fix each file. A red-first test must fail on the current tree and pass after. Mutation-test by reintroducing one glyph. **DO NOT** simply extend `_ASSERTED_CLEAN` with the 28 names - that preserves the exact defect (an allowlist cannot find #29), and it is the reason this row exists. **DO NOT** assume every hit is authored: check whether any is upstream data RC does not own (a DDragon champion name carries U+2019 legitimately, which is why the gate has an exemption predicate at all) and exempt those explicitly rather than silently.
- **RM-299a OPEN (filed 2026-08-31, lane 8 cycle 41; LANE 7, Tier-1)** - **the citation drift guard proves a line NUMBER is in range and checks nothing about what is on it, whenever the cited symbol is not backticked.** `tools/citation_audit.py:388` `_claim_tokens` states in its own docstring that "only backticked spans are mined"; a citation with no mined token is graded `RESOLVES / UNCHECKED`, which passes `tests/test_citation_drift_guard_rm171.py` exactly as a verified one does. The docs already follow the convention of naming a symbol after the line - it is just not in backticks, so it buys nothing. MEASURED this cycle on `docs/RC2_QA_CONSOLIDATED.md`: row 22 `main.js:5344 _syncAutoAccept` and row 27 `routes_scouting.py:1` both grade UNCHECKED; row 25 `routes_pickban.py:1263` was in `_KNOWN_BROKEN` as out-of-range, silently came back INTO range when this cycle's fix grew the file 1235 -> 1310 lines, and landed on a COMMENT line - correct only by coincidence. Backticking its symbol moved it to `RESOLVES / CONFIRMED` with claim token `_serve_counter_picks`. **ACCEPTANCE:** enumerate the `RESOLVES / UNCHECKED` population with `tools.citation_audit.audit()`, backtick the symbol wherever the prose already names one, and report how many moved to CONFIRMED and how many are UNCHECKED because the row genuinely names no symbol. A red-first test should assert that at least one previously-UNCHECKED row now grades CONFIRMED, mutation-tested by un-backticking it. **DO NOT** make UNCHECKED a hard failure in the same pass - the population is unmeasured and some rows legitimately have no symbol to name; measure first, then decide whether the guard tightens.

- **RM-299b OPEN (filed 2026-08-31, lane 8 cycle 41; LANE 8, Tier-1)** - **nothing caps GET query work anywhere in the dashboard; the POST side has had a cap since cycle 4.** `dashboard/_handler.py:160` `_MAX_POST_BYTES` bounds request bodies (lane 8 cycle 4, which closed "a negative Content-Length pinned a handler thread from the tailnet"), and there is no GET-side equivalent. This cycle added `_MAX_ID_PARAMS = 32` to `dashboard/routes_pickban.py` only, because that is the one module where the fan-out was MEASURED (one correlated-subquery per id, 13.5 ms each against the live 1.87 GB `rewind_history.db`). 20 route modules parse comma-separated query params (`grep -l 'split(",")' dashboard/routes_*.py`); a first pass found only `routes_pickban` running per-element SQL, and `routes_personal_vs.py` - the module `routes_pickban`'s own cache comment cites as its sibling pattern - takes a single `champ_id` and is NOT affected. **This is a CANDIDATE SET to check, not a proven defect count** - filing it as "19 more instances" would be exactly the instrument-survey error. **ACCEPTANCE:** for each of the 20, state whether its parsed list drives per-element work (SQL, subprocess, network) or a single bounded operation, with a file:line for each verdict; cap only the ones that fan out, and say how many did. :8888 binds `::` (all interfaces, measured 2026-08-31 `Get-NetTCPConnection`), so this is remote-reachable, not loopback-only. **DO NOT** add a blanket cap to `_parse_csv_ints` or to a shared helper as a drive-by: the parser's documented contract is that a junk TOKEN is dropped rather than 400ing a whole draft, and the modules differ in what a legitimate list length even is.

- **RM-299c OPEN (filed 2026-08-31, lane 8 cycle 41; LANE 8, Tier-1)** - **the two guards that already let this advisory die silently once are still broad enough to do it again.** `dashboard/routes_pickban.py:709` and `:716` wrap imports in `except Exception: return None` (both `# noqa: BLE001`). The in-line comment at `:685` records that cycle 8 found the package-root import raising `ImportError` and "the swallow below kept this advisory silently dead" - the IMPORT PATH was corrected, the SWALLOW was not. Any non-`ImportError` raised while importing `agents.daemon_slayer.cc_conditional` / `_per_spell_cc` (a registry JSON that has moved, a malformed data file - both `FileNotFoundError`/`JSONDecodeError` at import time) still returns `None` with no log line at any level, and `_compose_cleanse_advisory` simply never fires. These are 2 of the 6 `except Exception` sites in the file; the other 4 are the per-handler outer guards, which are correct. **ACCEPTANCE:** narrow to `ImportError` and log the rest at WARNING before re-raising or returning, with a red-first test that raises a non-`ImportError` from the import and asserts the failure is VISIBLE. Mutation-test by restoring `except Exception` so that test must go red. **DO NOT** narrow it as a drive-by: the broad catch may be load-bearing precisely because a DS registry import can fail on data rather than on module resolution, and turning a silent degradation into an unhandled raise inside a `:8888` handler is a behaviour change that needs its own evidence. Establish what those imports can actually raise FIRST.
