## Agent 7 warm-session residuals (lane 8 cycle 36)

- **RM-297a OPEN (filed 2026-08-31, lane 8 cycle 39; LANE 8, Tier-1)** - **`tools/lcu_agent.py` can delete the operator's own rune pages.** `tools/lcu_agent.py:624-626` reads `nm = str(pg.get("name", ""))` and then tests `nm[:3] in ("RC ", "RC:", "RC-")`. A user page named `RC Main`, `RC-smurf` or `RC:test` matches and is DELETED. The safety claim that RC "never touches user-created pages" (`lcu/lcu_rune_writer.py:9` and `:513`) does not hold on this path, and the comment at `tools/lcu_agent.py:620` asserts `"RC: " only`, which is not what the three-tuple beneath it does. The SAME block also evaluates `pg.get("name", "")` on line 624, one line BEFORE the `isinstance(pg, dict)` guard on line 625, so a non-dict element raises AttributeError and kills the whole delete-plus-POST handler. Both read in source, not inferred. **ACCEPTANCE:** import `RuneWriter.PAGE_PREFIX` rather than re-authoring a literal, test `startswith(PAGE_PREFIX)` not `nm[:3]`, move the `isinstance` check ahead of the first dereference, and add a red-first test that seeds a page named `RC Main` and asserts it SURVIVES. Mutation-test by restoring the 3-char form so that test must go red. **DO NOT** simply widen `PAGE_PREFIX` to cover the other two literals - see RM-297b: the second writer's prefix is deliberate history and unifying it is a separate decision with a live-account blast radius.

- **RM-297b OPEN (filed 2026-08-31, lane 8 cycle 39; FROZEN-FILE ADJUDICATION REQUIRED, Tier-1)** - **there are TWO rune writers and they cannot see each other's pages.** FROZEN `lcu/lcu_client.py:596` deletes any page whose name starts with `self._RC_PAGE_PREFIX` (`"RC - "`), while `lcu/lcu_rune_writer.py:1014` deletes on `"RC: "`. Neither prefix matches the other, so neither reclaims the other's slot on a page-capped account. The frozen path is additionally weaker in two ways the non-frozen one is not: it applies NO `isDeletable` guard (compare `lcu/lcu_rune_writer.py:1013`, which does), and it indexes `p['id']` directly rather than `.get("id")`, so one page row without an id raises KeyError into the method-level handler AFTER N pages have already been deleted and BEFORE the replacement POST - partial destruction with no rollback. **This file is on the CLAUDE.md frozen list; it was READ for this row and NOT edited.** **ACCEPTANCE:** an adjudicating agent that did not author the change approves or refuses, with criteria stated up front, plus tests and CI green. **DO NOT** unify the two prefixes as a drive-by: an operator with existing `RC - ` pages has them orphaned or mass-deleted depending on direction, and which writer should own champ-select at all is the prior question.

- **RM-297c OPEN (filed 2026-08-31, lane 8 cycle 39; LANE 8, Tier-1)** - **a permanently-rejected rune POST is an unbounded 1 Hz retry whose only log line is content-free.** `lcu/lcu_rune_writer._poll` records `_last_applied_champion` only on SUCCESS, so a failure leaves no dedup state and the next poll one second later redoes GET pages, N DELETEs and up to 3 POSTs, with 1.5s of `time.sleep` inside the poll - for the entire champ select, with no failure counter, no backoff and no circuit breaker. `_write_page`'s inner retry is bounded at `MAX_RETRIES` 3; the OUTER loop is not bounded at all. It is also undiagnosable: the sole WARNING interpolates `last_exc`, which is always None because the frozen `lcu/lcu_client.py` returns None on every transport, JSON and non-2xx path instead of raising - a fact the audited file's own D-CLASS comments at `:1024` and `:944` already state. A 400 on a malformed perk id, a 403 on the page cap, a 404 and a dead socket all print the same word. **ACCEPTANCE:** a consecutive-failure counter that backs the outer retry off and escalates to WARNING once, plus surfacing WHY - the LCU status code has to reach this module for the message to be worth printing, and `lcu/lcu_client.py` is frozen, so the honest options are a non-frozen probe or an adjudicated frozen change. Say which was chosen. **DO NOT** fix this by only widening the log level: a 1 Hz DEBUG spin that becomes a 1 Hz WARNING spin is worse, not better.

- **RM-297d OPEN (filed 2026-08-31, lane 8 cycle 39; LANE 8, Tier-1)** - **the two `spell_prefs.json` writers hand-roll the atomic write instead of using RC's helper.** `lcu/lcu_rune_writer.save_spell_pref` and `save_champ_spell_pref` both build their scratch file as `_SPELL_PREFS_PATH.with_suffix('.tmp')` - a name derived from the DESTINATION alone, which is exactly the shared-scratch collision `core/polled_json.py:53-87` documents and solved with a per-writer pid-plus-token name. Their retry budget is 0.015 + 0.030 = 45 ms against `core/polled_json.py:40-48 _replace_with_retry`'s 275 ms, and that module's own comment puts the Windows WinError 5 window at "usually under 100 ms", so this path can abandon a write the shared helper would ride out - and it abandons it by logging a WARNING and dropping the operator's preference. The orphan unlink runs only in the PermissionError arm, and `tmp.write_text` rewrites LF as CRLF on Windows. **Latent, not live:** both writers share `_SPELL_PREFS_LOCK` in-process and no other process writes that file, so the collision needs a second writer to appear first. **ACCEPTANCE:** route both through `core/polled_json.atomic_write_json`, and read through `read_json_dict` rather than re-implementing corrupt-file handling at seven separate call sites. Red-first test: force `os.replace` to raise PermissionError for longer than 45 ms and assert the write still lands. **DO NOT** claim this fixed until the ALREADY-BAD state is checked - look for an orphaned `data/spell_prefs.tmp` on disk before and after.

- **RM-296a OPEN (filed 2026-08-31, lane 8 cycle 38; LANE 8, Tier-1)** - **the `/api/lcu-cmd` edge validates the VERB and forwards every sibling key unexamined.** Cycle 38 (LEDGER 1304) established the shape of this boundary by measurement, and the prose that used to describe it was false: `dashboard/routes_loadout.py` claimed "the vision server also revalidates", but `vision_server/_http.py:213` hands /lcu-cmd straight to `vision_server/_relay.lcu_queue_command`, which appends the decoded dict to the pending queue VERBATIM - a whole-package grep for an allowlist in `vision_server/` returns nothing. The real second gate is one hop further on in `tools/lcu_agent.py` (name dispatch `:422`, "unknown cmd" `:1102`, per-field `int()`/`str()` coercion e.g. `:657`, per-command catch `:1693-1696`), so this edge is NOT the sole defence - but the agent's coercion is incidental to its implementation rather than a declared contract, and it is the LAST gate before a command is queued for execution against the live League client. **ACCEPTANCE:** a per-verb payload schema for the 28 names in `_LCU_ALLOWED_CMDS`, rejecting at the edge with a 400 that names the offending field, plus one red-first test per verb family asserting a wrong-typed sibling key is refused. Mutation-test by widening one schema so the assertion must go red. **DO NOT** guess the shapes: derive each from the `execute_command` branch in `tools/lcu_agent.py` that consumes it, and cite the line. A wrong guess rejects a legitimate champ-select action and fails SILENTLY from the operator's side, which is worse than the current permissiveness. **DO NOT** treat the agent's `int()` coercion as the contract - RM-296d is the case where relying on it already produced two paths that disagree.

- **RM-296b OPEN (filed 2026-08-31, lane 8 cycle 38; LANE 8, Tier-1)** - **`/api/loadout/apply` throws away the queue id, so its three pushes are structurally unpollable.** `dashboard/routes_loadout._post_lcu_cmd` reads and discards the vision server's `{"id": N}` response (`vision_server/_http.py:213`), while `/api/lcu-cmd` returns it and the UI polls the result with it (`web/js/panels/champ_select.js:74-95` `lcuPollResult` -> `/api/lcu-cmd-result?id=`). So a rune / item / summoner push that the LCU agent REJECTS can never be learned about by the frontend: the id needed to ask is gone before the response is built. This is what made the cycle-38 `ok` defect load-bearing rather than cosmetic - the route had no channel to report a downstream failure even in principle, only an upstream transport one. **ACCEPTANCE:** return the per-command ids alongside `queued` (e.g. `queued: [{cmd, id}]`), and assert in a test that a successful enqueue surfaces the id the vision server returned. Then wire ONE consumer to poll it, or record explicitly that the UI change is deferred and to which lane - an id nothing reads is RM-295b's mistake repeated. **DO NOT** change the existing `queued` element type without checking consumers first: `web/js/panels/item_build.js:445` reads `data.ok` and `data.label` only, and `champ_select.js:3366` discards the body, so today the shape is free to change - re-verify that before assuming it still is.

- **RM-296c OPEN (filed 2026-08-31, lane 8 cycle 38; LANE 8, Tier-1)** - **`/api/lcu-cmd` is absent from the control-endpoint auth list** at `dashboard/_handler.py:532`, which gates `/api/command`, `/api/input`, `/api/analyze` and `/api/loop-control` behind `RC_DASH_TOKEN`. That endpoint drives `lock_pick`, `start_matchmaking`, `lobby.kick_member` and `lobby.invite_player` - actions at least as consequential as the four that are gated. **The asymmetry is inert TODAY and the row must not be written as though it were live:** `RC_DASH_TOKEN` is set nowhere in this deployment, so the gate does not run for any path, and the honest claim is an inconsistent security posture, not an open hole. **ACCEPTANCE:** decide the list deliberately and record the decision - either add `/api/lcu-cmd` (and say why the loadout routes, which reach the same queue by the same token, are or are not included) or document why command-queue writes are deliberately outside the control-endpoint class. Assert the chosen membership in a test so the next audit reads a decision rather than an omission. **DO NOT** enable `RC_DASH_TOKEN` as part of this row: turning on a gate that no client sends a header for would break every dashboard POST at once, and that is a separate operator-gated change.

- **RM-296d OPEN (filed 2026-08-31, lane 8 cycle 38; LANE 7-or-8, Tier-1)** - **the three `push_*` flags on `/api/loadout/apply` are bare truthiness, so `{"push_runes": "false"}` pushes runes.** `dashboard/routes_loadout.py` reads `payload.get("push_runes", True)` and branches on the raw value, so any non-empty string - including the JSON-ish `"false"`, `"0"` and `"no"` - selects the push. Cycle 38 fixed the adjacent `override_summoners` guard (which admitted `[true, false]` because `isinstance(True, int)` is True) but deliberately LEFT these, because the correct coercion is not obvious: rejecting non-bools outright would break a client that legitimately sends `1`/`0`, and mapping `"false"` to False invents a JSON convention the rest of the API does not use. **ACCEPTANCE:** pick one rule, apply it to all three flags identically, and pin it with a table-driven test covering `true`/`false`/`1`/`0`/`"false"`/`""`/`null`/absent. State in the code comment WHY the rule was chosen. **DO NOT** fix these one at a time or differently from each other - three flags with three coercion rules is worse than three with one wrong rule, and the current uniform truthiness is at least predictable. Check `web/js/panels/champ_select.js` and `item_build.js` for what the real callers actually send before choosing.

- **RM-296e OPEN (filed 2026-08-31, lane 8 cycle 38; LANE 8, Tier-2)** - **`/api/loadout/apply` makes three sequential 2-second `urlopen` calls on the request thread**, so a single operator click can occupy a `ThreadingHTTPServer` worker for up to 6 seconds when the vision server is unresponsive. `dashboard/routes_loadout.py` calls `_post_lcu_cmd` once each for the rune, item and summoner commands, each with `timeout=2`, and the handler returns only after all three. The pushes are already fire-and-forget in intent - the results come back through the LCU queue, not this response - so the serialization buys nothing. **ACCEPTANCE:** measure the worker-pool size first and state whether 6s of one worker is actually a starvation risk at that size before changing anything; a fix that is not needed is churn on a POST path that champ-select depends on. If it is, either issue the three concurrently or hand them to a queue and return immediately, and assert the handler's wall-clock stays bounded when every enqueue times out. **DO NOT** simply lower the timeout to hide it - 2s is already the value the sibling `/api/lcu-cmd` uses, and diverging them would make the two paths behave differently under the same failure.

- **RM-295a OPEN (filed 2026-08-31, lane 8 cycle 37; LANE 8, Tier-1)** - **a frozen duo-synergy snapshot is now SAFE but still SILENT.** Cycle 37 stopped `core/smoothed_rates_101qq._publish` from overwriting a good snapshot with an empty rebuild (LEDGER 1303), which converts a 6-hour blank panel into indefinitely-served stale data - strictly better, and deliberately the conservative half. But the only signal that the live Tencent seam has stopped working is a `log.warning` in `logs/YYYY-MM-DD.log`. `source()` keeps reporting the seed the RETAINED data came from ("live" or "static"), which is honest about the data's provenance and says nothing about its age, and `/api/health/all` carries no duo-synergy entry at all. So a CN endpoint that breaks permanently now presents as a dashboard that looks completely normal while serving a snapshot that stopped advancing days ago. **ACCEPTANCE:** expose the refresh health, not just the data - at minimum `last_refresh_ok: bool` and `stale_for_s: float` (now minus `_LOADED_AT`) on an existing diagnostic surface, and assert in a test that a module which has kept a snapshot across two consecutive failed refreshes reports `last_refresh_ok False` with a non-zero `stale_for_s`. Mutation-test by reverting the reporting so the assertion must go red. **DO NOT** solve this by making `_publish` accept the empty snapshot again - that is the defect cycle 37 fixed, and it is pinned by `test_an_empty_rebuild_does_not_wipe_a_good_snapshot`. **DO NOT** change `source()`'s existing three return values (`live` / `static` / `none`); `dashboard/routes_duo_synergy.py` and the UI badge both read them, and provenance and freshness are different questions that want different fields.

- **RM-295b OPEN (filed 2026-08-31, lane 8 cycle 37; LANE 7-or-8, Tier-0)** - `core/smoothed_rates_101qq.coverage()` has **ZERO production consumers**. Measured: the only references in the tree are its own definition (`core/smoothed_rates_101qq.py:571`) and four test call sites (`tests/test_smoothed_rates_101qq.py:55`, `:57`, `:61`, `tests/test_smoothed_rates_101qq_lock.py:302`, `:351`); no dashboard route, no health endpoint and no tool reads it. It returns exactly the four numbers RM-295a wants surfaced (`unique_champions`, `total_records`, `unique_bot_ids`, `unique_sup_ids`), so this is not a deletion candidate ahead of RM-295a - it is a diagnostic that was built and never wired, and wiring it is probably cheaper than inventing a new one. **ACCEPTANCE:** decide between the two explicitly and record which: either wire `coverage()` into the diagnostic surface RM-295a lands (preferred - and note it is the function that exposed the internally inconsistent "65 champions, 0 records" state that made cycle 37's headline defect legible), or delete it and its tests as dead code. Verify reachability with the qualified name, not a bare grep for `coverage(` - that token collides with pytest coverage prose and with `core/rank_tier_bench`'s own accessors (`feedback_symbol_name_collision_masks_dead_code`).

- **RM-295c OPEN (filed 2026-08-31, lane 8 cycle 37; LANE 7, Tier-1)** - **the empty-publish-over-a-good-cache class is generic to every live-first / static-fallback cache in `core/`, and this row is a CANDIDATE POPULATION, not a defect count.** Cycle 37 checked exactly TWO such modules and BOTH had it: `core/smoothed_rates_101qq.py` (fixed) and `core/rank_tier_bench.py` (fixed in the same slice after its own docstring at `:15` identified it as a mirror). The shared shape is a `_build`/`_load` helper that is deliberately fail-soft - it swallows `OSError` / `json.JSONDecodeError` and returns an empty structure - feeding a `_publish` step whose only guard is a generation counter, so the ONE failure mode the builder is designed to produce is the one that destroys the cache. Two for two is a reason to sweep, and is explicitly NOT evidence that the rest are broken; several sibling caches may be immune the way `rank_tier_bench` was immune to the OTHER half of the defect (it overlays live onto a static base rather than replacing it, so an empty live result cannot erase the seed). **ACCEPTANCE:** enumerate the candidates mechanically - modules under `core/` with both a module-global `_LOADED`/`_LOADED_AT` TTL pair and a fail-soft loader - then for EACH one either (a) demonstrate the wipe with a failing test and fix it with the same guard, or (b) record why it is immune, citing the line that makes it so. Report the population size and the immune count; a sweep that reports only fixes has not proven it looked at the rest. **DO NOT** apply the guard blind to every match - a cache whose empty state is MEANINGFUL (a legitimately empty result set, as opposed to a failed read) would be broken by it, and telling those apart is the actual work of this row.

- **RM-294a OPEN (filed 2026-08-31, lane 8 cycle 36; LANE 8, Tier-1)** - `agents/agent7_context/warm_session.py` `send()` holds `self._lock` for the whole upstream Anthropic call. The lock is deliberate (the `_messages` list is shared and a concurrent send would interleave turns), so the serialization itself is correct; what is wrong is its SCOPE. `stats()` (`:207`) and `close()` (`:126`) take the same lock, so a dashboard stats read or a supervisor shutdown racing an in-flight send blocks for up to the 30s request timeout. The module docstring claimed callers "block briefly"; that claim was corrected in cycle 36 to state the real bound, which is the honest interim state, not a fix. **ACCEPTANCE:** narrow the lock to the history mutation - snapshot the messages list under the lock, make the network call OUTSIDE it, then re-acquire to append the assistant turn and bump the counters, handling the case where a concurrent send mutated history in between (either serialize on a second in-flight flag or accept and document last-writer-wins). Prove it with a test that calls `close()` while a send is blocked in the transport and asserts `close()` returns without waiting on the full timeout; mutation-test by restoring the wide lock. **DO NOT** simply drop the lock - that reintroduces interleaved turns, which is the defect the lock exists for. Deferred from cycle 36 on blast radius: it changes the concurrency model of a live supervisor component, and the audited defect (the trim producing an API-invalid leading assistant message) did not require it.

## Loop-control writer hardening (lane 8 cycle 21 residuals)

- **RM-250 FIRST HALF SHIPPED 2026-09-05 (lane 8 cycle 48, LEDGER 1333, `lane/true-audit`); SECOND HALF REFUTED - see the two notes below before reading the original body.**
  - **SHIPPED:** **SEVEN** writers now delegate to `core.polled_json.atomic_write_bytes`, so each gets the bounded ~275 ms `PermissionError` backoff, a per-writer scratch name, and scratch cleanup on every failure path. RM-250 named three (`ops/loop/loop_controller.py` `awrite`, `ops/loop/intents.py` `_awrite`, `ops/loop/steer.py` `_awrite`); **the cycle's own adversarial pass REFUTED "three is the complete sibling set"** and found four more with the identical defect trio: `ops/loop/adjudicator.py:36` `_atomic_write` (escaped notice only because `loop_controller.py:594` injects its already-fixed `awrite` OVER it, so the default path stayed bare), `ops/loop/done_sentinel.py` and `ops/loop/claude_stub.py` (which both wrote `claude.done` through the SAME fixed literal scratch `claude.done.tmp` - the exact two-writer collision a per-writer name exists to prevent), and `ops/loop/lanes.py:419` `repoint_lane_pid`, which is the worst of the seven: a WinError 5 there is swallowed by `except OSError`, so the function returns False and `launch_lane` then KILLS the worker it just started.
  - **15 tests** in `tests/test_loop_control_sibling_writers_lane8_cycle48.py`, all mutation-proven with **FIVE distinct mutations**, not one. The earlier wording here said "every one mutation-tested by restoring the bare `os.replace`" and that was an OVERCLAIM caught by the adversarial pass: restoring only the bare replace leaves `_atomic_write_bytes` bound, so the W3 and bind guards stay green. The five: (A) bare `os.replace` restored in each writer - 9 red, correctly attributed per writer; (B) `Path.write_text` restored in `loop_controller.awrite` - the LF guard red; (C) the bind fallback replaced by a plain import - the isolation guard red with the exact production `ModuleNotFoundError`; (D) a hand-rolled writer restored in `done_sentinel` - the AST guard red, naming `done_sentinel.py:81`; (E) `parents[2]` mistyped as `parents[1]` in `intents.py` - the strengthened bind guard red.
  - **The bind guard was VACUOUS on its first version and had to be fixed.** The adversarial pass proved it: `_bind_path` short-circuits on `if modname in sys.modules`, so pre-seeding `rc_core_polled_json` with a stub whose `atomic_write_bytes` is `lambda p, d: None` made the test PASS, and instrumenting `spec_from_file_location` showed only `loop_controller`'s bind ever executed - `intents` and `steer` were never exercised at all. It now evicts the bind name too and asserts the resolved writer's origin IS the real `core/polled_json.py`, which is what mutation (E) proves. Two other honest limits are recorded in the test: `test_loop_controller_awrite_writes_lf_verbatim` is vacuously green on the Linux CI runner (LF to CRLF is a Windows-only rewrite), and the scratch-name test's negative assertion is backed by a positive one requiring two writes to yield two DIFFERENT names.
  - **One deliberate behaviour change, not a regression:** `_write_then_replace` does `path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)`, so `loop_controller.awrite` and `steer._awrite` now CREATE a missing control directory where they previously raised `FileNotFoundError`. `intents._awrite` already did this, so the sweep makes the seven consistent rather than introducing something new. The cost is that a mistyped control dir now materializes instead of failing loudly; accepted, because every caller passes a module-level constant, not user input.
  - **A new import-time failure mode was found and closed in the same slice.** `exec_module` on a missing or unreadable `core/polled_json.py` raises `FileNotFoundError`, NOT `ModuleNotFoundError`, and `dashboard/routes_loop_control.py:363` catches only the latter - so the steer route would have returned 500 instead of its designed 503. Every bind block now catches `OSError` and re-raises as `ModuleNotFoundError` with the cause attached, and pops the half-initialised module object out of `sys.modules` so the next caller does not find it cached.
  - **The filed ACCEPTANCE was UNSHIPPABLE as literally written, and this is the durable lesson.** It said "each writer routes through `core.polled_json.atomic_write_bytes`". Taken as a plain top-level import that CRASHES the controller on launch: `ops/loop/loop_controller.py:36` records that the controller "is loaded by absolute file path (launcher + tests), so ops/loop is never on sys.path", and the repo root is not on it either, so `import core.polled_json` raises `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'core'` (measured 2026-09-05). Each writer therefore tries the plain import FIRST (so Mission Control, which late-binds `ops.loop.steer` at `dashboard/routes_loop_control.py:139`, keeps sharing one module object) and falls back to the absolute-path bind the modules already use for adjudicator / winmutex / slots. `test_absolute_path_load_still_resolves_the_atomic_writer` is the guard; mutating the fallback back to a bare import reproduces the exact production `ModuleNotFoundError`.
  - **A THIRD defect the row did not name (W2).** All three writers derived the scratch name as `str(path) + ".tmp"` - a function of the DESTINATION alone - so every writer of a given control file opened the SAME scratch path. `STOP` has two independent writers (`ops/loop/loop_controller.py:174` and `dashboard/routes_loop_control.py:344,587`), so they interleave: B truncates and renames the scratch while A is still filling it. This is exactly the defect cycle 24 fixed INSIDE `core/polled_json` via `_scratch_path`; the siblings never inherited it because they never routed through it. Retry-only fixes would have left it live.
  - **SECOND HALF REFUTED 2026-09-05 - do NOT action it.** The row claims "`core.polled_json.atomic_write_text` itself uses `Path.write_text` ... so its 6 callers put more bytes on disk than they counted". FALSE against the current tree: `core/polled_json.py:137-147` reads `_write_then_replace(Path(path), content.encode("utf-8"))` and its docstring says "Writes UTF-8 bytes verbatim - see atomic_write_json on why this is not Path.write_text". Fixed by `74497a15e` (lane 8 cycle 24), committed 2026-08-30 - the SAME DAY cycle 21 filed this row. The row was overtaken within hours and nobody reconciled it. No caller needs a per-caller byte-count decision; there is nothing to decide. Only `loop_controller.awrite` still used `write_text` and cycle 48 closed that (W3); `intents._awrite` and `steer._awrite` already wrote bytes.
  - Original body, retained for the chain: three SIBLING hand-rolled atomic writers carry the WinError 5 exposure that cycle 21 just closed in `dashboard/routes_loop_control.py`, and they write the SAME `ops/loop/control/` files. Measured writers: `ops/loop/loop_controller.py:126-129` `awrite` (writes `STOP` at `:174` and `cycle.txt` at `:1057`), `ops/loop/intents.py:99-113` `_awrite` (rewrites the INTENT file with `consumed: True` at `:230`), `ops/loop/steer.py:96-101` `_awrite` (writes `STEER.cursor` at `:243`). All three do `tmp.write_*` + a BARE `os.replace` with no `PermissionError` retry; NONE import `core/polled_json.py`. The contention is not theoretical: `STOP` is read from three poll loops - `loop_controller.py:772/786` every 5 s (`ops/loop/config.json` `poll_sec`), `ops/loop/claude_gui_bridge.ahk:299` every 1 s, and `dashboard/routes_loop_status.py:450` `read_text()` on every browser poll of `GET /api/loop-status` (`web/mc/mc.js:885`, 5 s). **ACCEPTANCE:** each writer routes through `core.polled_json.atomic_write_bytes` (or `atomic_write_text`), and each gets a regression test holding the destination open in a background thread that RELEASES inside the ~275 ms backoff window - a reader held for the whole attempt tests the exhaustion path, not the retry path, and will look like a failing fix. Mutation-test each by restoring the bare `os.replace`. **SECOND HALF, do not drop it:** `core.polled_json.atomic_write_text` itself uses `Path.write_text`, which rewrites LF as CRLF on Windows, so its 6 callers (`agents/agent1_lead/scheduler.py`, `agents/agent7_context/input_parser.py`, `lib/scrapers/_base.py`, `Share/src/tools/daemon_slayer_extract.py`, `tools/daemon_slayer_extract.py`, and `core/polled_json` itself) put more bytes on disk than they counted. Cycle 21 deliberately did NOT change `atomic_write_text`, because doing so silently alters the on-disk bytes for every existing caller; it added `atomic_write_bytes` alongside instead. Decide per-caller whether the byte count matters before flipping any of them. See `reference_windows_write_text_crlf_byte_count`.
- **RM-251 OPEN (filed 2026-08-30, lane 8 cycle 21; LANE 7, Tier-0/1)** - prose + duplication residue, no behaviour change. **(a)** Mission Control S10 Task 9 de-registered `POST /api/loop-control` from the `:8888` dispatch table, but two stale references survive: `dashboard/_handler.py:532` still lists `/api/loop-control` in the D9 auth tuple, a dead literal since the route 404s on `:8888` regardless (verified: it is absent from `dashboard/_dispatch.py:195-212` `_POST_CACHE`, and `tests/test_mission_control_server.py:468-476` asserts the non-match); and `dashboard/routes_auto_accept.py:18` copies the old trust-model prose, citing `/api/loop-control` as a peer dashboard POST. **(b)** Three private `_atomic_write_bytes` re-rolls now that `core/polled_json.py` carries a public one: `core/rofl_archive.py:815`, `lib/icons/downloader.py:29`, `tools/ddragon_mirror_refresh.py:145`. **ACCEPTANCE:** the two stale references corrected or removed, and the three re-rolls either consolidated onto the shared helper or given a one-line comment saying why they stay local (the icons + ddragon ones may deliberately avoid a `core` import). Prove each consolidated caller still passes its own tests; do NOT change any on-disk byte behaviour while doing it.

## Daemon Slayer scorer calibration

- **RM-215 REFUTED 2026-08-15, BY ITS OWN AUTHOR, ~20 minutes after filing - and the refutation is the durable part, not the row.** The row claimed the gate false-positives on an honestly-measured `--collect-only` baseline. **It does not, and it never did.** `CLAIM_COUNT` at `tools/stop_claim_gate.py:38` is `(?:(\w+)\s+)?\b(\d[\d,]{0,9})\s+passed\b` - it recognises **only** `N passed` and has never matched `N collected` as a claim at all. So the gate was not objecting to the collect-only baseline; it was matching `10605 passed` and `19114 passed` inside the filer's own PARENTHETICAL, where prior-session pass counts were being cited as corroboration. **Both flags were TRUE POSITIVES. There was no false positive to fix.** **THE FILING ERROR IS THE REUSABLE PART: the gate was diagnosed by reading its EVIDENCE side (`observed_counts` at `:317`, `EV_PASSED` at `:147`) and never its CLAIM side, then a row was filed on the inference.** That is the identical shape as this session's other two corrections and as LEDGER 1258 - a measurement that is right, and an inference from it that is not, because only half the mechanism was opened. **A guard has two halves and a false-positive claim requires opening BOTH.** Caught by TDD: the red-first run showed the supposedly-broken case PASSING on the unfixed gate, which is what a wrong premise looks like when the test is written before the fix. **RESIDUAL, recorded accurately and NOT the row as filed:** because `CLAIM_COUNT` matches only `passed`, a collect-count assertion (`"50000 collected"`) is entirely UNCHECKED - a PERMISSIVENESS gap, the mirror image of the false-positive gap this row alleged. Whether that is worth closing is unjudged; it is a smaller hole than the one it replaces and closing it makes the gate STRICTER, so do not treat it as urgent. **Do NOT re-file the original claim.** Original body follows. _(filed 2026-08-15)_ - `tools/stop_claim_gate.py` cannot see `--collect-only` evidence, so an HONESTLY MEASURED baseline count is permanently unbackable and flags as `count_mismatch`.** Measured this session: the gate built `observed_counts` at `tools/stop_claim_gate.py:317` from pass-shaped output only - `EV_NODE_PASS` (`:115`, node's `pass N`) and the pytest `N passed` form - while its ONLY reference to collection is `EV_VACUOUS` at `:148` (`collected 0 items`), which exists to detect a vacuous run, not to harvest a count. A baseline measured with `python -m pytest <dir> --collect-only -q`, which prints `N tests collected`, therefore contributes NOTHING to the observed set, and any sentence quoting that number is flagged forever. Reproduced live: a truthful `DS 10605 collected` flagged on three consecutive Stop hooks in one session, alongside a GENUINE catch in the same sentence (a `19114` inherited from `WAKEUP_NOTES.md` prose), so the finding is a mixed true/false pair rather than pure noise. **This is the same class the batch it was found in kept hitting: a guard whose recognition of its own population is narrower than its name - see RM-201's `_SEAM_PREFIXES` blindness to `include_conditional`, and `feedback_guarded_doc_line_is_not_a_guarded_number`.** The failure direction matters: it does not miss a false claim, it manufactures a false one, which trains the reader to discount the gate - and a gate that is routinely wrong on honest work is a gate that gets ignored when it is right. **ACCEPTANCE:** add a collect-shaped evidence pattern (anchored, in the `EV_*` family, matching `N tests collected` / `N test collected` and NOT the `collected 0 items` vacuity string) so a `--collect-only` count lands in `observed_counts`; then prove it non-vacuously - assert a sentence quoting a genuinely-observed collect count passes AND that a fabricated collect count still flags, since widening evidence risks turning the guard permissive. Do NOT widen to bare `\d+ collected` prose, which would let any sentence containing that phrasing back itself. **DO-NOT-REDO:** the `19114` half of the observed failure was a REAL violation (a count carried forward from a prior session's notes) and the gate was right about it - do not "fix" that away, and do not weaken `CLAIM_COUNT`. Tier-1, one module plus its own tests, no engine change, no live gate, no operator gate.

- **(FUTURE, SCOPE CHANGE not an axis fix) `ds.hps` prices no champion ABILITY DAMAGE at all, so 11 enchanter-primary champions have that whole axis unpriced on their routed scorer** _(2026-08-02, measured while resolving the RM-118 Janna UNRESOLVED line; LEDGER 1162)._ **This is filed as an OBSERVATION with a real blast radius, not as a build row, and it must NOT be built as a fourth instance of the RM-87 / RM-91 / RM-118 coupling lever.** Those three are cases where a scorer DOES price ability damage but drops one caster-stat axis; here the scorer prices none of it, deliberately - `hps.py:42-46` states champion-ability damage is out of scope, and the single ability-model reference at `hps.py:655` (`compute_ability_hps`, a documented circular-import workaround) selects `attribute_kind in {"heal","shield"}` only at `ability_hps.py:623-626`. **Measured, not inferred:** mutating Janna W `damage_blocks[1].caster_bonus_ms_pct` `30.0 -> 30000.0` leaves the entire `/rank-enchanter` payload byte-identical (48126 chars, 141 rows) while the SAME mutation on the SAME block moves `ds.ability` total ability DPS `13.10 -> 327.58`; two further controls on that block (`ap_pct 50 -> 5000`, `base 175 -> 99999`) are also byte-identical, so it is the whole `damage`-kind block that is out of scope, not the movespeed field. Population: **11 of 173 champions are enchanter-primary (Ivern, Janna, Lulu, Milio, Nami, Renata, Seraphine, Sona, Soraka, Yuumi, Zilean) and ALL 11 carry at least one `damage`-kind block.** Magnitude is small where it was measurable - Janna's MS term is 7.87 pct of her W per cast and ~2.4 pct of her total ability DPS at a realistic build. Doing this properly means teaching a heal/shield THROUGHPUT scorer to score damage, i.e. a blended enchanter objective, which is a design decision and a Tier-2 blast radius across every enchanter ranking. **Do NOT seed Janna's movespeed term into a coupling registry** - `caster_bonus_ms_pct` is 1 block / 1 champion across all 173 and would not fix the class anyway.

- **(FUTURE) RM-142-T tank + enchanter build output carries ZERO champion differentiation** _(2026-08-02, surfaced draining `ops/audit/P6_LOLMATH_PARITY.md`; measured, not inferred - a coverage gap, NOT a defect, do not file a bug against `ehp.py`)._ All 28 champions resolving to `tank` share ONE byte-identical `mixed` order (`3143, 3111, 3083, 3053, 6665, 3065`) in ALL THREE tables - `build_orders_{sr,aram,arena}.json` - and `enchanter` is the same shape at 10 of 11. Independently re-measured this session per archetype (sr/aram: tank n=28 distinct=1, enchanter n=11 distinct=2, mage n=45 distinct=6, bruiser n=44 distinct=22). **Root cause, and why the obvious fixes do not apply:** the EHP objective's only inputs are HP pool, resists and the incoming damage-type mix, so champion identity enters as a near-SCALAR gain on the baseline pool (measured span 0.890 Thresh - 1.000 Alistar); a scalar cannot re-order candidates, so live `/rank-tank` really is champion-sensitive (per-item spread reaches 21 pct) while top-1 stays `3143` for all 28 even with every kit axis forced ON and `score_by="cc_blended"`. **Two REFUTED fixes - do not re-pitch:** (a) "the tank branch silently drops `target_armor`/`target_mr`/`target_max_hp`/`target_current_hp_pct` at `core/daemon_slayer_client.py:2326-2339`" is FALSE - `compute_ehp` (`agents/daemon_slayer/ehp.py:1358`) has no such parameter at all, they are damage-side inputs describing the enemy you HIT, and there is no receiver to drop them into; (b) flipping the axis is settled and irrelevant - `_ARCHETYPE_AXIS["tank"]=None` (`core/archetype_picks.py:137`) is deliberate and a magic-damage tank still wants durability. **The honest residual:** the comp bias carries NO penetration profile, yet `compute_ehp` DOES accept `enemy_lethality` / `enemy_armor_pen_pct` / `enemy_magic_pen_flat` - so `burst_heavy` and `mixed` (both 0.5/0.5 shares) are provably identical cells for every tank, and giving the comp axis a pen profile is the one cheap lever that would make 2 of 4 cells do real work. Beyond that, real tank itemization turns on mana pool (Fimbulwinter on mana tanks, dead on Zac/Rammus), HP-ratio abilities (Cho'Gath/Sion/Ornn) and engage-vs-peel role - **none of which are EHP terms and none of which have a scorer**, which is exactly why this archetype collapses. A fix is a new objective axis, not a tuning pass; do NOT build blind. Sibling row on the AD side with a DIFFERENT root cause: the (B) champion-kit-aware DPS crediting entry above.
- **Item Shaper full re-rank engine seam (FUTURE, from OQ14).** The shipped Shaper strip is an HONEST emphasis PREVIEW only (`core/shaper.apply_shaper` over the archetype_weights.json [alpha,beta] baseline). It does NOT re-rank the actual item list from the knobs, because `agents/daemon_slayer/rank.py` `rank_items` takes NO weight-dict input (pure DPS-delta ranker) and the archetype blend is a 2-axis [alpha,beta] per champ, not a 3-axis {damage,survivability,utility} dict. A real "nudge -> re-ranked build" needs (a) a defined 3-axis weight surface the shaper nudges, mapped onto the scorer/hybrid weighting, and (b) threading the nudged weights through the rank path - an ENGINE SEAM (schema + ranker change), NOT a UI slice. Do NOT build blind: a wrong re-rank is worse than the current honest preview. MED-HIGH effort; gate on an operator decision about which weight surface the axes drive.

Extractor + per-patch data are in place (`tools/daemon_slayer_wiki_stats_extract.py` -> `data/daemon_slayer/<patch>/wiki_stats.json`, current through 16.14.1); re-run on a patch bump with `tools/daemon_slayer_wiki_stats_extract.py --patch <patch>` (see memory `reference_lol_wiki_access`). DATA CEILING (do NOT re-hunt): only 61/171 champs have a REAL AA cast time in ANY source; the 110 engine-default-filled entries are byte-identical to no-sidecar.

Neither path logs, counts, or surfaces the skip, so the report shrinks and reads as an improvement. **This was found by ruling it OUT, not by observing it fire.** The 2026-08-15 regen moved `stale_champions` 75 to 74 with Sylas removed and nothing added, and the builder stated it could not distinguish convergence from a silent skip. The verifier settled it by live-fetching `Template:Data Sylas/Chain Lash`: the page resolves, the label `Magic Damage` still matches, and the wiki now reads `[40.0, 120.0]`, equal to the frozen Meraki `[40.0, 120.0]`, so `compare_champion("Sylas", ...)` correctly returns empty. **Sylas is a TRUE convergence and must not be re-investigated** - the defect is the mechanism, not that instance. **Also corrected by the same measurement: the belief that this report's stale set can only GROW is REFUTED.** The reasoning was that the stored Meraki side is frozen at content patch 25.15 while the wiki tracks live, so drift can only accumulate; in fact a live change can move the wiki value back ONTO the frozen value, which is exactly what Sylas did. Do not re-derive that premise. **ACCEPTANCE:** both skip paths account for themselves - a counter plus a named list in the report body (`_skipped_no_page`, `_skipped_no_label` or equivalent), so a shrinking stale set can be read as convergence or as lost coverage without a live wiki fetch. Red-then-green: rename a label in a fixture, assert the champion appears in the skipped list rather than vanishing. **DO NOT REDO:** do not re-audit the Sylas row (settled above). Do not treat `_pages_fetched` or `_checked_champions` as a backstop - both were 754 / 171 across the copy-forward AND the regen, because they derive from the frozen `champion_abilities.json` roster and not from what the wiki actually returned. Neither live-gated nor operator-gated.
- **RM-218 CLOSED 2026-08-16 (LEDGER 1269) - and the row's CENTRAL CLAIM IS REFUTED. There is no vocabulary gap. Every label this row called "missing from the wiki" is ON the wiki page, and the defect was entirely in `parse_leveling_bases`.** Zero aliases were shipped, because none were needed. **The evidence that killed the filing is the pages themselves:** Alistar Trample reads `{{st|Magic Damage Per Tick|{{ap|80/10 to 200/10}}...|Total Magic Damage|{{ap|80 to 200}}...}}` - `Total Magic Damage` is the SECOND pair of the same block, and the parser read only the first. Gangplank R carries all six of its stored labels. Three mechanisms, one parser: (1) an `{{st|}}` block may hold many label/value pairs and only pair one was read; (2) an endpoint may be an EXPRESSION (`80/10`, `40*12+40*3`) and a non-numeric endpoint dropped the whole label; (3) a label may embed a template (`True Damage with {{ii|Death's Daughter}}`) which never matched the plain stored spelling. **THE ROW'S OWN INSTRUCTION "do not fold (B) into (A), they have different fixes" WAS ALSO WRONG** - the zero-label rows were the same root cause and fell from 54 to 27 as a side effect, with no work aimed at them. **Measured, both numbers as the acceptance demanded:** `skipped_labels` **357 to 109** (-69 percent) while `findings` ROSE 146 to **208** and `stale` 84 to 87 (Aatrox, Rell, Rumble) - lost coverage recovered without a single champion dropping out, and **ZERO existing findings changed value**, so the parser only added comparisons rather than perturbing settled ones. Two net-new findings were verified against raw wikitext rather than trusted: Ahri Q reads `{{ap|35*2 to 135*2}}` = 70 to 270 against a stored 80 to 280 (a real nerf), and Aatrox's sweetspot resolves through the EXISTING vardefine path as `b1*sd`, which is the arithmetic and variable features interlocking correctly. **The shared `_AP_WRAPPER_RE` in `tools/daemon_slayer_wiki_ability_extract.py` was deliberately NOT touched** - that extractor is Share-mirrored and feeds the engine, so widening it is a Tier-2 change to a data producer; this reader defines its own looser regex. Arithmetic is a whitelist walk over the AST with no `eval`, and `2**9999999`, names and calls are rejected by test. **RESIDUE, honestly stated: 109 skips remain** - 27 zero-label rows across 14 champions (Aphelios, Jayce, Nami, Nilah, Nocturne, Ornn, Qiyana, Rumble, Sona, Udyr, Velkoz, Yasuo, Yunara, Yuumi) and 82 with a live label set. That is a genuine further parser tail; file it as RM-220 if it is wanted, but **do not re-open it as a vocabulary or aliasing problem** - that hypothesis has now been refuted once by measurement. **DO NOT REDO:** do not add a label alias table; do not match on numbers instead of labels; do not re-derive these counts by reading code, run the tool. _(closed 2026-08-16; original body follows, and it is wrong about the mechanism)_ **RM-218 (filed 2026-08-16, measured by the RM-216 instrumentation on its first full sweep; LANE 6, Tier-1)** - the staleness checker cannot compare 323 stored damage rows because Meraki and the wiki use DIFFERENT LABEL VOCABULARIES, and 79 champions are certified current on the strength of comparisons that never ran.** Measured on a read-only `--full` pass at 16.15.1 (171 champions checked, 754 pages fetched, no `--write`): `stale=74 findings=127 skipped_pages=101 skipped_labels=323`, with **137 of 171 champions appearing in at least one skip list and only 34 wholly clean.** The skips split into two mechanisms and the row is about the FIRST. **(A) 273 rows: the page was fetched and parsed fine, but the stored label has no counterpart in it.** 125 distinct stored labels are unmatched; the head of the distribution is `Total Magic Damage` (37 rows), `Total Physical Damage` (21), `Maximum Magic Damage` (15), `Reduced Damage` (13), `Magic Damage` (11), `Maximum Physical Damage` (8), `Increased Damage` (7). The pattern is that Meraki publishes DERIVED AGGREGATES while the wiki publishes the COMPONENT it aggregates - Samira W stores `Total Physical Damage` against live `Physical Damage per Hit`, Nasus E stores `Total Magic Damage` against live `Magic Damage` + `Magic Damage Per Tick`, Gangplank R stores six labels against a single live `Magic Damage Per Wave`. **(B) 50 rows: the page yielded ZERO labels**, so nothing could match by construction, across 23 champions (Akali, Alistar, Ambessa, Aphelios, Fiddlesticks, Jayce, Karthus, Nami, Nilah, Nocturne, Ornn, Qiyana, Rell, Renekton, Rumble, Singed, Sona, Swain, Udyr, Varus, Yasuo, Yuumi, Ziggs). That is a `parse_leveling_bases` gap at `tools/ds_wiki_staleness_check.py:179`, NOT a vocabulary gap, and it wants its own diagnosis before any aliasing work - **do not fold (B) into (A), they have different fixes.** The consumer site is `:327` `live = wiki_bases.get(attr)`. **Acceptance:** for (A), an alias resolution proven per-alias by test against the committed fixtures - never a fuzzy or substring match, since `Magic Damage` is itself an unmatched stored label 11 times and would over-bind to `Magic Damage Per Tick`; an alias that cannot be justified from a cited live page stays unaliased and keeps counting itself. For (B), a diagnosis naming which wiki construct the parser misses, with one champion reproduced red-then-green. Success is measured as skipped_labels FALLING while `findings` does not silently fall with it - report both, since suppressing a skip by dropping the row is the failure mode this whole area exists to prevent. **DO NOT REDO:** RM-216 is CLOSED and the instrumentation already exists - do not re-file the silence, and do not re-derive the counts by re-reading the code, run the tool. Do not "fix" this by widening `_TOL` or by matching on the numbers instead of the label; the label IS the identity of the quantity. The 101 page-level skips are NOT this row, they are RM-219. _(filed 2026-08-16)_

Today that is filed as "cannot compare" and discarded, but a stored label that no longer exists upstream is exactly the staleness this tool was built to find. Decide whether an unmatched label should be REPORTED as a suspected label or damage-type drift rather than counted as lost coverage - that is a product call about what the report asserts, and it should not be quietly folded into (A). **Acceptance:** for (A), each of the three `{{ap|}}` forms red-then-green from a verbatim fixture, with the existing plain and arithmetic forms kept as negative controls, and a full sweep reporting BOTH `skipped_labels` falling and `findings` not falling - RM-218's measurement discipline, which is what proved that fix added comparisons rather than perturbing settled ones. For (B), per-alias evidence. For (C), a written decision before any code. **DO NOT REDO:** RM-216/218/219 are CLOSED - do not re-file the silence, the title construction, or the pair-split parser. Do not re-open (B) as the blanket vocabulary gap RM-218 was refuted for. Do not widen `_TOL` or match on numbers instead of labels. Do not touch `_AP_WRAPPER_RE` in `tools/daemon_slayer_wiki_ability_extract.py`; it is Share-mirrored and feeds the engine, so the reader keeps its own regex. Re-measure before starting - these counts move every time a part lands. _(filed 2026-08-16)_
- **RM-217 OPEN (filed 2026-08-15; LANE 7, Tier-1) - `tools/stop_claim_gate.py` has no way to record a RETRACTION, so a correctly-flagged claim keeps blocking every subsequent turn even after it is honestly withdrawn.** Measured this session: the gate flagged three unbacked test counts that had been carried forward from subagent reports. All three were true positives. Two of them named intermediate states of `tests/test_ds_feed_index.py` that no longer exist on disk - a later slice added tests to that file and another regenerated the data underneath it - so they are UNREPRODUCIBLE by construction and can never be backed by a fresh run. They were explicitly retracted in-session twice; the gate re-flagged them both times, because `_scan` re-reads the whole transcript each turn and the offending strings live in earlier assistant messages that cannot be edited. Net effect: an honest retraction is indistinguishable from ignoring the gate, and the only way to finish a turn cleanly is to never have said the number. **DO NOT FIX THIS BY LOOSENING `CLAIM_COUNT` (`:38`) OR BY WIDENING `CLAIM_COUNT_ORDINAL` (`:43-46`).** That is RM-215, which was REFUTED by its own author on 2026-08-15 - the pattern matches only `N passed` and never `N collected`, and its flags were true positives then as they are now. The regex is not the defect. **ACCEPTANCE:** an explicit, auditable retraction path - for example a `RETRACTED:` marker the scanner honours when it appears in a LATER assistant message naming the same figure, or a small on-disk retraction ledger under `ops/runtime/` that the gate reads and reports as "retracted" rather than "unbacked". The retraction must remain VISIBLE in the report, never silently dropped, or the mechanism becomes a bypass. Consider also whether the gate should distinguish a claim the agent MADE from one it ATTRIBUTED to a subagent while flagging it as unverified - the standing rule is that no agent-reported number may enter a status report at all, so attribution is not an excuse, but the report would be more actionable if the two shapes were separable. **DO NOT REDO:** do not re-file the gate as producing false positives; it does not. Neither live-gated nor operator-gated.

A per-enemy spike cue therefore needs a one-field backend extract (`"level": p.get("level")` into the players builder) + a new stateful panel (`web/js/panels/enemy_spike_cue.js`) = NOT presentation-only, so NOT headless-blind-shippable. MED effort, no new Riot/Claude dep, no ENGINE bump. Do-not-build-blind: verify against a live enemy roster first.
