## Platform / observability

- **RM-375 OPEN (filed 2026-09-06, LANE 10 queue cycle 8; LANE 8, Tier-1) - the Perseus vault reader loops forever on any non-JSON byte the vault binary writes to stdout, and it sits at the head of EVERY lane cycle.** Found by the RM-355 sibling sweep and filed rather than swept, because it is a DIFFERENT root-cause class: RM-355's loop exits on a counter the body declines to advance, this one exits on a content match. `tools/perseus_sync.py:114-124` and the byte-near-identical `tools/perseus_recall.py:125-135` both run `while True: line = self.proc.stdout.readline()`, returning only on EOF (`if not line: return None`) or a successful `json.loads`, and `continue`-ing past a blank line or a `JSONDecodeError`. The source is `subprocess.Popen([PV, "serve", ...], stdout=PIPE)` - an external process's stdout, unbounded by construction and not under this repo's control. Any non-JSON chatter the binary emits (a startup banner, a log line, a progress bar, a deprecation warning) is skipped forever: there is no budget, no deadline and no read timeout. **Priority is higher than the exposure math suggests**, because `tools/perseus_recall.py` is the MANDATORY recall gate at the head of every lane cycle (`CLAUDE.md` "Memory recall"), so one chatty vault release wedges all lanes at once, before any of them reaches its row. **The correct shape already exists in-tree: `core/obs_publisher.py:157-171`** (the deadline is assigned at `:157`, immediately above the `while True:` at `:158`) solves the identical problem (an unbounded `await ws.recv()` whose body skips non-matching messages) with a wall-clock deadline - a quantity the loop body cannot decline to advance. **ACCEPTANCE:** a test asserting that each of the two readers returns (or raises) rather than looping when fed a stdout that emits non-JSON lines indefinitely, plus a stated bound - a deadline, a skipped-line budget, or both. Fix BOTH sites in one pass and say whether they should share one helper; they are near-identical today and will drift apart otherwise. **Not already closed:** RM-351 (`lib/http/client.py` unbounded response read) is a different file and a different mechanism, and the sweep confirmed neither perseus reader has any existing test. `tools/overlay_live_frame_probe.py:67-71` carries the same shape over a CDP websocket, but is a dev probe with no production role - include it only if it is free.

- **RM-374 OPEN (filed 2026-09-06, LANE 10 queue cycle 7; LANE 8, Tier-1) - an absolute URL passed to `ScraperBase.fetch` is authorized by the WRONG site's robots.txt and cached under the wrong site's directory.** Carried as the un-acceptanced "runner-up" inside the RM-354 row body (`docs/_research_refill_2026-09-05.md:433-437` - POST-closure line numbers; the same commit's CLOSED banner shifted that paragraph down by 6, so a citation read off `git show HEAD:` lands at `:427` mid-sentence in RM-354's own ACCEPTANCE); filed with an id so it survives that row's closure. `lib/scrapers/_base.py:155` `url = path if path.startswith("http") else f"{self.base_url.rstrip('/')}/{path.lstrip('/')}"` lets any absolute URL bypass `base_url` entirely, while `can_fetch` at `:130` still consults `self._robots`, parsed at `:118` from `f"{self.base_url}/robots.txt"`. `RobotFileParser.can_fetch` matches on PATH only and ignores the host of the URL it is handed, so site A's robots rules authorize a fetch to site B, and the response is then cached under site A's directory (`cache_path` at `:137` joins `self._cache_dir`, rooted at `SCRAPED_ROOT / self.site` by `:107`) and stamped under site A's bucket in `_last_fetch.json`. **Line numbers are post-RM-354** - that fix inserted the marker registry above `fetch`, so a citation taken from the refill doc's pre-fix `:87` will land in the wrong place. **This is a DIFFERENT root cause from RM-354 and was deliberately not swept with it** - RM-354 is about the acceptance test on a response BODY, this is about how the request URL is constructed. **Exposure today is latent, not live:** both shipped subclasses build `path` from a `MODE_PATH` template (`site_d.py:35`, `site_b.py:28`), so neither can currently pass an absolute URL, and the sole production caller `agents/agent2_backend/pipeline/orchestrator.py:142`/`:155` goes through `fetch_champion`. It is a public method on a base class with no guard, so the first caller that passes a full URL gets the bug. **ACCEPTANCE:** a test asserting that `fetch("https://evil.example/x", cache_key="k")` on a scraper whose `base_url` is `https://fake.example` either raises, or is refused, rather than being authorized by `fake.example`'s robots rules; plus a test pinning that a same-host absolute URL still works if that is the behaviour kept. Decide and STATE which of the two the fix chooses - silently making absolute URLs illegal is a contract change, and the row must say so. **Not already closed:** `lib/scrapers/` had zero tests before RM-354 added `tests/test_scrapers_bot_wall_rm354.py`, which covers the body gate only and asserts nothing about URL construction.

- **RM-373 OPEN (filed 2026-09-06, LANE 10 queue cycle 7; LANE 8, Tier-1) - TWO more caches take a 200 body with no acceptance test on it: `lib/icons/downloader.py:82` with no gate at all, and `lib/ddragon/fetch.py:91` with an encoding gate but no shape gate. The RM-354 root cause, in two further modules.** Found by the RM-354 sibling sweep, filed rather than swept because the fix is not a mechanical extension of it. `_download` at `:71-83` gates only on `resp.status != 200` (`:79`) and then calls `_atomic_write_bytes(target, resp.body)` (`:82`), so any 200 whose body is not an image - an error page, an interstitial, a truncated read - is written over the icon file and `_download` returns `True`, which `champions()` and its siblings count as a success. **Two measured differences from RM-354, both reasons this is its own row and not a copy of that fix.** (1) The right predicate is different: the payload is a binary PNG, so the check is magic bytes and/or `Content-Type`, NOT the HTML marker list `lib/scrapers/_base.py:31-45` uses - do not import `BOT_WALL_MARKERS` here. (2) Exposure is materially lower: `:72-73` `if target.exists() and not force: return False` means an existing icon is only ever overwritten under an explicit `force=True` refresh, the host is Riot's own `ddragon.leagueoflegends.com` rather than a bot-protected site, and **`IconDownloader` has ZERO production callers** - measured 2026-09-06, the only `.py` references outside its own package are `lib/icons/__init__.py:5` re-exporting it, `lib/ddragon/fetch.py:66` naming it in a comment, and `tests/test_ddragon_version_path_guard.py:217`. Unwired is a reason it was missed, not a reason to leave it. **ACCEPTANCE:** a test asserting that with `force=True` and a stub returning `Response(status=200, body=b"<html>error</html>")`, the pre-existing icon file is byte-unchanged AND `_download` returns False; plus a test that a real PNG byte prefix still writes and still returns True. **Note for whoever takes it:** RM-251 already has an open row touching `lib/icons/downloader.py:29` (the `_atomic_write_bytes` re-roll), so check whether that landed first and rebase onto it rather than colliding. **SECOND SITE, same row - `lib/ddragon/fetch.py:85-93` `_pull`, but only half of it.** Partly refuted while sweeping: `resp.json()` at `:90` parses the body BEFORE `_atomic_write_json` at `:91` and propagates `JSONDecodeError`, so an HTML bot wall, an empty body or a truncated read genuinely CANNOT reach that cache - do not re-file those. **What survives is that the parse gates ENCODING, not SHAPE:** a 200 carrying well-formed JSON that is not the requested document (`{"error":"blocked"}` from a WAF, a bare `"maintenance"` string) parses fine and is written straight over `champion.json`. `_pull` is annotated `-> dict` and validates nothing; `_read_cached` at `:99-109` catches only `JSONDecodeError` on the FILE, so a shape-poisoned cache survives every later read and every coach reads it. **Sub-acceptance:** a test asserting that a 200 whose body is `b'{"error":"blocked"}'` leaves the pre-existing `champion.json` byte-unchanged and raises, plus one asserting a valid `{"data": {...}}` envelope still writes. Exposure is lower than the icons half (Riot CDN failures are HTML, which the parse already catches), so take the icons half first if the row is split.

- **RM-372 OPEN (filed 2026-09-06, LANE 10 queue cycle 6; LANE 7 or LANE 8, Tier-1) - `scripts/cache_ddragon_assets.py` creates a version directory from `_index.json` without validating it, and is safe today only by inheritance.** Found by the adversarial pass on the RM-353 fix (LEDGER 1344), and filed rather than fixed in-cycle because it is defense-in-depth, not a live exposure: this lane ships one row per cycle. `scripts/cache_ddragon_assets.py:19-21` `latest_version()` reads `latest_pulled` out of `data/meta_build/ddragon/_index.json`, `:44-45` joins it into BOTH `data/meta_build/ddragon/<version>` and `web/data/ddragon/<version>`, and `fetch()` at `:31` calls `dest.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)` - the exact shape RM-353 closed one level up. It is safe TODAY only because the two writers of that key both validate after RM-353 (`lib/ddragon/fetch.py` `fetch_all` writes `dd.version`, `tools/ddragon_mirror_refresh.py` `main()` writes the validated `latest`), so the file cannot currently hold a poisoned value. That is inheritance, not a guard: a third writer, a hand-edited `_index.json`, or a restore from an older backup reopens it, and this script is the only remaining site that turns a version string into a CREATED directory without checking it. **ACCEPTANCE:** the script validates before the join - reusing `tools.ddragon_mirror_refresh.validate_version` if the import is clean when the script is run from the repo root, otherwise a local check of the same `^\d+\.\d+\.\d+$` shape so the two cannot disagree - plus a test asserting a poisoned `_index.json` (`latest_pulled` of `"../../../pwned"` and of `"maintenance"`) creates no directory. **Not already closed:** RM-353 (LEDGER 1344) deliberately fixed only the four entry points it could reach from `lib/ddragon/fetch.py` and `tools/ddragon_mirror_refresh.py`; this file was named in that cycle's sibling sweep and left open on purpose, with the verifier recording it as covered-at-source-only.

- **RM-371 PARTIAL (filed 2026-09-06, LANE 10 queue cycle 4; LANE 8, Tier-1; the `core/rofl_archive.py` third SHIPPED 2026-09-06 by lane 8, the other two still OPEN) - `lib/http/client.py` is NOT the single outbound chokepoint it documents itself as, and THREE external fetchers that bypass it still read unbounded.** RM-351 put a `max_bytes` ceiling on the shared client, and its filed body called that client "the single chokepoint for every outbound third-party fetch in the tree" - the wording the client's own docstring carries at `:26` and again in its SECURITY POSTURE block at `:22-46`, which calls the blocklist "the whole control". **That premise is false, measured by sweeping every `urlopen` in the tree rather than trusting either doc.** The three genuine, still-unbounded external sites: **`core/riot_api.py:319`** - `body = r.read()` (the `urlopen` is at `:318`) against `{region}.api.riotgames.com`, url built at `:619`/`:648`/`:700`; **`core/rofl_archive.py:503-504`** `_http_get_bytes` - `timeout=120` and a bare `return resp.read()`, the worst of the three because it is a replay-archive download whose body is legitimately large, so it is also the one that must NOT simply inherit a small default; and **`core/sgp_client.py:167`** - `return response.status, response.read()` inside `_urllib_transport`, against the `*.pp.sgp.pvp.net` / `*.lol.sgp.pvp.net` hosts tabled at `:81-92`, wired as the DEFAULT transport at `:187` (`transport or _urllib_transport`), with zero cap constants anywhere in the module. None of the three inherits the byte cap, the blocklist, the rate limit, the circuit breaker or the scheme allowlist. **`core/sgp_client.py` currently has no non-test importer**, so it is unwired rather than hot - which is a reason it was missed, not a reason to leave it: SGP is the route that DOES return event-mode match data (RM-106), so it is a live candidate to be wired. **MY FILED COUNT WAS WRONG TWICE BEFORE THIS ROW WAS COMMITTED - four, then two, now three - and how it was wrong each time is the reusable part.** The first draft named `core/augment_external_source.py:212` and `core/rank_tier_source.py:278` as unbounded; probing rather than trusting the grep showed both are ALREADY BOUNDED by prior lane-8 work the pattern could not see - a 32 MiB cap at `augment_external_source.py:67` read as `_MAX_BODY_BYTES + 1` at `:220` (lane 8 cycle 33), and a chunked 2 MiB cap at `rank_tier_source.py:81` with the loop at `:286-296` and the over-cap raise at `:305`. Both use the read-one-past-the-cap shape RM-351 arrived at independently - convergent prior art a later pass should copy rather than re-derive. **The second draft then said "exactly TWO" and MISSED `core/sgp_client.py` entirely, and the cause was not the pattern but the PAGINATION: the census grep was piped through `head -30` and silently truncated.** An independent verifier swept without a limit and found it. A truncated census reads exactly like a complete one - lane-doc trap 12 and trap 4 landing together, twice, on a row at the moment of filing. **THE LOCALHOST SET IS DELIBERATELY EXCLUDED**, checked site by site rather than filtered by pattern: `core/daemon_slayer_client.py:104,118` (`:8860`), `core/liveclient_cache.py:200` and `core/minimap_blob_detect.py:383` (`:8889`, `_VISION_URL` at `:214`), `core/lcu_pool.py:175`, `core/replay_camera.py:221,230` and `core/replay_seek.py:132,141` (`:2999`), and `core/moon_proxy.py:111,155,185` - whose `MOON_BASE` at `:23` is built from `MOON_HOST = "127.0.0.1"` at `:21`, so it is an internal RC service and NOT the third-party fetch an earlier draft of this row called it. `core/moon_proxy.py` is on the frozen list at `CLAUDE.md:42` in any case. None of these is a third-party fetch and the blocklist has nothing to say about them. **ACCEPTANCE:** all three external sites bounded - either routed through `lib/http.get_client()` (preferred: it inherits every control at once) or given their own bounded read - each with a test proving an endless stream terminates, and `core/rofl_archive.py` sized for a real `.rofl` rather than handed the 16 MiB default; PLUS a guard test that fails when a NEW `urlopen` to a non-localhost host appears outside `lib/http/`, so the population cannot silently regrow. **Re-census the population yourself with an UNLIMITED sweep before scoping the work** - the count in this row has already been wrong twice, once from pattern and once from a truncated pipe. **Two fences.** (1) Do NOT "fix" the chokepoint claim by deleting the docstring sentence - the claim should become TRUE, and the guard test is what keeps it true. (2) `core/riot_api.py` carries its own Riot-specific rate limiting, and the shared client's gate is 1 req/sec per hostname, so routing it through must have that interaction MEASURED, not assumed - a per-region Riot key allows far more. **Not already closed:** lane 8 cycle 27 audited `lib/http/client.py` for the blocklist failing open and never asked who bypasses it; no `tests/` file greps for `urlopen` outside `lib/http/`. **PROGRESS 2026-09-06 (lane 8, deep audit of `core/rofl_archive.py`).** The third of the three is CLOSED: `_http_get_bytes` now reads through a bounded `_read_bounded` against a replay-specific `MAX_REPLAY_BYTES = 64 MB` ceiling (deliberately NOT `lib/http/client.py`'s 16 MB default, exactly as this row warned) and raises `ReplayTooLarge`, which `download_replays` catches per item so one oversize body cannot abort a sweep. **This row UNDERSTATED that site, and the correction is the reusable part:** it cited the transfer read at `:503-504` alone, but `download_replays` gunzipped BEFORE validating the container magic, so `gzip.decompress` applied the full expansion ratio to bytes nothing had checked - the transfer cap alone would not have bounded it. The gunzip is now incremental against the same ceiling, and the body is bounded a second time inside `download_replays` because the transport is INJECTED and every caller supplying its own fetcher bypasses `_http_get_bytes` entirely. Guards in `tests/test_rofl_archive_lane8_download_bounds.py`, all six mutation-proven red-then-green; one guard SURVIVED its first mutation (a zero-filled bomb fails the magic check on its own, so it proved nothing) and was rewritten to use a bomb carrying valid magic. **STILL OPEN and unchanged: `core/riot_api.py:319` and `core/sgp_client.py:167`.** A sibling sweep for `urlopen(...).read()` also found `core/daemon_slayer_client.py:105`, `core/liveclient_cache.py:201`, `core/minimap_blob_detect.py:384`, `core/moon_proxy.py` (FROZEN, four sites), `core/replay_camera.py:223,230` and `core/replay_seek.py:132,143` - these are LOOPBACK readers, not third-party fetches, so they do NOT mean this row's count of three external sites was wrong again; they are a separate and lower-severity population that needs its own filed row before anyone acts on it.
- **RM-370 OPEN (filed 2026-09-06, LANE 10 queue cycle 3; LANE 7, Tier-1) - the queue lane's CI acceptance is STILL unsatisfiable: `workflow_dispatch` runs cancel EACH OTHER, for the same reason push runs did.** Cycle 2 diagnosed that every push run on `main` was cancelled by the next cycle's push, and fixed it (commit `5ccee94b4`) by having each cycle trigger a `workflow_dispatch` run instead, reasoning at `tools/headless-queue.md` section 9 that "Only another `workflow_dispatch` can supersede it, and the next one is a cycle away because cycles are serial." **The first half is true and the conclusion does not follow.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:39` sets `group: ${{ github.workflow }}-${{ github.ref }}-${{ github.event_name }}` with `cancel-in-progress: true` at `:40`, so two dispatch runs on `main` share ONE group. Serial cycles only help if a cycle is LONGER than a run, and it is not: a `ci` run is roughly 45 minutes (its `check` job carries `timeout-minutes: 75` at `:97`) against a cycle of roughly 26. The next dispatch therefore arrives BEFORE the previous one finishes and kills it. **MEASURED, not inferred:** dispatch run `34012397811` (cycle 2, headSha `173377358`) reports `conclusion: cancelled` with job `check` cancelled and its step `full dual suite (RM-119 - push CI now gates the whole tree)` cancelled - cancelled by dispatch run `34013317217` (cycle 3, headSha `bc9744920`), which this cycle triggered. So cycle 2 recorded the fix and cycle 3 proved the fix does not hold, which is the same defect one layer up. **ACCEPTANCE:** a `ci.yml` change that gives each `workflow_dispatch` run its OWN concurrency group so a later dispatch cannot cancel an earlier one - e.g. folding `github.run_id` into the group only for that event - plus evidence that two dispatch runs triggered under 26 minutes apart BOTH reach a terminal non-`cancelled` conclusion on their `check` job. **Two fences.** (1) Do NOT simply set `cancel-in-progress: false`: that keeps push runs queueing up and re-creates the cost problem RM-157 already measured and rejected (see the "Cut CI cost by cutting push VOLUME" row in this file) - the change must be scoped to the dispatch event alone. (2) `ci.yml` is guarded: `tests/test_ci_job_timeout_headroom_rm156.py` pins the job timeout and `tests/test_ci_docs_guard_coverage.py` pins the docs-guard complement, so grep for every test asserting over that file BEFORE editing it and expect to update a guard in the same slice. **Not already closed, but NARROWED mid-cycle by an independent convergence - read this before working it.** A parallel session landed `e4bcff9b7` ("docs(queue): serialize the CI wait - moving concurrency groups did not fix it") WHILE this cycle was running, and it reached the identical root cause from the identical evidence, citing the same two run ids: "The group was never the problem: a `ci` run takes ~45 minutes and a cycle takes ~26, so any group a cycle re-enters is self-cancelling." Two independent derivations agreeing is corroboration of the DIAGNOSIS, not evidence for either fix. **That commit already shipped the PROCESS half:** section 9 of `tools/headless-queue.md` now makes the worker BLOCK until its run reaches `completed`, polling up to 60 minutes with one re-dispatch allowed, on the reasoning that cycles are already serial so nothing can trigger the superseding run while the only thing that would trigger it is waiting. It also promotes the local dual suite to a per-cycle pre-push gate, which is the same command `ci.yml:504-505` runs and therefore the same evidence about 40 minutes sooner. **So the interim guidance this row originally carried is SUPERSEDED - a cycle now waits rather than declining to cite.** What remains open is only the `ci.yml` half above, and it is now an OPTIMIZATION rather than the sole remedy: a per-dispatch concurrency group would let the lane stop paying a ~45 minute serial block per cycle, which is roughly a 2.7x cycle-time cost at the current cadence. Weigh that against leaving it alone - the blocking fix is already correct, just slow. `5ccee94b4` and `e4bcff9b7` both changed only `tools/headless-queue.md` prose; `ci.yml` is untouched since, so the concurrency group is exactly as described. **MEASURED REFINEMENT, and it makes the just-landed blocking fix insufficient as written - the 60-minute poll budget is UNDER-SET for the very runs it polls.** Cycle 3 blocked on its own dispatch `34013317217` and reached **67 minutes with the run still `in_progress`**, both jobs still executing their dual-suite step. The ~45-minute figure the budget was derived from is a PUSH-run figure; a `workflow_dispatch` is not comparable, because `ci.yml:44` starts `nightly-full-suite` ALONGSIDE `check`, so a dispatch fans out two ~23k-test suites that contend for runners. **The escape hatch is worse than the timeout:** the "one re-dispatch allowed" clause, taken at 60 minutes, triggers a second dispatch into the SAME group and so cancels the very run it was waiting on, converting a slow green into a guaranteed cancellation. Any fix must therefore either (a) raise the budget above the observed dispatch duration - the `check` job's own ceiling is `timeout-minutes: 75` at `:97`, so 80 is the honest floor - or (b) stop fanning out `nightly-full-suite` on dispatch, or (c) do the `ci.yml` concurrency change above and drop the block entirely. **Do NOT keep the re-dispatch clause in any of them.**
- **RM-369 OPEN (filed 2026-09-06, LANE 10 queue cycle 1; LANE 7 or LANE 8, Tier-1) - two `tests/test_queue_loop.py` cases read LIVE MACHINE STATE, so they are green in CI and red on the box exactly when the lane is working.** `test_main_maps_every_flag_onto_run_loop` (`:399`) and `test_main_once_is_exactly_one_cycle` (`:421`) both monkeypatch `qloop.run_loop` to a stub, but still call the real `qloop.main()`, which acquires the real Windows global named mutex `Global\RC_QUEUE_LOOP_DRIVER` before it ever reaches `run_loop`. The lane-10 driver holds that mutex for its whole multi-hour run (LEDGER 1338 (e) chose that deliberately), so both tests fail with `queue-loop refused: another driver already holds Global\RC_QUEUE_LOOP_DRIVER` whenever the queue lane is actually draining. **Measured 2026-09-06** by the verifier gate on LEDGER 1339: `pytest tests -q -n 8` returned exactly these 2 failures, reproduced solo without `-n 8`, on a tree whose diff touched neither `ops/loop/queue_loop.py` nor its test. **This is the same class as the defect the commit `af157607f` already fixed one test over in this very file** - `test_stop_reason_reads_the_GIVEN_control_dir_not_the_live_one` asserted a live `ops/loop/control/STOP` as a precondition; the fix there was to plant a sentinel in a FAKE control dir and monkeypatch `qloop.CONTROL`, and the same shape of fix applies here. **The failure mode is the dangerous direction:** CI never runs a driver, so CI stays green and the redness only ever appears to a lane mid-cycle, where it reads as that lane's own regression. LEDGER 1339's cycle spent gate time classifying it. **ACCEPTANCE:** both tests pass with a live driver holding the mutex - proven by acquiring `Global\RC_QUEUE_LOOP_DRIVER` in the test process (or a child) for the duration of the assertion, not by asserting it is free; the singleton acquire is stubbed or injected rather than reached, and a SEPARATE test still proves `main()` genuinely refuses when the mutex is held, so the singleton behaviour LEDGER 1338 (e) added is not silently unguarded by the fix. Sweep the rest of the file for any other case that touches a real named mutex, a real `ops/loop/control/` path, or a live pid. **Not already closed:** `af157607f` fixed a DIFFERENT test in this file and did not touch either of these two; no LEDGER/ROADMAP/BACKLOG hit for `RC_QUEUE_LOOP_DRIVER` outside LEDGER 1338's design note and LEDGER 1339's report of this filing.
- **RM-368 OPEN (filed 2026-09-05, operator-raised; LANE 7 or LANE 5, Tier-1) - three harness mechanisms from `github.com/affaan-m/ECC`, re-implemented narrowly, with the whole-package lift REFUTED up front.** ECC is "the agent harness performance optimization system" - 68 agents, 286 skills, 94 commands, hooks, a memory vault, cross-harness adapters for Claude Code / Codex / Cursor / OpenCode. Measured 2026-09-05 via `gh api repos/affaan-m/ECC`: 250044 stars, 37627 forks, `pushed_at` 2026-09-05, 49386 KB, created 2026-01-18, 10+ contributors. **LICENSE GATE PASSED, and all three sources were read rather than one:** `LICENSE` is MIT with a REAL grantor (`Copyright (c) 2026 Affaan Mustafa` - not the `{{ organization }}` template that burned RM-127 Phase 3), `package.json` declares `"license": "MIT"` with no `private: true`, and `pyproject.toml` declares `license = {text = "MIT"}` plus the OSI classifier. No contradiction in either direction, so vendoring would be legal with attribution. **DO-NOT-LIFT SET, measured with `gh api search/code`: 10 files say "adapted from" and therefore carry upstream authorship the maintainer cannot unilaterally relicense** - `commands/prp-commit.md`, `commands/prp-pr.md`, `commands/prp-prd.md`, `commands/prp-plan.md`, `commands/prp-implement.md`, `commands/code-review.md`, `agents/database-reviewer.md` (and its `.kiro/agents/` copy), `skills/react-performance/SKILL.md`, `CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md`. **THE FULL LIFT IS REFUTED, four reasons, so a taker must not re-open it:** (1) `npx ecc-universal setup` installs into `~/.claude/skills/`, `~/.claude/agents/` and REWRITES `~/.claude/hooks/hooks.json` - RC's hooks are the authoritative gate and `CLAUDE.md` documents two measured ways a hook is present and silently inert, so handing that file to a third-party installer buys back a failure class already paid for twice; (2) its core loop (plan / test / implement / review / verify / remember) is what `CLAUDE.md` already mandates and the superpowers plugin already installs; (3) its Memory Vault is Markdown files plus an optional MCP server with NO embeddings, strictly weaker than Perseus, whose `--verify` asserts `embedded == active` precisely because keyword-only recall degrades silently; (4) cross-harness support is dead weight - the loop has been single-vendor Claude since 2026-08-01. **NEVER run the installer to evaluate any of this. Read the files.** **ACCEPTANCE - three independent sub-rows, each shippable or REFUTABLE on its own, and a REFUTE with a measurement is a completed sub-row:** **(a) instincts / `continuous-learning-v2` + `/evolve`.** ECC extracts patterns from real sessions with a CONFIDENCE score, injects the high-confidence ones at SessionStart under a cap (default 6) and a threshold (default 0.7), and clusters related instincts into skills. RC's equivalent is 100 percent manual - every `memory/*.md` is hand-written and `MEMORY.md` is hand-curated. Ship a narrow version over the EXISTING Perseus store and the EXISTING SessionStart injection path: a candidate memory carries a confidence, injection is capped and thresholded, and both are tunable by env var. **Never a second memory store** - `project_mnemoverse_removed` is what a duplicate store cost last time. Acceptance: a test proving the cap and the threshold are honored, plus a measured before/after of SessionStart injected bytes, since context is the binding budget here. **(b) hook profiles.** ECC has `ECC_HOOK_PROFILE=minimal|standard|strict` plus `ECC_DISABLED_HOOKS=pre:bash:x` granular disable. RC has four hooks in `.claude/settings.json` and two ad-hoc escape hatches (`RC_FULL_SUITE`, `ops/runtime/allow_visual.flag`). Acceptance: a named profile plus per-hook disable honored by all four, one test per hook proving disabled means NOT RUN (not merely quiet), and a test proving the DEFAULT profile leaves today's behaviour byte-identical. **(c) `skills/eval-harness` + `agent-eval`.** RC has zero agent evaluation: lane quality is asserted by verifier subagents and never measured, so nobody can say whether lane output is getting better or worse. Acceptance: either a harness that scores N past lane cycles against their own filed acceptance criteria and reports a baseline, or a filed REFUTE naming what made it not worth building. **Do NOT file a fourth sub-row without a measurement; the rest of the catalogue is domain packs (healthcare, homelab, django, flutter, energy procurement) with no RC surface.** **Consumed RM-368; next free id RM-369.**

- **Local / free-tier LLM to replace the paid Gemini advisor** _(operator-raised 2026-07-01; setup = one QA session with Claude, then autonomous)._ GROUND TRUTH (verified 2026-07-01): Gemini is used in THREE roles, not one. (1) `tools/gemini_ask.ps1` - Claude asks a mid-session research/context Q; Claude sends ONLY the question + a short preamble, Gemini AGENTICALLY reads repo files itself (`--approval-mode plan`) bounded by `.geminiignore` (excludes Share/, _archive/, node_modules, .git, *.db, logs/, ops/runtime/, secrets); default model already `gemini-2.5-flash`. (2) `tools/gemini_audit.ps1` - nightly 03:00 RC-GeminiAudit; context is DETERMINISTICALLY assembled (commits + `git diff --stat` + ROADMAP head 120 + BACKLOG head 80 + full diff HARD-CAPPED 60k chars ~17K tok); default flash, fallback flash. (3) `ops/loop/loop_controller.py` director (`/gemini-headless-upgrade`) - `build_director_context` = ORCHESTRATION_PLAN (capped) + 25 commits + LEDGER newest 60 lines (40k-byte cap) + directive chain + ROADMAP head 120; ~30-40K tok (hard-budgeted after a 572KB starvation incident); THIS is the ONLY role on premium `gemini-3-pro-preview`. So: context is CURATED, never a full-repo dump - fits any local/free model's window; and the $ pressure is essentially the director role only (ask+audit are already ~free). PLAN: add a thin `tools/llm_advisor.py` (OpenAI-compatible) behind `RC_LLM_BACKEND=local|groq|gemini`, move ask+audit to a free tier FIRST (Groq Llama-3.3-70B / Cerebras / already-free AI-Studio flash - zero judgment risk, immediate $ drop), keep director on pro OR trial DeepSeek-R1 / a free-model ENSEMBLE -> Claude-arbiter (RC already assumes verify-before-trust, so consensus on the advisory roles is a clean fit), QA once against real director+auditor transcripts before cutover, keep Gemini as the fallback slot the scripts already have. Local option = Ollama on Legion (out-of-game only, GPU shared with League/OBS/Vanguard; Qwen3-32B / Qwen3-30B-A3B MoE / GPT-OSS-20B fit ~16-20GB; Llama-3.3-70B 40GB+ likely too big). The agentic self-read role (ask) needs a tool-calling agent runner locally (aider / small Python loop), not just a raw chat API. Honest caveat: a 20-32B or free-tier model is weaker than gemini-3-pro at director-grade judgment - ensemble narrows but does not fully close it; keep Claude's verification gate hard on the director path.
- **Cut CI cost by cutting push VOLUME, not coverage - move the headless loop to branch-plus-PR** _(filed 2026-08-04 out of RM-157's second half, which measured the alternative and rejected it)._ **The measurement that produced this.** RM-157 priced the per-push full suite against real billing and decided to KEEP it: it is the only pre-merge gate (zero `pull_request` runs in the 2026-08-01..04 window - the loop pushes straight to `main`), it caught 4 failures in the 37 push `check` jobs that ran to completion (~11 percent), and the cheap narrowings are already taken (`branches: [main]`, `paths-ignore '**/*.md'`, `cancel-in-progress`). So the remaining lever is the 62 code pushes to `main` in 3.44 days, which is the headless-loop cadence, not the suite. **What the shape would be.** Loop cycles land on a work branch and open one PR per cycle instead of pushing per commit; `ci.yml` already triggers on `pull_request: branches: [main]`, so no workflow change is needed to gate it. The win is that `cancel-in-progress` then collapses a cycle's intermediate commits into ONE completed gated run rather than a chain of supersedes - **25 of 62 push `check` jobs in the window were `cancelled` mid-flight and still billed 287 minutes for zero signal.** That 287 is the floor of the saving, and the true figure is larger because each surviving cycle runs the suite once instead of once per commit. **Why it is worth doing but is NOT urgent.** Actions minutes are doubling month over month (May 1467, Jun 3003, Jul 6030) and July was the first month with a real bill, NET 18.13 USD. The current-cadence projection is the thing to watch, not the historical curve. **Do NOT solve this by deleting the per-push suite** - that is the option RM-157 explicitly rejected with numbers, and re-pitching it should cite a catch rate, not a cost share. **Second-order risk to design around:** a branch-plus-PR loop must not merge its own PR without the `check` run being green, or the gate is nominal; and `docs-guards.yml` fires on the docs-only pushes that `ci.yml` declines, so the pairing pinned by `tests/test_ci_docs_guard_coverage.py` has to survive the move.
- **Streaming vision**: delta-encoded frames instead of full JPEG every 2s. ~5x bandwidth reduction.
- **Mode-specific overlay settings (SR vs ARAM) + mode-specific out-of-game stats overlay** _(operator-requested 2026-06-30, queue for planning + implementation)._ The overlay widget field (positions / visibility / opacity / scale) is currently ONE global layout (`web/js/lib/overlay_layout.js` `_layout` keyed by widget id, persisted to localStorage `LS_KEY` + the rc-shell on-disk mirror via `window.rcShell.getWidgetLayout`/`setWidgetLayout`). Operator wants the layout to be PER-MODE so SR and ARAM (and Arena) each remember their own widget arrangement - e.g. the minimap-area widgets differ by map, the ARAM-only panels want a different home than the SR ones. AND the same per-mode notion for the OUT-OF-GAME companion "stats" overlay (the lobby/idle stats surface) so it can show mode-appropriate stats. Design questions for planning: key the layout store by mode_key ({sr,aram,arena}) with a shared/default fallback; migration of the existing single layout into the SR slot; how the rc-shell mirror + Alt+Shift+R reset interact with per-mode slots; whether the launcher menu gets a "copy layout to other mode" affordance. The out-of-game stats panel specifically needs a MODE SELECTOR (SR / ARAM) that AUTO-SELECTS ARAM when the player is in/queued for ARAM (and stays manually switchable), so the idle/lobby stats reflect the right mode by default (operator add 2026-06-30). Touches overlay_layout.js (layout keyspace), the rc-shell preload bridge (mirror schema), and the companion stats surface (the mode selector + auto-select-on-ARAM wiring off mode_key). Each its own session + the per-page UI-audit ritual.
- **(FUTURE) Vite-style push hot-swap** - on asset change push a ws event naming the file; client hot-swaps the CSS `<link ...?v=hash>` in place (state-preserving), full reload only for JS; upgrade to ADR-008 after the wss:// overlay fix lands.
- **(NOW-cheap) uxpatterns.dev + taste-skill design dials** folded into the 5-phase UI-audit prompt (Tooltip-vs-Popover, Comparison-Table / Card-Grid / Statistics-Display, AI Loading/Error states; variance/motion/density dials).
- **(NOW-adjacent, LOW risk; needs a tool install PLUS a `CLAUDE.md` rule edit) `ast-grep` structural search + an LSP-preference rule** _(from RM-121 item 4, the `random.txt` part-1 ingest, 2026-07-28; QA'd against live state, not adopted wholesale)._ Two halves of one piece of advice, adjudicated separately. **(a) GENUINE GAP - `ast-grep` / `sg` is NOT on PATH on Legion (measured 2026-07-28).** Structural (AST) search in place of regex grep is real value in a tree this interconnected: RC's recurring miss is a seam or rename edit that an `rg` pattern skips because the call SHAPE differs from the literal - the same class as the `deny-sweep by ID SUFFIX never by name` and `NAME is not the ID` lessons, one layer down. **(b) PARTIALLY HAVE - the Claude Code harness already exposes an `LSP` tool**, so "query true symbol references instead of grepping" is available today; the only gap is that no repo rule tells a session to prefer it, so it goes unused. **Why this is BACKLOG and not a free docs edit:** the payload is an install plus a usage rule in `CLAUDE.md`, which is CI size-budgeted under 60KB and where rule changes are operator territory - it needs approval to spend budget lines, not a headless cycle to add them. Ship both halves as ONE rule so the guidance reads as a single "structural before textual" habit. Do NOT re-file (a) as a research question - the reachability probe is done and the answer is "absent".

- **RM-131 SHIPPED 2026-08-01 (LEDGER 1157) - `probe_qq_synergy_shape()` is the 4th signal in `tools/upstream_drift_check.py`, sentinel-tracked as `qq_synergy_shape`, pinned by `tests/test_upstream_drift_qq_synergy_probe.py` (13 tests).** Fingerprint is `n<row-count-bucket>+<first-row-key-set-hash>` over the bot/support canary pair - SHAPE only, never values: win rates move daily and a value-sensitive signal would report drift every run, which is the same as reporting nothing (proven both ways in-test and by live experiment). A dead endpoint or a rejected envelope - the exact silent-fallback-to-the-May-25-seed case this row was filed for - now shows as ERR rather than nothing. `_null_sentinel()` was refactored to derive from `FIELD_NAMES` so the baseline and the probe list can no longer drift apart. **Durable:** the carry-forward test was TAUTOLOGICAL on its first pass (it re-implemented `upstream_drift_check.py`'s carry-forward expression in the test body instead of calling `advance_sentinel`); caught by the verifier, rewritten to write a real sentinel to a tmp path, and mutation-proven - stubbing the production line to `out[name] = cur` now turns it red. _(original filing kept below)_ - the 101.qq.com duo-synergy source has NO drift/staleness guard.** MEASURED 2026-08-01 (lane-research, subagent + independent grep): `tools/upstream_drift_check.py` (the `RC-UpstreamDriftCheck` daily task) tracks exactly THREE upstream signals - ddragon `versions.json` (`:60,138`), meraki `patchLastChanged` (`:61,154`), cdragon `content-metadata.json` (`:64,167`) - and carries ZERO reference to qq / synergy / 101 / getRankDouble (grep empty, verified twice). But `core/synergy_external_source.py:33` fetches the Tencent `getRankDouble` endpoint and, on any HTTP error or envelope-shape change, `_validate_rows` (`:102`) fails SILENTLY back to the frozen May-25 static seed (`core/smoothed_rates_101qq.py`), so RC can serve stale duo-synergy indefinitely with no alarm. The `RC_DUO_SYNERGY_LIVE=0` kill switch and the live `/api/duo-synergy` route are unaffected. **ACCEPTANCE:** add a 4th probe `probe_qq_synergy()` to `upstream_drift_check.py` returning a stable content fingerprint (canary lane-pair row-count bucket or first-row key-set hash), sentinel-tracked at `ops/runtime/upstream_drift.json` exactly like the other three (fail-soft to `None`, carry the prior value forward on error, report `changed=True` only on a real fingerprint delta); a new `tests/test_upstream_drift_qq_synergy_probe.py` asserts (a) `None` fail-soft on HTTP error without raising, (b) a changed fingerprint reports `changed`, (c) an errored probe is never `changed`. Lighter CI-only alternative if a live fetch is unwanted in the suite: `test_synergy_seed_freshness` asserts the on-disk seed capture-date is within N patches of `current.txt`, so a dead-endpoint + stale-seed state fails loudly. No ENGINE bump, no Share. **Companion to RM-128** (both wire a new signal into the same drift-guard family). **Do-not-redo:** the Riot Match-V5 / SGP host table (`core/sgp_client.py:81-97`) is deliberately NOT filed here - a live-reachability probe is live-gated (needs a real LCU session) and Match-V5 is an official versioned API with no hand-maintained data mirror, so it is out of the mirror-drift frame.

## Draft + coach lane (Haiku-to-ZERO north star)

- **Radar target-profile reference polygon - SHIPPED 2026-07-23 (LEDGER 1010)** as the operator's own recent wins (`core.player_gpi._reference_block`, `/api/player-profile`, dashed teal ring). **REMAINING TAIL:** the CHI-paper mechanics-radar static bands (positioning / combo-diversity / monitoring / pings, Diamond+) are still open - they need an external Diamond+ distribution RC does not have.

- **(NOW-adjacent, the module already EXISTS) Wire `tft/tft_roll_odds.py` into a TFT coach - this is the actual Haiku retirement for the roll question** _(2026-07-30, LEDGER 1128)._ The compute half SHIPPED: `tft/tft_roll_odds.py` is stdlib-only, pinned by parametrized invariants over level x cost x pool-state rather than float pins, and **wired into NO coach**, so today it is dead weight. It exists because `POOL_SIZES` and `UNITS_PER_COST` had **ZERO consumers repo-wide** and `TIER_ODDS` was read only to FORMAT PROMPT TEXT - i.e. "should I roll" was an LLM reading a table out loud, the exact shape the Haiku-to-ZERO charter targets. The remaining work is the consumer: pick the surface (the TFT coach `next`/`watch` channel is the obvious one), thread the live board/level/gold state into it, and shadow-log agreement against the current Haiku answer before any flip. **TWO GATES, both real:** (a) it must NOT flip until the Set 17 constants row above is resolved, because a deterministic answer computed from wrong odds is worse than an LLM guess - `CONSTANTS_SET_VERIFIED = False` is the machine-readable form of this gate; (b) the flip needs live validation in a real TFT game, so the flip itself is a `docs/LIVE_GAME_GATED_SYNC.md` act. Do NOT flip blind.
- **RM-164 overflow: Lane B consumer wiring, the measurement bodies** _(2026-08-06, LEDGER 1203)._ ROADMAP carries the verdict and the fences; the numbers live here. **Coverage was already full and machine-guarded** across three table families (comp-archetype 692 cells/mode, flat 519, durability variants 346), so the only reachable defect was item TIER, and that is now pinned: `is_starter_tier()` + `iter_order_item_ids()` in `core/build_order_precompute.py` plus a guard over 3 families x 3 modes, verifier-walked over all 9 family x mode tables and 28026 slots with **0 violations**. **The rule is `Lane` tag AND gold < 1000, derived from the 16.15.1 bulk and independently re-derived:** 78 Lane-tagged items, 76 under 1000g, exactly 2 above (Atma's Reckoning 223039 / 663039 at 2500g), dearest sub-ceiling starter 950g, and NO Lane item between 950g and 2500g - so the ceiling sits in a provably EMPTY band. It is deliberately NOT a `depth` test: mode mirrors ship `depth: None` at a flattened 2500-2750g, so `depth >= 2` would flag 71 of 138 ids. ENGINE_VERSION UNCHANGED at 1.275.0 - this was pure table-invariant work, not engine math. **`sort_by="efficiency"` was MEASURED and REJECTED, not skipped:** it changed **52 of 60 cells** over 15 champions x 4 comp archetypes and pulled Doran's Helm / Doran's Bow / Guardian's Blade into slots 3-5 of the final six, i.e. it DEGRADES the tables. **The engine-side starter leak is a separate, non-obvious call:** `_filter_candidates` at `agents/daemon_slayer/rank.py:687` has no starter gate, which may be CORRECT for early next-buy advice, so it wants an operator ruling rather than a unilateral deny. (One process note kept because it cost a fix-up commit: the first pass shipped a comment citing `_eligible_items` at `rank.py:747`, a symbol that exists NOWHERE - corrected at `ab9b2b18`.)
- **RM-167 overflow: the prompt-cache floor, measured** _(2026-08-06, LEDGER 1206)._ 20 real `messages.create()` sites, 12 carry `cache_control`, and the markers are MEASURABLY INERT: across 51 real ledger days, 38,319 calls and 62.1M input tokens, `cache_in = 126` and `cache_write = 126`, i.e. **0.0002 pct**. Cause is the model cache FLOOR, not a wiring bug - aram `_SYSTEM` static tail is 6177 chars (~1670 tok against the 2048 Haiku floor), arena 3082, vision 616 against 1024. Compounding it, `coaches/aram_coach.py:920` interpolates all 5 placeholders into the FIRST 145 chars of `_SYSTEM`, so the marked prefix mutates every tick and could never have been cacheable regardless of size. A hoist yields a stable 6177-char prefix that STILL misses the floor by roughly 380 tokens, so clearing it needs DELIBERATE PADDING - and padding plus reordering shifts Haiku positioning, which is why behavioural equivalence needs a live ARAM A/B rather than a test. Two things a future pass must not assume: the real token count of that tail is unsettleable offline (estimates span 1670 to 3089, straddling the floor - a live `count_tokens` settles it), and this is **NOT covered by the item-286 closure**, which measured only the four augment/TFT prompts.
  - **RE-MEASURED 2026-08-08 on the POST-REPAIR ledger (run 2026-08-08-01 S2, LEDGER 1236). Use THESE figures - the ones above were taken against the pre-repair ledger and are stale in the denominator, not in the conclusion.** **56,166 calls / 62,375,169 tokens_in, `cache_in = 138`, `cache_write = 138` = 0.000221 pct.** The CALL count grew 46.6 pct (38,319 -> 56,166) while the token count barely moved, which is itself the tell that the earlier read was a partial ledger. **The sizing correction that actually matters: `aram_coach` alone is $40.02 of all-time spend across 12,413 calls, 31.2 pct of the total.** RM-167 is filed as "roughly 10-15 pct of TOTAL spend"; that is the LOW end of a pool twice as large. **Nothing about the BLOCKER changed and it must not be read as newly-actionable:** the hoisted prefix still misses the 2048-token Haiku floor and needs deliberate padding, and padding plus reordering shifts Haiku positioning, so this stays NEEDS-OPERATOR pending a live ARAM A/B for behavioural equivalence plus a live `count_tokens`. A third headless re-measurement of the cache ratio adds nothing - the number is settled at ~0, and the open question is the A/B, not the arithmetic.
- **FUTURE tail (one-liners -> lane):** domain-decomposed post-game sub-scores (farming/ganks/itemization/vision, each 1 strength + 1 mistake) -> PGR S3-S5; per-phase timeline mistake-pinpointing (failed-engage / missed-objective) -> PGR; standardized GD@10/XPD@10/CSD@10 delta + replacement-level vocabulary -> PGR 0-100 axes; decoded-replay HF dataset (Apache-2.0, patch-12 behavioral only) -> a Haiku-to-ZERO precompute mining SESSION; OBS-overlay URL-param browser-source route on `:8888`.
- **RM-204 (OPERATOR-GATED) - the Arena augment play-line is BUILT, TESTED, markup-clean and DELIBERATELY INERT; the remaining hop retires a live Haiku call and therefore needs live validation** _(2026-08-14, filed by the orchestrated-run docs sync out of the S2 + S7 slices; the ROADMAP NEXT row is a pointer to this body)._ **State:** `core/arena_augment_playline.py` exists and is exercised, `core/arena_deterministic_coach.py` consumes it through `_safe_augment_advice`, and **`dashboard/_deterministic_coaching.py:1481` still passes no `owned_augments=`** - so the module reaches no served output and no player can see it. That inertness is deliberate, not an oversight. **The markup blocker is CLOSED** (the guard is now a prose-character allowlist rather than a token blacklist; zero markup across 450 probes, zero innocent rows lost), so do not re-file it. **THREE things must be true before the hop lands, and none of them is code:** (1) **there is ZERO shadow det-vs-live agreement history for this column** - it has been hardcoded empty since v1, so unlike every other deterministic field there is literally nothing to diff a deterministic answer against, and the usual "shadow-log agreement before flipping" gate cannot be satisfied from the existing corpus; it has to be ACCRUED first, which means a shadow-write hop before the serve hop. (2) It is **unvalidated against a real or replayed Arena game** - the 225 rows are catalog text, and no run has confirmed the live apiNames arriving per tick actually resolve against them in a real match. (3) **The display-name resolution path is untested against real vision output**, which is the half most likely to fail quietly, because a name that fails to resolve degrades to something plausible rather than to something obviously broken. **Coverage as shipped is 109 with-effect / 116 name-only**, and that split is a deliberate honesty floor - 127 of 225 descs carry unresolved `@Placeholder@` vars whose values live in a per-rarity `dataValues` array, and guessing the rarity tier would manufacture wrong numbers. **Do NOT "improve" coverage by resolving placeholders against a guessed rarity** - a wrong precompute is worse than a Haiku call, which is the charter rule this whole slice was built to respect. **Scope fence: only the PLAY half of the field is closed. The SELECT half - which augment to take - is still Haiku-only and is NOT in this row**; closing it is a separate program and needs its own source, since "what do I do with what I have" and "which should I pick" are different questions with different data. **Why operator-gated rather than headless:** flipping a coach off a live LLM call is exactly the class the charter says must not be flipped blind, and acceptance terminates in a judgement about a real game, which an agent cannot self-adjudicate.

## LCU integration lifts

_(From the 2026-07-05 open-source LCU-toolkit teardown, R81 companion. GPL-class copyleft on the source toolkit -> never vendor; re-implement in RC's own code. Full record: `docs/_archive/2026-07-28-research-consolidation/COMPETITOR_LIFT_INDEX.md`.)_

- **(NOW, in-transport LCU, no ToS) Legal-move champ-select getters** - `/lol-champ-select/v1/{pickable,bannable,disabled}-champion-ids` (RC does not call these; improves pick-card legality). New getters in `lcu/lcu_pregame.py` + surface in `dashboard/_champ_select.py`. Existing Basic-auth transport, no new dep.
- **(NOW, in-transport LCU) Riot recommended-runes read as a degrade fallback** - `/lol-perks/v1/recommended-pages/champion/{id}` when RC's own DS-driven rune plan is thin (`lcu/lcu_rune_writer.py` WRITES runes today but never READS Riot's recommendation). Cheap; RC's DS runes stay primary.
- **(FUTURE, MED, architecture) LCU WebSocket event stream** - subscribe `OnJsonApiEvent` over `wss://riot:<token>@127.0.0.1:<port>` (WAMP) for instant gameflow / champ-select / rune-write-timing pushes instead of RC's ~1 Hz poll. New `lcu/lcu_events.py` sidecar owning reconnect-on-lockfile-rotation into the asyncio AppLoop (do NOT edit the frozen `lcu/lcu_client.py`). Read-only event stream, no ToS issue; latency/efficiency win.
- **(FUTURE, LOW-MED) Tagged-players note layer** - user-authored notes/tags on encountered players (a table alongside `rewind_history.db`), surfaced on the scouting card.
- **Overlay App E LCU rune-page auto-write** - push DS-recommended runes into the client (mirror lcu_rune_writer). Needs a non-frozen wrapper around `lcu/lcu_client.py`. (The companion "UI consumer for `core/personal_build_wr.py` / `GET /api/personal-build`" tail is DONE - `web/js/panels/personal_build.js` renders the `#csv-personal-build` block from `web/js/panels/champ_select.js`.)

It caught `Exception` and set `snap = None` with no log line, so a fault RM-312 had just named disappeared one frame higher. Post-fix: the flag gate is `_state_builder.py:173`, the speaking except is `:177-179`, the helper is `_log_inprocess_caller_degrade` at `:108` with its throttle constant at `:103` and its rationale comment block from `:81`. **THE CLASS RM-312 PROVABLY CANNOT COVER IS AN `ImportError` ON THE LAZY IMPORT.** When `from dashboard._lcu_inprocess import lcu_summary_inprocess` raises - broken or renamed module, circular import, a failure inside `lcu.snapshot_shape` at module load - RM-312's `_log_degrade` does not exist to run, because its module never loaded. That is a structural limit of a module-local logger, not a defect in RM-312, and it is pinned by a dedicated arm that ALSO asserts RM-312's module state stays untouched, so neither seam can mask the other. **SAME SHAPE AS RM-312, DELIBERATELY DIFFERENT PROSE, INDEPENDENT STATE:** WARN on CHANGE of the signature `(exception type, innermost frame basename, lineno, func)` then at most once per 60s, with its own lock and throttle so a module-seam fault cannot suppress a caller-seam fault and a log reader can tell WHICH layer faulted. The helper cannot raise - it runs inside a fail-soft `except`. **NO PAYLOAD LEAK, RE-PROVED AT THIS SEAM RATHER THAN INHERITED:** no `str(exc)`, no `exc_info`, no `stack_info`, no f-string; a verifier drove `RuntimeError("puuid=SECRET-LEAK-MARKER")` live and observed the marker absent from `getMessage()`, `record.args`, `record.__dict__` and a full `Formatter` render. The repo is PUBLIC and LCU payloads carry PUUIDs, so this is measured per seam, never assumed. **CONTRACT BYTE-FOR-BYTE UNCHANGED:** still returns `None` on fault, still falls through to `lcu_summary()`; success path, the ordinary `snap is None` (League not running) path and the flag-unset path all stay SILENT. **THE PRE-FIX RED IS EXPLICITLY NOT OFFERED AS DEFECT EVIDENCE - IT WAS WEAK BY CONSTRUCTION.** All 19 arms went red before the fix, but on a MISSING TEST HELPER rather than on the defect; LEDGER 1390 had to make the same correction one entry earlier, so this slice anticipated it instead of being caught by it. **The real evidence is anti-vacuity: stub the log call to a no-op and 13 of 19 arms go red, while the 6 that survive are exactly the ones that must** - the arms asserting the silent paths. An INDEPENDENT verifier reproduced that 13/6 split from a scratchpad copy rather than inheriting the number. **TIER-0 IN THE SAME SLICE:** RM-312's shipped comment claimed a 1 Hz cadence from the stale "1.0s TTL" prose. Measured truth is `RC_STATE_CADENCE_SEC`, default **0.5 s** with a 0.1 floor (`dashboard/routes_state.py:167-178`), about **2 Hz**, so the worst case is ~7200 lines/hr not ~3600; and the path is DARK today because the gate at `_state_builder.py:173` reads `RC_LCU_INPROCESS`, unset on this box. Corrected at the source rather than restated. **THE SLICE STALED ITS OWN CITATIONS AND THE FIRST VERIFIER PASS CAUGHT IT, NOT THE AUTHOR** (+83 lines in `_state_builder.py`, +5 in `_lcu_inprocess.py`); repo-wide re-derivation followed at +82 and +5 onto byte-identical code checked against `git show HEAD:`, with citations already wrong at HEAD left alone and reported. **That sweep did NOT reach `ROADMAP.md` or `BACKLOG.md` - neither file is in the 9-file diff - and the RM-312 row below was still carrying pre-shift offsets until the 2026-09-11d docs-sync re-derived them.** **NOT LIVE-VERIFIED AND NOT CLAIMED:** `RC_LCU_INPROCESS` is unset and no live champ-select was driven, so the WARN's production text is proven by unit test only. **OBSERVED, both figures re-run independently by a verifier to an exact match: 19 passed on the new guard; 192 passed / 21716 deselected / 56 subtests on the widened scope; ruff clean on 8 `.py` files; 0 codepoints above 126 across all 9.** Pre-push sibling-name sweep CLEAN (28588 bytes, 9 files, 1 commit message, 0 binary/LFS blobs, which are never content-scanned). **THE FENCE STAYS:** this does NOT authorise a general bare-`except Exception` sweep - the remaining sites in `_state_builder.py` and `tools/lcu_agent.py` are still a CANDIDATE POPULATION needing its own census, and their line numbers must be RE-DERIVED because this slice moved code in that file. Guard `tests/test_state_builder_inprocess_degrade.py`, 19 arms.
- **RM-404 SHIPPED 2026-09-11 (filed and shipped the same day; LEDGER 1389; Tier-1, MED; measurement-gated, TDD, independently verifier-gated from a frozen worktree) - 4 of the 5 printed commands were BROKEN, and the failure mode is SILENT.** **MEASURED, not reasoned about, by feeding each block to `[System.Management.Automation.Language.Parser]::ParseInput` under Windows PowerShell 5.1.19041.6456 - parsing only, nothing was ever registered:** `ds_matchdb_mcp_server.py:64`, `keybind_listener.py:31`, `screen_agent.py:42` and `:44` each return **errors=0, statements=2**, the AST extents showing statement 1 ending at the bare `^` and statement 2 being the orphaned `/TR "..."`. So PowerShell does NOT choke - it runs `schtasks /Create` stripped of its payload and then a garbage command. A syntax error would have been kinder. `liveclient_relay.py:14` returns **errors=0, statements=1** and is FINE; the filed row's "may be fine" hedge was correct and that site was deliberately left UNTOUCHED. Reproduced independently by a verifier against `git show <merge>^:` with its OWN extractor. **"JUST LABEL THEM cmd.exe-ONLY" WAS NOT AVAILABLE for 4 of 5:** `keybind_listener`, `liveclient_relay` and both `screen_agent` blocks interpolate `$env:LOCALAPPDATA`, which is PowerShell-only - in cmd.exe it stays literal and the task gets a junk path. Those blocks were PowerShell-TARGETED and PowerShell-BROKEN. Only `ds_matchdb_mcp_server` was shell-ambiguous. **THE REPAIR SHAPE IS CONSTRAINED AND THE OBVIOUS ONE IS BANNED:** do NOT hand-fix the caret escaping. `Register-ScheduledTask` takes the executable and its arguments as SEPARATE parameters, so the continuation/quoting question does not arise at all; parameter existence was confirmed via `Get-Command ... .Parameters`, metadata only. **THE GUARD PARSES AND NEVER RUNS** - `tests/test_scheduled_task_docstring_commands.py`, 5 arms; the verifier read `_PS_PARSE_SCRIPT` for an execution path and found none (no invocation operator, no `Invoke-Expression`, no `Start-Process`; the block text is passed as JSON DATA). **FALSE-RED HANDLED PROPERLY, which is the RM-392 class:** the PowerShell arm gates on `shutil.which("powershell") or which("pwsh")` and SKIPS naming the interpreter rather than erroring. Verified the hard way - a child PATH was built by dropping every directory containing `powershell.exe`/`pwsh.exe`, the child was ASSERTED unable to resolve either (`which` returned `None None`) BEFORE the result was trusted, then `4 passed, 1 skipped`, rc=0, zero errors. The caret arm is pure Python and NEVER skips, so CHECKED-AND-FOUND-NOTHING stays distinct from COULD-NOT-CHECK. **ANTI-VACUITY, AND A REAL NEAR-MISS CAUGHT BY MEASUREMENT:** the builder's first extractor returned **11** blocks from 5 real commands - the extra 6 were its OWN English repair notes inside those same docstrings, i.e. the guard was grading prose as commands. Fixed by requiring the block to NAME its task on the same line, pinned by `test_prose_mentioning_the_cmdlets_is_not_graded_as_a_command`. The verifier reproduced the 11 with a deliberately loose pattern, confirming the lookahead does real work, and separately stubbed `_embedded_task_commands` to `[]`: **two arms fail** (`test_the_extractor_finds_the_known_embedded_task_command_sites`, and the parse arm on "anti-vacuity: nothing to parse"). **CORPUS IS PINNED BY TASK NAME, NOT LINE NUMBER**, which matters because the repair MOVED every site (pre-repair 64/31/14/42/44, at HEAD 70/37/14/48/52) - a line-pinned guard would have self-staled in its own commit. **POPULATION RE-DERIVED INDEPENDENTLY AT EXACTLY 5** over 2391 tracked `.py`: `ops/rc_supervisor.py:455` is a genuine code-issued `["schtasks", "/Run", "/TN", task_name]` argv call, correctly excluded because it is `/Run` and not a paste-me block; no `Unregister-ScheduledTask` exists anywhere. **RESIDUAL, STATED RATHER THAN CLAIMED AWAY:** parse-clean does NOT prove registration would succeed - elevation for `-RunLevel Highest`, `pythonw.exe` resolution and `$env:LOCALAPPDATA` expansion are all UNVERIFIED, and registering a task is an operator act that stays untested by design. Arm 2 was exercised against PowerShell 5.1 only; `pwsh` 7.6.5 is present but unmeasured. **SEPARATE, NOT FIXED, FLAGGED FOR THE OPERATOR:** these are non-raw docstrings, so the SOURCE shows `C:\\RC-Agent\\` while `help()` renders `C:\RC-Agent\` - an operator pasting from the source file rather than from `help()` gets doubled backslashes. Pre-existing convention across all these files and outside this row's scope. **PROVENANCE:** LW's 2026-09-11 00:30 note, where LW published a `schtasks` line, the operator ran it, and it failed with `ERROR: Invalid argument/option - '--once /F'`. LW had an arm proving the tool PRINTS the line and never executes it, and had verified nothing about whether the line WORKS. The transferable rule: **an arm proving a command is not executed says nothing about whether it is correct.** Original row body follows.
- **RM-404 (original text, filed 2026-09-11, Tier-1, LOW-MED) - a command RC PRINTS for a human to paste is a deliverable with no test, and RC ships five of them.** **MEASURED candidate population, 5 sites, all `schtasks /Create` blocks embedded in module docstrings for an operator to copy:** `tools/ds_matchdb_mcp_server.py:64` (`RC-DS-MatchDB-MCP`), `tools/keybind_listener.py:31` (`RC-KeybindListener`), `tools/liveclient_relay.py:14` (`RC-LiveClientRelay`), `tools/screen_agent.py:42` (`RC-ScreenAgent-League`) and `:44` (`RC-ScreenAgent-UI`). Four of the five continue lines with a cmd.exe caret `^`; PowerShell continues with a BACKTICK, so a caret-continued block pasted into PowerShell does not parse as one command. **FILED AS A CANDIDATE POPULATION, NOT A DEFECT COUNT** - not one of the five has been executed to confirm it fails, and `tools/liveclient_relay.py:14` is a single line that may well be fine. Re-measure before repairing; the honest headline is "5 untested printed commands", never "5 broken ones". **Provenance and why it is worth a row rather than a shrug:** LW published a `schtasks` registration line on 2026-09-10, the operator ran it, and it failed with `ERROR: Invalid argument/option - '--once /F'` - cmd.exe `\"` escaping stripped by PowerShell while building argv. LW had an arm proving the tool PRINTS the line and never executes it, and had verified nothing about whether the line WORKS. **The generalisable rule, which is the actual deliverable here:** an arm proving a command is not executed says nothing about whether it is correct. **The repair shape is constrained and the obvious one is wrong:** do NOT hand-fix the escaping. LW's own finding is that `Register-ScheduledTask` takes the executable and its arguments as SEPARATE parameters, so the quoting question does not arise at all. Any guard must PARSE the emitted text rather than RUN it - registering a scheduled task is an operator act and must stay one - and must skip with a reason naming the missing interpreter where no PowerShell exists. **Do NOT widen this into a general "every code block in every docstring" sweep** without re-deriving the census above; that predicate is unbounded and this one is five sites.

Every one corroborated TWICE - a LEDGER entry AND a source probe - because a doc agreeing with another doc is not evidence. ROADMAP also contradicted ITSELF on RM-250, which sat in a SHIPPED/CLOSED pointer cluster and in an OPEN row at once. **THE COST IS MEASURED, not hypothetical:** the 2026-09-10e hand-off named RM-387 + RM-388 as this session's fallback work, and both had shipped on 2026-09-08. That is the second consecutive session to open on rediscovery (the first was Q5, LEDGER 1370). **THE BINDING RULE IS THE ENTIRE SUBSTANCE OF THE GUARD, and a naive per-line regex gets it wrong in both directions.** A disposition word binds to the NEAREST PRECEDING `RM-<n>` and ONLY when no other id intervenes. Two live shapes are pinned as CONTROLS and must not be broken: (1) `ROADMAP.md` carries `RM-281 HALF-CLOSED + RM-283 OPEN`, where the OPEN belongs to RM-283 - which is genuinely open - so a guard binding it to RM-281 reports a FALSE POSITIVE; (2) `RM-204`'s BACKLOG row opens `- **RM-204 (OPERATOR-GATED) - the Arena augment play-line is ...` and its first status-vocabulary hit sits deep inside lowercase prose, so the BACKLOG rule requires the row-leading marker to be reachable across SHOUTED QUALIFIERS ONLY (which is what `RM-250 FIRST HALF SHIPPED` and `RM-291 FULLY CLOSED` need) and treats a lowercase letter in the gap as prose having begun. **A FIRST PARSER REPORTED RM-204 AS A FIFTH DRIFT ROW AND THE PARSER WAS NARROWED, NOT ALLOWLISTED** - do not "fix" a future false positive with an exemption list. Confusable ids (RM-250/RM-350, RM-291/RM-391) are handled by the EXISTING `_ID` negative lookahead in `tools/rm_id_registry.py`; do not hand-roll another. **BUILT ON `tools/rm_id_registry.py` DELIBERATELY:** that module is pure text - zero `subprocess`, zero `shutil.which`, zero git - so the guard carries NO false-RED exposure of the RM-392 class, which is the whole reason it was not built on `tests/_repo_walk` (whose `tracked_relpaths` DOES shell out). Every pre-existing symbol in the module is BYTE-UNCHANGED: the diff is **181 added / 0 removed**, verified by `--numstat` and by a zero-count of `-` content lines. **ADR-015 does NOT apply** - a two-named-file guard is not a repo-root enumeration (`docs/adr/ADR-015-shared-repo-enumeration.md:117`, "A subdirectory walk is not a root walk") - but the anti-vacuity half of `CLAUDE.md:238` does, and it is PROVEN rather than asserted: with both parser entry points stubbed to return an empty mapping, **15 arms fail**, named in the verifier record (`ParserFindsRealContent.test_the_parser_finds_many_bound_ids_in_both_live_docs`, `..._finds_real_disposition_markers_...`, `..._both_live_docs_still_carry_open_rows`, plus the RM-251 pin and both synthetic controls). The invariant arm is vacuous ALONE, which its own docstring says; those arms are what make it bind. **RE-FIREABILITY WAS PROVEN WITHOUT MUTATING A TRACKED FILE:** driving the parser over `git show <merge>^:ROADMAP.md` returns exactly `[250, 291, 387, 388]` pre-repair and `[]` post-repair. **CI ROUTING, measured and counter-intuitive:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` carries `paths-ignore: ['**/*.md']` (`:29-30`, `:33-34`), so a docs-only commit NEVER runs it - the workflow that actually gates this class is `.github/workflows/docs-guards.yml`, whose module set is derived at CI time by `tools/md_guard_selector.py`. The new module IS selected. **Two trap notes for anyone extending it:** the selector derives its universe from GIT, so a new guard is NOT selected while untracked - `git add` first, then re-run the selector; and the selector's documented IO-call requirement is STALE (it dropped `test_doc_size_budget.py`), mere reference now selects. **Residual NOT closed, stated rather than implied:** the guard reads exactly two files, so ROADMAP-vs-LEDGER and BACKLOG-vs-LEDGER drift are UNGUARDED, as is the reverse direction (a row marked SHIPPED in ROADMAP while BACKLOG still says OPEN) - the reverse was not observed live and was deliberately not built on speculation. `ROADMAP.md:81` also still carries a stale `Next free id = RM-252` figure, left alone as out of scope and already covered by the standing rule that the live pin is the guarded one in `docs/DS_SWEEP_TRACKER.md`.
- **RM-402 REFUTED AS SCOPED 2026-09-10 (filed and refuted the same day; LEDGER 1386; Tier-1, LOW; NO CODE SHIPPED. The body below is the PRESENT TENSE OF ITS FILING and is retained as the historical record - read this SETTLED block first, it refutes the filing's own numbers).** MEASURED over a FROZEN 93-transcript corpus (93, not the 92 RM-401 used - one session landed since, so the baseline was RE-DERIVED, never inherited), by FOUR independent passes: a census, a narrowing-and-attack build, a CI-residue re-derivation, and a verifier adjudicating the first two. **THE ROW'S FILED NUMBERS ARE BOTH WRONG, IN OPPOSITE DIRECTIONS.** "6 live false positives at turn granularity, 2 of them this class" - there are **ZERO** live findings of this check corpus-wide (whole gate yields 28 findings: `count_mismatch` 23, `full_suite_claim_over_filtered_run` 2, `commit_claim_without_commit` 1, `file_claim_without_edit` 1, `hook_bypass` 1, `push_claim_without_push` **0**), and the latent non-git population is **15 across 9 sessions**, not 2. **CAUSE, verified by hand at `tools/stop_claim_gate.py:480`:** `did_push` is computed ONCE over the whole session's bash record, BEFORE the sentence loop opens at `:495`, so a push at the last turn retroactively backs a claim made at the first. All 15 non-git uses sit in sessions where `did_push` is already true. **Narrowing `CLAIM_PUSH` therefore buys ZERO reduction in live findings.** It would only matter if `did_push` were ever scoped to the turn, which is a different change and would expose all 15 at once. **THE DOMINANT CLASS IS NOT THE ONE THE ROW NAMES.** It is the drift-guard budget-overflow idiom ("my entry pushed it over", "pushed ROADMAP to 89.9 pct", "pushed the dual suite past the 55-min ceiling") at 10 of 15 - repo-native prose the wrap ritual produces routinely. The third-party repo listing the row leads with is 1 of 15. **EVERY REMOVING CANDIDATE IS ONE-PHRASE LAUNDERABLE, measured by running it, not reasoned about.** The row's own named attack "Committed and pushed back to origin/main." silences the unguarded idiom lists; object-guarded variants fall to "Everything is pushed back up, tree clean." and "Pushed today."; a `pushed it over` rule falls to "The fix was ready so I pushed it over."; a stars-AND-forks repo-listing shape falls to "Session scale: 12 files / 0 stars / 0 forks / pushed." **THE ROW'S OWN SUGGESTED EVIDENCE-DERIVED ALTERNATIVE (bash record instead of words) MEASURES WORSE IN BOTH DIRECTIONS:** 5 of 15 non-git uses have a `git push` in-window anyway and 98 of 415 genuine git uses have none, because the budget idiom is WRITTEN DURING THE WRAP, which is exactly when pushes happen; a 5-event window catches 2 of 6 while killing 49 genuine claims, a 20-event window is inert, and it is launderable by tool-call ORDERING alone. **PROCESS FINDING WORTH MORE THAN THE ROW - A CIRCULAR MEASUREMENT WAS CAUGHT BY ADJUDICATION.** The build slice enumerated the non-git population with an IDIOM-SHAPED SCAN and returned 6; exhaustive classification returns 15, and all 9 it missed lie outside its own three shapes. It was evaluating idiom-list narrowings while enumerating the population through an idiom list, so it was STRUCTURALLY INCAPABLE of measuring its own false negatives. Two agents agreeing would have shipped that 6; the divergence was resolved against the artifact, not split. The census slice was also partly wrong on MEMBERSHIP (its advocacy item "the amendment LW pushed for" occurs ZERO times in the scored population, and it missed a `be7f3ef0` item), which is why the total is right at 15 but the composition came from the verifier. **DO NOT RE-PITCH** an idiom / word-sense exclusion list, a repo-listing shape, or a bash-window discriminator for `CLAIM_PUSH`. The standing remedy is unchanged and costs one character pair: BACKTICK the non-git sense, which `strip_prose_noise` at `:334` deletes before any check runs and which cannot be used to launder a real claim. **ORIGINAL FILING FOLLOWS.** `tools/stop_claim_gate.py` `CLAIM_PUSH` matches NON-GIT senses of "pushed", so a sentence about shoving something, or a third party's GitHub listing, is scored as an unbacked push claim.** MEASURED over a FROZEN 92-transcript corpus copy (never the live directory - it holds the running session's own growing transcript): `CLAIM_PUSH` is `re.compile(r"\bpushed\b", re.I)` at `:131`, consumed at `:507-508`. Of the **6 live false positives at TURN granularity** the RM-401 census found, **2 are this class**: `5a864578` "pushed back correctly on one of my own" (an argument was pushed back, nothing was sent anywhere) and `6048e0f9` "250044 stars / 37627 forks / pushed today" (ANOTHER PROJECT'S GitHub repo listing, quoted while evaluating a third-party lift). The same non-git sense recurs corpus-wide in budget prose - "my entry pushed it over". These are the largest remaining false-positive class, and unlike the adjectival shape RM-401 was refused for, the target is not one word away from an assertion: a repo listing and a rhetorical shove are not git operations at all. **PROVENANCE, stated because it changes how much you may lean on it: the 428/69/19 census and the laundering demonstration were re-derived independently at merge, but THESE TWO SENTENCES were hand-opened by a build agent and re-confirmed by a verifier who hand-opened all 16 candidates - they were not personally re-read by the merger. Re-open them first.** **SCOPE FENCE, and it is the load-bearing half of this row.** (1) **DO NOT touch the `_negated` window.** The RM-401 writeup named it as a co-residual and called it evasion-free; that was REFUTED and corrected before merge. `_negated` on `CLAIM_PUSH` is ALREADY launderable today - measured against the shipped gate, "This is not a guess: I pushed all six commits to origin/main.", "Without further gating, I pushed the lane branch to origin." and "Nothing was left uncommitted, and I pushed main to origin." are ALL suppressed while the bare "I pushed all six commits to origin/main." flags. Those are the exact RM-398 laundering phrases. Widening the 40-char window strictly ENLARGES that surface. (2) **DO NOT re-pitch a part-of-speech, noun-class or determiner narrowing of the ADJECTIVAL shape.** RM-401 is REFUTED on measurement with four separating rules built and killed. (3) This row is NOT a licence to reopen RM-401 under a new number. **THE SUSPICION THAT MUST BE MEASURED, NOT REASONED ABOUT, because asserting evasion-freedom is exactly what went wrong twice on 2026-09-10:** an IDIOM EXCLUSION LIST is a prose-shaped rule, and the party this instrument polices writes the prose. Before shipping one, construct the attack directly - can a session that pushed nothing buy silence by writing "pushed back to origin/main", or by formatting a claim to look like a repo listing? If the answer is yes for any candidate spelling, the exclusion list is the RM-396 shape and must be refused like the others. An evidence-derived alternative worth costing first: these two sentences differ from real claims in that neither session had ANY git remote activity in the surrounding turn, so the discriminator may live in the bash record rather than in the words. **Acceptance:** a narrowing measured against a FROZEN copy of the corpus reporting BOTH removed findings AND new FALSE NEGATIVES, with the attack families derived from the implemented mechanism; or a recorded REFUTE carrying that scoring. **Note before measuring: a finding-level before/after is UNAVAILABLE on this check** - `did_push` is true by the END of any session that eventually pushed, so `push_claim_without_push` is 0 corpus-wide at HEAD and no narrowing can move it. Score at the PATTERN level, at TURN granularity, the way RM-401's census did. Cite the RM-401 block for the method rather than rebuilding it.
- **RM-401 REFUTED AS SCOPED 2026-09-10 (filed the same day, LEDGER 1384; Tier-1, LOW; body below is the PRESENT TENSE OF ITS FILING and is retained as the historical record - read the SETTLED block at the END before acting on anything above it, because the row's PREMISE is upheld and its PROPOSED FIX is refused) - `tools/stop_claim_gate.py` `CLAIM_PUSH` matches an ADJECTIVAL "pushed", so a sentence that asserts nothing about having sent anything is scored as an unbacked push claim.** MEASURED: `CLAIM_PUSH` is `re.compile(r"\bpushed\b", re.I)` at `tools/stop_claim_gate.py:131`, consumed at `:507-508` gated only on `did_push` and `_negated`.
