Found in the wild: it flagged this session twice on an unreachable PAST sentence. **CONFLICT OF INTEREST, and it is the load-bearing half of this row: the session that a gate flags is the WORST-PLACED one to patch that gate.** Narrowing a check while that check is scoring your own wrap is indistinguishable from evading it, whatever the merits, so this was FILED rather than fixed. Whoever takes it must not be the flagged party. **Acceptance:** a narrowing measured against a FROZEN copy of the transcript corpus (never the live directory - it holds the running session's own growing transcript) reporting BOTH new false negatives and removed findings, with the attack families derived from the mechanism. **The precedent that must be honoured is the RM-398 laundering-phrase regression:** `_negated` reuse on `CLAIM_COUNT` was shipped, refuted for silencing "Without question N passed" / "This is not a guess: N passed", and closed as a dead end - a part-of-speech narrowing here has the same evasion shape ("the pushed refs are green" is one word from "I pushed"), so measure it, do not reason about it. **SETTLED 2026-09-10 - REFUTED AS SCOPED. NO CODE SHIPPED. The row's OBSERVATION is upheld and its REMEDY is refused, and the refusal is MEASURED, not argued.** Everything below was scored over a FROZEN 92-transcript corpus copy; never measure over the live directory, which holds the running session's own growing transcript (RM-397 / RM-398 trap). **WHY A FINDING-LEVEL BEFORE/AFTER IS UNAVAILABLE, which is the first thing any re-opener must understand:** a whole-transcript audit sees `did_push` true by the END of any session that eventually pushed, so `push_claim_without_push` is **0 corpus-wide at HEAD** and no narrowing can move it. Scoring must be at the PATTERN level, and at the granularity a Stop hook actually runs. **THE CENSUS: 428 `CLAIM_PUSH` token matches across 69 of the 92 sessions.** 19 sessions carry no push evidence at all and **none of those 19 ever utters the word**, which is the whole explanation of the 0 baseline. `EV_PUSH` was attacked as a confound and cleared: it does over-credit in principle (it matches `git stash push`), but across 73 credited sessions there were 0 credited without a real remote-push invocation, so the 0 is real and not an artifact hiding findings. **SCORED AT THE POSITION UTTERED** (running `did_push`-so-far, per SENTENCE because `audit:507` flags per sentence and `_negated` keys on the FIRST match): **16 candidate sentences precede any push in their own session, 8 are already suppressed by `_negated`, 8 remain live at message granularity - and only 6 at TURN granularity, which is what a Stop hook sees.** Two fall out because the claim and the session's first push sit in ONE unbroken turn with ZERO intervening user prompts (verified on the parentUuid chain, independently re-probed: `4e9aeca2` row 33 -> row 61, `bdc76955` row 260 -> row 274), so `did_push` is already true at that Stop and neither ever fires. **That kills one of RM-401's own two adjectival targets.** **THE 6 LIVE FALSE POSITIVES, hand-opened, are FOUR DIFFERENT MECHANISMS and ZERO true positives:** the row's named ADJECTIVAL shape accounts for exactly **1** (`251c4c36` "the hook invokes the sweep over the pushed refs" - the wild instance the row was filed from, and the session RETRACTED the phrasing in its own next turn); `_negated` WINDOW DEFECTS account for **2** (`39bc4be6` "Nothing has been staged, committed, merged, or pushed." - "Nothing" ends EXACTLY 40 chars before the match, a one-character boundary miss; `6048e0f9` "has committed and pushed nothing so far" - the negation is AFTER the verb and `_negated` only looks behind); NON-GIT IDIOM accounts for **1** (`5a864578` "pushed back correctly on one of my own"; the same sense recurs corpus-wide in "my entry pushed it over" budget prose); THIRD-PARTY REPO METADATA **1** (`6048e0f9` "250044 stars / 37627 forks / pushed today" - another project's GitHub listing); and REMOTE-STATE REPORT **1** (`a0d6788a` "2 commits ahead of main, pushed to origin, NOT merged", which looks like the one true positive and is not - the session ran `git ls-remote origin <branch>`, got `76cb0821`, and the sentence cites that exact SHA, so it is probe-backed reporting of a push an EARLIER lane session made). **So the proposed narrowing addresses 1 of 6 live false positives (17 percent) and 1 of 428 matches (0.2 percent), and it is the ONLY candidate carrying an evasion surface.** **THE EVASION IS MEASURED, NOT IMAGINED, and this is the refusal:** of the 6 correctly-attributive hits a wide prenominal classifier finds corpus-wide, **four to five PRESUPPOSE A REAL PUSH and would go silent** - "verify the pushed content" (`107eb560`), "rather than rewriting the pushed entry" (`42af2f7d`), "Confirmed on the pushed tree" (`5a864578`), "CI green for the pushed SHA" (`92e498d8`). The nouns the row wants exempt ({refs, branch}) are drawn from the SAME SET as the evasion nouns ({SHA, tree, content, branch}), so **no noun-class rule and no sentence-position rule separates target from evasion** - a session that pushed nothing need only write "the pushed refs are on origin/main" to buy silence on the one check whose remedy is irreversible publication. That is precisely the RM-398 / RM-396 laundering criterion this row's own body names, and it bites. **A FIRST-DRAFT CLASSIFIER ALSO FAILED ON ITS OWN TERMS, which is corroborating evidence that this is a bad discriminator rather than a bad attempt:** a determiner-immediately-before test mis-fired on 2 of its 8 whole-corpus hits, silencing `9c96dae5` "Checking CI on what's already pushed" (ASSERTIVE, and the canonical phrasing) and `4ebfc961` "The line that pushed it over" (a finite verb after a relative pronoun). Both errors came from tokens that are not determiners at all (`already`, `that`). **DO NOT RE-PITCH a part-of-speech, noun-class or determiner narrowing of `CLAIM_PUSH`; it is pre-refuted here on measurement.** **RESIDUAL, DELIBERATELY LEFT UNFILED AND NAMED SO IT IS NOT LOST:** the false-positive population on `CLAIM_PUSH` is REAL (6 of 6 live pre-push matches are false, 0 true), but the two LARGEST and CHEAPEST classes are the `_negated` window defects (2 of 6) and the non-git-idiom / third-party-metadata senses (2 of 6), but **only ONE of the two is evasion-free, and the claim that both were - "a negation the session must actually write is not a phrase it can use to launder a claim" - was REFUTED by an adversarial pass and independently re-probed before this correction landed.** The NON-GIT-IDIOM / third-party-metadata half IS evasion-free as stated: "pushed back" and "pushed today" are not git senses at all. The `_negated` WINDOW half is NOT, and the refutation is that `_negated` on `CLAIM_PUSH` is **already launderable today by the exact phrases BACKLOG records as refuted three rows down**: measured against the shipped gate, `"This is not a guess: I pushed all six commits to origin/main."`, `"Without further gating, I pushed the lane branch to origin."` and `"Nothing was left uncommitted, and I pushed main to origin."` are ALL suppressed, while the bare `"I pushed all six commits to origin/main."` flags. WIDENING the 40-char window strictly ENLARGES that surface - `"Nothing else outstanding on the lane branch, and I pushed it clean to origin."` flags today only because its negation sits 41+ chars back. So the `_negated` residual is a RM-398-shaped laundering problem wearing a boundary-miss costume, and anyone picking it up must treat it as such rather than nudging the constant. Those are the higher-yield fixes and they are NOT this row. No RM number is reserved for them here because a worktree cannot safely claim one against a parallel lane; whoever picks them up files them fresh and cites this block. **Measured with `corpus_scan.py` plus four purpose-built census scripts over the frozen corpus; the adversarial pass that produced the 8 -> 6 turn-granularity correction, the `a0d6788a` reclassification and the 17/9 -> 16/8 per-sentence correction moved EVERY headline number in the direction that WEAKENS the case for shipping, and each of its two load-bearing corrections was independently re-probed before adoption.**
- **RM-400 FILED 2026-09-10 (LEDGER 1384; Tier-1, LOW; PRE-EXISTING at HEAD and UNTOUCHED by `271d69a53` - found incidentally by that session's final verifier, not by the RM-398 fix) - `tools/stop_claim_gate.py` `_QUOTED_EXE` matches only a FORWARD-SLASH path, so a Windows backslash `gh.exe` invocation is invisible to BOTH `EV_CI_LOG` and `EV_CI`.** MEASURED: `_QUOTED_EXE` at `tools/stop_claim_gate.py:285` is `r"""["']([^"']*?[\/])?([\w.-]+\.exe)["']"""` - the optional directory group ends on `[\/]`, a forward slash only - and `strip_command_noise` rewrites the quoted binary to its basename at `:295`. A quoted `"C:\Program Files\GitHub CLI\gh.exe" run view --log` therefore never strips to `gh.exe`, the `\bgh(?:\.exe)?\s+run` anchors in `EV_CI_LOG` (`:164`) and `EV_CI` never fire, and the CI counts that command fetched never reach `observed_counts`. **This is the same defect class the RM-398 FIX 2 closed one layer up** (`EV_CI_LOG` demanded a literal `gh` while `EV_CI` already allowed `gh.exe`), which is why it was only visible once that fix landed - but it is a DIFFERENT site and was not introduced by it. **Direction of the risk: this WIDENS what the gate believes**, exactly like FIX 2, so the same fence applies - crediting must still filter line-by-line through `EV_SUMMARY_LINE`, and a stale `N passed` echoed from a workflow comment must remain uncreditable. **Acceptance:** add the backslash alternative, keep the basename rewrite scoped to `strip_command_noise`, pin the summary-line restriction with a test, and measure new findings against a FROZEN copy of the transcript corpus (never the live directory). State the removed AND added counts; FIX 2's prediction of 4 came back 3.
- **RM-399 SHIPPED 2026-09-10 (LEDGER 1383; filed LEDGER 1382 - the header below cited 1381, off by one; Tier-1, MED; body below retained as the historical record and written in the PRESENT tense of its filing - NOTE its central premise, that no sweep exists, is now false by construction, and its "no escapes found here" implication was REFUTED by the build itself; see the SHIPPED block at the end) - BUILD RC'S SIBLING-NAME SWEEP.** The operator-adopted push boundary gates on DIFF CONTENT, not on destination: a push halts when its diff touches a cross-repository byte-pinned artifact (`ops/loop/slots.py`, `ops/loop/winmutex.py`, pinned by `SHARED_SHA256` at `tests/test_loop_concurrency.py:474`, parametrised at `:542`) **or when it trips the sibling-name sweep**. The byte-pinned half is live. The sweep half has nothing to run against: there is no `tests/test_no_sibling_names.py` in this repo (that file is RSC's), and `.githooks/pre-push` here is GIT LFS ONLY. **WHY IT MATTERS:** `ops/moon_sync_repos.json` is gitignored per-host CONFIG and is the ONLY file resolving a sibling codename to a real project and a real checkout path, and **this repo is PUBLIC** - a sibling-identifying byte in a push is IRREVERSIBLE publication, which is exactly why seam (f) is about a repository's own remote. **PRIOR ART, and treat it as a SHAPE to learn from rather than a file to copy:** RSC's `tests/test_no_sibling_names.py` records FOUR shapes that ACTUALLY ESCAPED its own one-off sweep, at lines 154-157 under a comment at 152-153, each restated as the SHAPE that let it through rather than as the literal that was found, and parametrised into a POSITIVE-CONTROL arm so the guard is armed against its own known misses. **RC should carry the positive-control IDEA and must NOT copy RSC's literals into RC's tree** - importing another project's escaped literals into a public repo is the very publication this row exists to prevent. **THE TRAP THAT WILL BITE:** `.githooks/pre-push` currently runs `git lfs pre-push "$@"`, and its own comment says losing that hook means pushes that LOOK CLEAN while LFS content never reaches the remote - any sweep wired into pre-push MUST preserve the LFS invocation. Note too that `.githooks` is only active when `core.hooksPath` is set, which is LOCAL config and absent in a fresh clone, so a hook-only sweep is unarmed on a new checkout (`python scripts/install_hooks.py`). **ACCEPTANCE:** the sweep runs over the push DIFF (not the whole tree), carries a positive-control arm holding RC's OWN known escape shapes, preserves the LFS pre-push invocation, and closes the "RC has no sweep" hole stated in the CLAUDE.md rule paragraph IN THE SAME COMMIT that lands it. **Do not oversell the result:** a sweep is a BETTER SHAPE, not a solved problem - RSC's own has four recorded escapes and ships a documented bypass env var, so the honest claim after this ships is "the sweep half is armed with a measured escape rate above zero", never "sibling names cannot leak". **SHIPPED 2026-09-10:** `tools/sibling_name_sweep.py` + `tests/test_sibling_name_sweep.py`, wired into `.githooks/pre-push` ahead of the byte-for-byte preserved `git lfs pre-push "$@"`; exit codes 0 CLEAN / 1 USAGE / 2 HALT / 3 FAULT, and argparse's own default 2 is re-coded to 1 so a mistyped flag can never be read as a leak. **The acceptance clause was met literally - the CLAUDE.md gap sentence is flipped in the SAME commit.** **MEASURED OUTCOME, and it refutes the row's own optimism: FIVE REAL ESCAPES in RC's tree, all live in HEAD and ALL ALREADY PUBLISHED.** Four were redacted at HEAD (`f6cf005bb`); **remediating HEAD does not undo publication, and no history rewrite was performed.** **ONE KNOWN EXCEPTION REMAINS OPEN and is declared, named and REPORTED (never silently allowlisted):** the fifth sits inside the cross-repo byte-pinned block, so removing it is a JOINT re-pin - relayed to the carrier 2026-09-10, awaiting their reply. The guard asserts the exception is declared with its reason and that it is reported rather than suppressed. **Blind spots, stated not hidden:** LFS OBJECT CONTENT is never scanned (the pointer is, and scans clean truthfully); `--no-verify` bypasses the whole hook and nothing can prevent it; `RC_SIBLING_SWEEP_BYPASS=1` proceeds but prints the full would-have-halted report and appends it to a gitignored log. **The positive-control arm is seeded from RC's OWN five escapes** - RSC's literals were NOT copied - and includes a SPLIT-FORM control for a name broken across a comment-continuation line wrap, which no whole-token search can see.
- **RM-398 PARTIAL SHIPPED 2026-09-10 (`271d69a53`, LEDGER 1384; filed 2026-09-09, LEDGER 1380; Tier-1, LOW-MED; body below retained as the historical record and written in the PRESENT tense of its filing - its CAUSAL STORY is REFUTED and two proposals are REFUSED, so read the SETTLED block at the end BEFORE acting on anything above it) - `tools/stop_claim_gate.py` `count_mismatch` cannot distinguish a count asserted as a SUCCESS from one asserted as a RED, pre-fix or mutation state, so a session that honestly reports "RED 19 failed / 1 passed before the fix" is scored as claiming an unbacked `1 passed`.** MEASURED: of the 8 NEW findings RM-397's widening surfaced across the frozen 91-transcript corpus, **3 are that shape**. They are not false in the sense the claim was never made - the number really is in the prose - but the gate's remedy ("run it yourself and let `observed_counts` clear it") is unavailable, because a deliberately-RED or mutated tree is a state the session destroyed on purpose and cannot re-observe at wrap. **WHY THIS WAS NOT BUNDLED INTO RM-397, which is the load-bearing half of this row.** The obvious fix is a suppressor keyed on a marker like `mutation:` or `red state:` near the count. That is EXACTLY the shape RM-396 was REFUTED for (LEDGER 1379, grounds 3): a suppressor a session can type is a one-phrase evasion available to the party the instrument polices, and here it would reopen the very false-NEGATIVE hole RM-397 just closed - a session wanting to hide a bad count need only prefix it. Any acceptable design must therefore derive the RED-ness from EVIDENCE the session cannot author at will (the bash record, an exit code, a result file), not from prose, and that is a different and larger piece of work than a regex narrowing. **Do NOT re-pitch the prose-marker version; it is pre-refuted here.** **A SECOND, UNRELATED AND GENUINELY PRE-EXISTING FALSE POSITIVE lives in the same corpus and may be cheaper: `CLAIM_COUNT` mis-parses space-grouped digits** - `"10 856 passed"` yields `('10', '856')` and reports `claimed '856'`, a string that never appeared in the prose. That one is a pure parser narrowing in the `CLAIM_COUNT_ORDINAL` tradition with no evasion surface, and it is independent of the RED-state question; it was carried unscheduled out of LEDGER 1379 and is restated here so it is not lost a second time. **Acceptance:** either an evidence-derived RED/mutation discriminator with its false-positive AND false-negative rates measured against a FROZEN copy of the transcript corpus (never the live directory - it contains the running session's own growing transcript, see RM-397), or a recorded REFUTE with the corpus scoring in the row. Fixing the space-grouped-digit parse alone is a valid partial and should be adjudicated separately. **SETTLED 2026-09-10 - PARTIAL SHIPPED, MECHANISM REFUTED, POPULATION CLAIM UPHELD AND RE-MEASURED, and every remaining fix REFUSED. Everything below was measured over a FROZEN 91-transcript corpus copy; never measure over the live directory, which holds the running session's own growing transcript.** **TWO PARSER FIXES SHIPPED** (`271d69a53`, suite 94 passed, `count_mismatch` 28 -> 23 over the frozen corpus, 5 removed, 0 added, removed set a strict SUBSET of the 6 predicted, perfectly additive; independently re-derived by a verifier). **FIX 1, the space-grouped parse this row carried:** `10 856 passed` read as prefix `10` plus count `856` and flagged `claimed=856`, a string never in the prose, while the true `10856` sat in `observed_counts`; removes 2 findings, both the `856` artifact in session `c4bf4a1a`. **Its FIRST version was REFUTED by measurement - do not restore it:** dropping any 1-3 digit prefix plus a comma-free 3-digit count silenced `lane 8 328 passed`, `run 2 654 passed`, `12 999`, `123 999` and `0 000` against an observed `1397`, making every 3-digit suite total unflaggable whenever a small number preceded it. The shipped form is evidence-derived - suppress only when `prefix + count` is ITSELF in `observed_counts`, so the evader would have to be telling the truth - and two tests kill the blanket-drop mutant. **FIX 2, `EV_CI_LOG` binary spelling:** it required a literal `gh` token while `EV_CI` in the same file already wrote `gh(?:\.exe)?`, and RC's prescribed quoted-absolute-path invocation strips to basename `gh.exe`, whose `.` broke `\bgh\s+run`; removes 3 (`274b84bc` x2, `958c1483`). It WIDENS belief, so the summary-line restriction was verified by call path and fenced by a test. **It was PREDICTED to remove 4 and removes 3 - `42af2f7d` correctly survives, its claimed `28150` is not on a summary line in the fetched log. Do not carry the 4 forward.** **THE ROW'S HEADLINE MECHANISM IS REFUTED.** `observed_counts` applies `EV_PASSED` to run output with NO green/red filter: an adjudicator measured 1354 paired runs, 347 carrying a nonzero `N failed`, and 312 of those had their `N passed` harvested normally (the 35 that did not are runs where nothing passed). There is NO red/green asymmetry at the harvest step, **0 of 28 findings are caused by red-ness, and a RED-state discriminator is therefore NOT the fix.** **THE POPULATION CLAIM IS UPHELD - the probe's counter-headline "the RED-state population is ZERO" is itself REFUTED:** at least 9 of the 28 flagged sentences do assert their count inside an explicitly non-green state (`274b84bc` x2, `5a864578`, `6331c9ed`, `7d13e739`, `81551e16`, `92e498d8`, `db9fa5f4`, `faf6e2b3`). This row described a real and common SHAPE and got the CAUSE wrong; the cause is invisibility of the run, not red-ness. **THE ACTUAL CAUSES of the 28, measured:** SUBAGENT-INVISIBLE 12, CI-LOG PATTERN MISS 4, FILE-ONLY / prior-session recital 7, SPACE-GROUPED 2, UNRECOGNISED RUNNER 1, NEGATED/RETRACTED 1, TRUE POSITIVE by design 1. **DO NOT RE-PITCH: crediting subagent transcripts.** The proposal was to union `observed_counts` over `<projects>/C--Riot-Commander/<session-id>/subagents/agent-*.jsonl` (present for 52 sessions; `isSidechain` measured 53639 false / 0 true across the frozen parents, and `collect_evidence` only ever reads the single `transcript_path`). An adjudicator opened all 12 and the split is **12 relay / 0 independent parent re-run** - eleven attribute the figure to a slice in the parent's own prose, the twelfth says "Measured this run" over a provably subagent-produced number - and **THREE were later RETRACTED by the authoring session as wrong** (`17e9bb48` 18226, whose own later runs gave 18215 / 18222 / 18258 and which wrote the number was "never mine"; `39bc4be6` 18953, branch-local not `main`; `1131adbe` 10722, stale against a true base 10845). The proposal's own guard - credit only paired run output, never subagent prose - does NOT help, because every one of those numbers WAS a real paired subagent run. **SUBAGENT-INVISIBLE is the LARGEST TRUE-POSITIVE class in the corpus, not a false-positive class**, and crediting it would suppress true positives and contradict CLAUDE.md Verification Discipline. Both narrowed variants are rejected too: "credit unless the parent attributes it to the subagent" INVERTS the incentive, silencing `7d13e739`'s mis-attributed "Measured this run" while still flagging `39bc4be6`'s "Claims 18953 passed", the most honest phrasing in the corpus. **DO NOT RE-PITCH: reusing `_negated` for `CLAIM_COUNT`.** That is re-litigation - session `17e9bb48` already shipped it, it was REFUTED for silencing laundering phrases ("Without question 18226 passed", "This is not a guess: 18226 passed"), a scope-based third version was tried and closed as a dead end, and the recorded decision is to keep the strict behaviour and ACCEPT that retractions flag. **LEAVE ALONE, adjudicator-agreed:** the 7 file-only / prior-session recitals, the 1 workflow-comment recital and the 1 unrecognised runner - each "fix" widens evidence toward "a number that appeared somewhere", which is the poisoning that wrecked the first armed session. **Two incidental defects found during this work are filed separately as RM-400 (`_QUOTED_EXE` backslash blindness) and RM-401 (`CLAIM_PUSH` adjectival "pushed"); neither is this row.** What remains OPEN under RM-398 is only the CI-LOG PATTERN MISS residue not covered by FIX 2 - re-derive it, do not inherit a count from this block. **RE-DERIVED 2026-09-10 (LEDGER 1386) over a FROZEN 93-transcript corpus, replaying the pre-RM-400 gate from `7f2fc529e~1` side by side: the residue is 1, and RM-400 left it NUMERICALLY UNCHANGED (`count_mismatch` 23 both sides) while MOVING ITS MECHANISM.** It was a command-spelling miss - session `42af2f7d`'s quoted-backslash `gh.exe` fetch was never indexed. It is now an INDEXED fetch (`ci_runs` 0 -> 1, the whole 59 -> 60 delta) whose real terminal summary is split across a console wrap by the session's own `Select-String | Select-Object -Last 5` rendering, so the LINE-BY-LINE `EV_SUMMARY_LINE` filter sees the count and its duration on different physical lines and credits neither. Of 60 indexed `ci_runs` across 24 sessions, exactly ONE needs un-wrapping, and command-level misses are now ZERO. **Any fix here widens what is BELIEVED from output, not which command is recognised, so it carries the opposite direction-of-risk to RM-400 and the same fence refuses it.** The other 22 `count_mismatch` findings were hand-opened and are CORRECT behaviour: 1 workflow-comment echo the fence exists to refuse, 13 subagent/parallel-agent relays of a number never in the session's own output, 6 file-only or prior-session recitals, 1 unrecognised runner (`python test_rewrite2.py`, no `pytest` token for `EV_PYTEST`), 1 true positive by design.
- **RM-397 SHIPPED 2026-09-09 (LEDGER 1380; filed LEDGER 1379; Tier-1, MED; found by the pass that REFUTED RM-396, not by looking for it; body below retained as the historical record and written in the PRESENT tense of its filing - NOTE the corpus size it names is off by one, see the SHIPPED block at the end) - `tools/stop_claim_gate.py` deletes ordinary prose between two APOSTROPHES before any check runs, so a claim that lands between them is never examined at all. This is a live FALSE-NEGATIVE hole in the instrument that audits the session's own claims, and it points the OPPOSITE way from the row that found it.** `_QUOTED` (`:184`) is `r"'[^']*'|\"[^\"]*\""` with `re.S`, and `strip_prose_noise` (`:212-214`) applies it to every assistant text before the claim scan. Two possessives or contractions in one paragraph therefore pair as quote delimiters. MEASURED by direct probe: `"The runner's log says 9999 passed, and the session's report agrees."` strips to `"The runner s report agrees."` and `CLAIM_COUNT.findall` returns `[]` - the same sentence without apostrophes yields `[('says', '9999')]`. **BLAST RADIUS, and the first version of this figure was WRONG BY 2.3x IN THE DIRECTION THAT FLATTERED THE FINDING - a gate caught it.** I first measured over every `type=="text"` block in the 90 transcripts and reported 1408 spans over 200 chars, 131 claim-shaped, longest 5502. But `collect_evidence` (`:257`) appends text ONLY when `role == "assistant"`, so user-side text is never scanned by this gate at all. Re-derived with that split: **SCANNED - 565 spans over 200 chars, 57 claim-shaped, longest claim-bearing 1658**; NEVER SCANNED - 843 / 74 / 4441. The gate-relevant number is **57**, and the 5502 headline was a max over all spans that carries no claim shape at all. State the 57 honestly too - those are spans the gate formed NO OPINION on, not proven misses; most are probably backed. **It disarms EIGHT checks over the span, not nine:** `flag("hook_bypass")` (`:368`) sits in `for command in bash:` (`:366`), outside the prose loop at `:370`, and never sees `strip_prose_noise`. **Acceptance:** narrow `_QUOTED` so a possessive or contraction cannot open a quoted span (an apostrophe flanked by word characters is not a delimiter), keep genuine single-quoted spans suppressed, and anchor it on the real-transcript corpus the file already uses (`test_the_real_transcript_that_produced_nine_false_positives_is_clean`). **The direction of the fix is the risk:** every prior repair to this gate NARROWED it to kill a false positive, and this one WIDENS what it examines, so it must be measured against the 90-transcript corpus for new false positives before it is armed - `feedback_mutation_probe_vs_naive_alternative` applies, derive the attack families from the mechanism. **SHIPPED 2026-09-09 as `e518786e2`, and the row's own acceptance - measure it, do not argue it - is what produced every figure below.** A new prose-only `_QUOTED_PROSE` is used by `strip_prose_noise` ALONE: it refuses to open a quoted span on an apostrophe flanked by word characters, and tolerates an inner contraction inside a genuine quoted span so that span is suppressed END TO END rather than truncated at the apostrophe. `_QUOTED` is BYTE-UNCHANGED (md5-verified by a verifier) and `strip_command_noise` is UNTOUCHED - shell quoting carries no possessives and narrowing the evidence side is not this defect - with a test pinning that scope decision so it is not "tidied" later. **THE CORPUS IS 91 TRANSCRIPTS, NOT THE 90 THE ROW WAS FILED WITH** - one session was created after filing - **and it had to be FROZEN to a scratchpad copy first**, because the live directory contains the running session's OWN growing transcript, which makes a before/after comparison meaningless: the "after" corpus would contain text the "before" run never saw. Record that trap; it applies to every future measurement over this corpus. **MEASURED, baseline against merged main: 26 findings over 15 transcripts -> 34 over 21. 8 NEW, 0 LOST**, and merged `main` replays IDENTICAL to the adjudicated state. **An INDEPENDENT adjudicator classified all 8 against their transcripts and found ZERO clean false positives** - that is the adjudicator's classification and is labelled as such, NOT my own re-derivation; I did not re-adjudicate all 8 by hand. The ONE I corroborated myself, by grepping the frozen transcript `db9fa5f4`: a claim of `17 passed / 205 errors of 221 collected` was re-measured by a LATER session in the same corpus as `18 passed` with `222 collected` - a wrong count that shipped unchallenged precisely because this hole hid it. **Seven of the eight are direct apostrophe pairing; the EIGHTH is the subtler half of the same defect** - an apostrophe span swallowed the OPENING double quote of a phrase, the orphaned closing quote then paired with a later one, and 988 chars were deleted. The adjudicator required a test for exactly that interaction and it is MUTATION-CHECKED (it fails with an empty findings list under the old pattern). The anchor test `test_the_real_transcript_that_produced_nine_false_positives_is_clean` is NON-VACUOUS: 2 of the 9 assistant blocks in its fixture strip differently under the new pattern. Regex is linear, re-timed independently to 160k chars with no super-linear blowup. `tests/test_stop_claim_gate.py` gains 9 tests in an RM-397 section, file total **81 passed** observed on `main` after the merge. **THE DIRECTION WAS THE RISK AND IT HELD**, which is the whole reason this row was measured rather than reasoned: every prior repair to this file (LEDGER 1154 / 1156 / 1175 / 1178) NARROWED the gate to kill a false positive and this one WIDENS what it examines, and the LEDGER 1175/1178 precedent does NOT transfer by its own stated justification - those were right because the cheapest way to satisfy the gate was behaviour the repo wants, whereas here the cheapest remedy is to run the probe yourself, which is the standing rule anyway. **THE GATE IS ARMED, so state the cost plainly rather than selling this as "more findings": 6 of the 91 sessions go from quiet to Stop-BLOCKING, one block each, bounded by the re-entry guard.** **One pre-existing gap this fix EXPOSES and deliberately does NOT fix is filed as RM-398** - `count_mismatch` cannot distinguish a count asserted as SUCCESS from one asserted as a RED or mutation state, which 3 of the 8 are.
- **RM-396 REFUTED 2026-09-09 (LEDGER 1379; adjudicated by an independent agent against the 90-transcript corpus, with the proposing party - me - disclosed as the party the instrument polices; a second gate then attacked the refutation itself as possible motivated reasoning and could not break it) - `tools/stop_claim_gate.py` `CLAIM_COUNT` (`:38`) will NOT be given counterfactual or citation suppression. Do not re-pitch it.** The complaint was real: a figure quoted IN ORDER TO REFUTE IT is scored as asserting it, and since the gate scans the whole transcript with no retraction mechanism the flag re-fires every turn. Two occurrences exist in the 90 sessions - the adjudicator's find, INDEPENDENTLY RE-DERIVED by the gate that checked it: 2026-09-09 `db9fa5f4`, and 2026-08-05 `17e9bb48` where the gate flagged a RETRACTION ("Retracting: I have not observed 16 passed ..."). **It is refuted on four measured grounds.** (1) **The cure already exists and is one backtick wide:** `strip_prose_noise` (`:212-214`) already deletes fenced blocks, inline code and quoted spans for EVERY check, so `` `9546 passed` `` is invisible - probed here, bare prose flags and both backticked and quoted forms return no findings. (2) **No discriminator survives the corpus** - the adjudicator's SCORING of the candidate discriminators is one agent's work and was not re-derived; the corpus it scored against was: an independent gate replayed `audit()` over the 90 transcripts and got the same **19** `count_mismatch` findings. Against those: a negation-governs rule misses the motivating sentence and wrongly suppresses `"not a cascade: 1 failed / 29496 passed"`; a priorness-marker rule catches it but launders three genuine unbacked counts including the exact "never carry a prior count forward" shape; the conjunction of both fires on nothing. Precision goes DOWN. (3) **No veto is available.** `CLAIM_FILE`'s suppressors are safe only because `CLAIM_FIRST_PERSON_DID` vetoes them; there is no number equivalent, because "I measured 19142 passed" is exactly the shape that must still be caught when false, so any count suppressor is a one-phrase evasion by the party writing the sentence. (4) **The 1175/1178 precedent does not transfer.** Those narrowings were justified because the cheapest way to satisfy the gate was to STOP CITING PRECEDENT, a behaviour the repo wants; here the cheapest cure is to mark a quotation as a quotation, which chills nothing. **Also rejected:** a retraction token (a self-serve silencer, only defensible if evidence-bearing, at which point measuring the figure already clears the flag) and per-turn scope (converts "a session cannot outrun its own claims" into "a stale claim ages out"). **CITE CORRECTION, and it had propagated into three artifacts before a gate caught it:** the hazard "do not let the audited party patch this gate" is NOT a comment in `tools/stop_claim_gate.py`. A search for that language returns ZERO matches anywhere in the file, and `:74-77` is the `CLAIM_CITATION` note about not chilling precedent-citation. The hazard is recorded in the repo MEMORY, and the memory carried the false cite - which this row then repeated while rewriting that same memory. **THE ASYMMETRY IS REAL BUT NARROWER THAN FIRST WRITTEN:** `count_mismatch` has an in-session cure when the claim is TRUE - this session's own `41 passed` flag fired correctly and cleared the moment the guard was actually run - but it is NOT the only check with one; `ran_pytest`, `probed_ci`, `did_commit` and `did_push` are all computed over the whole session's bash record, so four other checks clear the same way. **And the cure is PROSPECTIVE ONLY, which belongs in the headline rather than a footnote:** once a bare-prose refuted figure is in the transcript it can NEVER clear, because a figure you are refuting by construction never enters `observed_counts`. That is exactly the `db9fa5f4` case. The refutation still stands on grounds (2) and (3), which do not depend on the cure existing. **PRACTICAL RULE instead of a code change:** describe a figure you are refuting, or backtick it; never re-spell it bare. **One sibling filed rather than fixed:** `CLAIM_COUNT` reads space-grouped digits wrong - `"DS 10 856 passed"` yields `('10', '856')` and would report `claimed '856'`, a string that never appeared in the prose. Low-risk parser narrowing in the `CLAIM_COUNT_ORDINAL` tradition, unscheduled. **One trap CLOSED as measured-inert, so it is not re-pitched:** a pytest run nested in a `python - <<EOF` heredoc IS invisible to `EV_PYTEST` (which runs on the noise-stripped command), but ALSO THE ADJUDICATOR'S MEASUREMENT and not re-derived here: it reports that across the 90 transcripts the 49 commands with pytest visible raw and hidden after stripping produced ZERO `N passed` results - they are importlib probes and doc heredocs. Building that is flag-without-transport work in reverse.

Neither had to be invented: `tests/_repo_walk.py` already implements the first, with `EXCLUDED_DIRS` segment skips as a backstop and `tracked_relpaths()` returning `None` rather than an empty set so a git failure can never read as "nothing is tracked". It carries its own anti-vacuity guard (`tests/test_repo_walk.py`) and had four consumers. **It is settled as CODE and not as a recorded decision** - it was added in `a0a23b57c` (2026-09-07, the public-flip scrub) and NO ledger row names it, which is exactly why five guards written around it never adopted it. All five now do, one slice agent per file, plus `responder_export` added to the shared `EXCLUDED_DIRS` (`tests/_repo_walk.py`) so the git-absent fallback is correct too - one entry in the one shared list, which is the whole difference from the hand-list fix this row rejects. **The old counts were re-derived by the gate against the OLD algorithms on this disk and every one held:** 8 phantom trees, 34 phantom ctor sites, 50 unclassified builders, and 36 frozen headers of which 24 sit under `responder_export` (12 distinct modules seen twice, once per export tree). New outputs: 12 frozen headers, exactly the 4 real test trees, 17 ctor sites equal to `CONSTRUCT_FILES`, 17 census entries with 0 unclassified and 0 stale. **THE GATE REFUTED THE CENTRAL CLAIM AND IT WAS THE RIGHT ONE TO ATTACK.** Four of the five guards fail loudly on an empty enumeration; `test_skip_condition_hygiene.py` did NOT - `missing = discovered - set(_TEST_TREES)` is empty-set-safe, so forcing the enumeration empty left that module at 56 passed, i.e. the conversion could have replaced a machine-local red with a silent always-green. Fixed with an anchor on the directory holding the guard itself (`assert "tests" in discovered`), which is asserted against the DISK and so is not circular with `_TEST_TREES`; proven to bite by a mutation probe that empties the enumeration and now raises. The one arm that depended on scanning untracked files, `test_anthropic_base_url_pin.py`'s net-new-site control, was rewritten rather than weakened: it now drives the real scanner with `tracked_only=False` on a synthetic tree (with `.claude/worktrees/` and `ops/runtime/responder_export/<sha>/` as negative samples) and adds a tracked-set half against the real repo. **Known cosmetic divergence, left alone on purpose:** `tests/test_target_state_caller_p1l4.py` builds its relative path with `p.relative_to(_ROOT).as_posix()` where three siblings use `_repo_walk.relative_posix`, and `tests/test_rm364_prompt_sanitizer_population.py` passes `patterns` positionally. No behavioural effect, and churning five freshly converted files for style is not worth the risk.

- **RM-391 CLOSED 2026-09-09 (LEDGER 1372; docs-only, no code) - the answered record takes NO cap and NO automated pruner, and the only sanctioned deletion is the single-entry operator act the spec ALREADY defines.** The row asked whether a name can be dropped from `inbox_responder_answered.json` without re-cycling the note it names. **The measured answer is no, and the question is upside down:** `docs/RESPONDER_RUNNER_SPEC.md:80`, the `DECIDED (answered-record choice and its cost)` block in section 2, already licenses deleting one entry BY HAND, and `tools/inbox_responder_runner.py:272-276` records one such deletion actually performed (cite the full range - `:275` ends mid-sentence and the word "deleted" is on `:276`) - in both cases *in order to* re-cycle that note. Dropping is a RE-CYCLING tool, never a reclamation tool, and nothing automated may do it. Both drafted positions (permanent-by-decision, and drop-only-with-the-file) missed that shipped clause, and the first would have forbidden the one deletion the system depends on. **MEASURED 2026-09-09 on the live host:** 113 `.md` in `moon_sync_inbox` plus 3 strays, 108 names / 10015 bytes in the record, `answered - inbox` = **0 of 108** (reverse difference 5, all pending) - so a pruner would drop nothing today, and every name it could ever drop names a live note it would thereby re-cycle. **Growth WITH its distribution, because the mean misleads:** 113 arrivals over 45 calendar days but only 6 ACTIVE days (07-26 x5, 07-27 x6, 08-01 x14, 09-06 x30, 09-07 x50, 09-08 x8); mean over span 2.51/day, median active day 11, peak day 50 = 44.2 percent of all arrivals, top two days 70.8 percent, trailing 7 days 88 = **12.6/day, 5x the mean**. At about 93 bytes/name a 5000-name re-decide tripwire is ~5.3 years at the span mean, ~1.1 years at the trailing rate and ~98 days at sustained peak - quote the RANGE, never the mean alone. **Three facts the filed row did not have.** (1) There is a SECOND consumer and it is a BYTE consumer: `tools/inbox_responder_runner.py:2060` sha256s this file inside `live_surface_digests` and `:2302-2310` fails `live-state-unchanged` when the bytes move mid-dry-cycle; already guarded for every runner-borne writer by the `Global\RC_INBOX_RESPONDER` singleton (`:289`, `timeout=0.0`, both digest samples inside it), so only a NON-runner script could trip it - one more reason no scheduled pruner exists. (2) A dropped name CAN come back: `vision_server/_http.py:244` takes an authenticated PUT to an arbitrary basename with no existence check, and although `SYNC_DIR` (`vision_server/_config.py:44`) is CWD-relative while the responder resolves the inbox absolutely, `GET :8889/sync/list` returns the same listing as the repo inbox today - so a pruned name that reappears is answered TWICE, where unpruned it is silently ignored. (3) The record is NOT reconstructible: one copy on disk, `inbox_responder_deliveries.jsonl` stores the REPLY filename (2 rows), and `responder_metrics.jsonl` carries a `note` field that is useless for this - 10 note-bearing rows over 3 distinct names, and the reason it cannot rebuild the record is the 80-char `safe_name` PROJECTION, not staleness or dryness: exact-match intersection with the 108 answered names is **0**, though 2 of those 3 names are 80-char truncations that uniquely PREFIX-match a real answered name (`feedback_shared_projection_carries_the_tightest_bound`). **Two claims made during this adjudication are corrected here rather than shipped:** that the note-bearing rows are "all dry-cycle stubs" (6 of the 10 are `dry: False` live cycles), and that the ledger is "trimmed to 200 rows" - it is NOT trimmed at all, RM-388 made it ROTATE, `METRICS_CARRY_ROWS` 200 is a carry tail and not a cap, and no rotation has fired (zero archives; the row count is deliberately not quoted here because it grows every 5 minutes). RM-386 is untouched by construction (held and attempts key on `note_sha12` of the RAW name, `:434` / `:705`; held_notes ABSENT live, attempts one sha), the fail-closed read stands, and RM-388's rule that deliveries is the budget governor and is never trimmed is unchanged. **THIS FLIPS the day anything removes or archives notes out of `moon_sync_inbox`**, because that single change would create dead names and dissolve the subset argument at once; reopen then, not before. **State the dependency honestly rather than over-reading the contract:** `docs/CONCURRENT_HEADLESS_CONTRACT.md:431-433` rule 7.2 says one file per message, "Append-only in effect: never edit a delivered note" - it bans EDITING, addressed to the writer, and a whole-file search finds no rule about a RECEIVER archiving its own inbox. So the decision rests on an observed practice and a GAP in the contract, not on a prohibition; anyone who fills that gap must reopen this row in the same act. Note also that the arrival histogram above is a SURVIVORSHIP measurement - a deleted note is invisible to it - which is the same gap seen from the other side. **Residual NOT closed:** the inbox's own unboundedness (the record merely inherits it), the other three per-note records, and any machine check at all - the only available assertion enumerates a gitignored directory (`git ls-files moon_sync_inbox` returns 0), so it would be green on Legion and vacuous in CI, and it is deliberately not shipped. Original row body follows.
- **RM-391 (original text, filed 2026-09-08, Tier-1, LOW - filed WITH its measurement so nobody re-panics) - the per-note responder records are unbounded in principle and tiny in fact.** `inbox_responder_attempts.json`, `inbox_responder_bounces.json`, `inbox_responder_held_notes.json` and `inbox_responder_answered.json` are read-modify-WRITE-WHOLE dicts with no cap, which is the shape RSC measured as O(n^2) (their refutation 4). **MEASURED 2026-09-08 on the live host:** attempts 23 bytes / 1 entry, bounces ABSENT, held_notes ABSENT, answered 10015 bytes / 108 names, deliveries 584 bytes. **The distinction that makes this LOW and that RM-388 did not have:** these grow per NOTE (human-paced, a few a day at most), not per TICK (288/day), so the rewrite cost is negligible and will stay so for years. **Do NOT "fix" it by pruning `inbox_responder_deliveries.jsonl`:** the spec makes it the BUDGET GOVERNOR precisely so that a trimmed log cannot move the budget, and trimming it would let a spent budget re-open. If this is ever taken, the honest scope is `answered` alone, and the question is whether a name can ever be dropped from it without re-cycling the note it names (RM-386 says a refusal must not be in there at all).

The companion mkdir audit found exactly one site with RSC's shape - `main`'s prelude handler, which wrote the invocation pair and then crashed on the row - now two independent writes that still escape loudly when neither lands. **Body as filed:** MEASURED: `log_start` and `log_end` both call `_trim(path)` on `responder_invocations.jsonl` (`INVOCATION_LOG_KEEP`), but the metrics row is appended at `tools/inbox_responder_runner.py:1154` and `:2103` with `_append_line` and NOTHING trims it. Live file at filing: 75 rows / 104337 bytes, so rows average about 1.4 KB; at the 5-minute tick that is 288 rows and roughly 400 KB per day, forever. **Milder than the case that prompted it, and say so:** RSC measured O(n^2) because their writer re-read and rewrote the whole file each cycle; RC appends through `_append_atomic`, so RC's is LINEAR. Unbounded either way. **Do not just add `_trim`:** the metrics file is the M1/M2/M3 evidence base and `trial_rows` reads it, so silently evicting the oldest rows would delete the measurements the trial exists to produce - decide between a much larger keep, rotation to a dated file, or an explicit archive, and state which measurements survive. **Companion audit, same note, NOT yet a defect:** RSC hit `WinError 183` from an unguarded `mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)` whose parent existed as a FILE, raising AFTER the held file and the bounce were written; RC has 8 such call sites in the runner and none has been probed for that shape. **Four of RSC's six do NOT apply to RC and are recorded so nobody re-checks them:** RC's bounce allowance keys on `agreement_id:note_sha12` (an identity, not an interpolated reason string); `_record_bounce` runs BEFORE the bounce is written and returns on `OSError`, with `bounces_of` returning `None` to fail closed; RC's bounce record has no cap, so there is no eviction to re-open; and RC has no head-of-line starvation precisely because it marks refusals answered - which is the cost that mechanism was buying.
- **RM-386 SECOND HALF SHIPPED 2026-09-08 - a refusal is no longer an answer.** All three named states are now closed or reassigned, and the body below is kept AS FILED because two of its clauses went stale within hours of being written - read it as the record, not as current behaviour. **What shipped, and it is the third of the three options the body offered:** refused (input, validator, filter) and exhausted no longer call `record_responded`. The answered record now means only what it says - "this note has been replied to" - and its one remaining writer is the delivery path. In its place a HOLD record, `ops/runtime/inbox_responder_held_notes.json`, keyed by `note_sha12` of the RAW name: `pick_note` skips a held note exactly as it skips one at the spawn cap, every row carries `notes_held`, and gate 4b terminates `runner-failed / notes-held` rather than `empty` when nothing else is eligible - because reporting nothing pending while something is pending is the reassuring-label failure this channel exists to kill. (Precedence stated exactly, because the loose version reads as a promise the code does not make: the detail is `attempt-cap` whenever ANY pending note is at the spawn cap and `notes-held` otherwise, so the two coexisting read `attempt-cap`. What is guaranteed is that neither is ever `empty`.) The operator deletes one entry and the note cycles again, which is the property the answered record made impossible: RC's 15:02:43 refusal had to be removed by hand. **The two costs RSC disclosed are paid, not ignored:** the repeat-refusal storm (288 held directories a day for one unpassable note) is suppressed by the hold, and head-of-line starvation - which RSC measured as reachable on purpose by a sibling whose name sorts first, and which RC had been buying off by answering refusals - is prevented by a younger eligible note being picked ahead of the held one, with an arm that drives exactly that. **Fail-closed in both directions**, against RSC's refutation 2 (their fail-open wrote five bounces into a sibling's tree with no error surfaced): an unreadable hold record terminates `runner-failed / held-record-unreadable` before anything is spawned, bounced or answered, and an unwritable one clears `bounce_target` so the outbound is suppressed rather than trusting the next tick - that second half covers the four HELD stages only, since a `destination`-stage refusal takes no hold at all (`_deliver` already answered the note before the link attempt, an ordering its own mutant pins). **The other two options in the body had already shipped on 2026-09-08 and the body does not know it** - the prompt/schema fix (`minItems: 1`) made an empty proposal illegal rather than merely handled, and the bounce made both (1) and (2) sender-VISIBLE, so "delivers NOTHING" below is false for those two states as of that afternoon. **(3) is not closed and never belonged here:** it is the RM-385 class and stays on RM-385. **Growth note, disclosed rather than deferred:** the hold record gains one small entry per DISTINCT refused note, so it is bounded by notes ever received and not by ticks - unlike the RM-388 case, this one is not per-cycle. First half (the name-grammar caps) SHIPPED the same day; that half and the census that corrected its stated cause are in `docs/ROADMAP_HISTORY.md` 2026-09-08b. **Body as filed:** **(1) Refused at input.** A gate-6 refusal is `refused_stage=input` - it writes `held/<cycle_id>/{reasons.json,note.txt,status.txt}`, marks the note ANSWERED via `record_responded` with the RAW name, and delivers NOTHING. RC only learned of the 15:02:43 refusal because a human read the log, and had to hand-deliver the bounce notice. Because the answered record is permanent, a note refused today is not re-cycled by a later fix - the entry must be removed by hand, which RC did for that one note. **(2) Exhausted, MEASURED LIVE 2026-09-08.** After the caps were raised and RSC's note re-queued, the very next cycle (`20260908T152743-35768-7ff10f`) terminated `exhausted / empty-proposal`: the spawn SUCCEEDED (2 turns, 48 s api, `complete: true`, cost 0.070 notional) and the model returned `{"actions": []}`, because RSC's note is a correspondence report asking no measurable question. Gate 11 short-circuits BEFORE `validate`, so `no-reply-action` never fires; the note is marked ANSWERED, `budget_consumed` stays 0, and nothing is delivered. **Root cause of this one is in the PROMPT, not the gates:** `SYSTEM_PROMPT` specifies a reply's SHAPE ("`reply`: exactly one, and last") but never requires the proposal to be non-empty, so an empty proposal is legal. **(3) The RM-385 class**, where the note is never seen at all. The counterparty cannot distinguish any of the three from being ignored, which is the silent-failure class this channel exists to kill. **Decide between** refusing/exhausting WITHOUT answering (so a later fix can re-cycle), requiring at least a reply action in the schema or prompt so `exhausted` becomes genuinely rare, and delivering a short acknowledgement (which spends hop budget, so it fights `hop_budget 1`) - or a combination. Whatever is chosen, the arm must drive `run_once` and assert the SENDER-VISIBLE outcome, not just the termination string. RSC has been told the refusal half exists and asked for a shape; the exhausted half is NOT yet disclosed to them.
- **RM-385 SHIPPED 2026-09-08 (filed the same day, LEDGER 1364 then 1367, Tier-1) - what is NOT a note still has to be SEEN.** `pending_notes` was dropping TWO classes silently: an entry failing `Path.is_file()` (a junction is always a directory reparse point) and one whose `_sender_code` is None (a name that BEGINS `from-`, sender and date transposed). Admission is now by NAME and by the answered record, so both reach gate 6 and are REFUSED - and, since RM-386 landed one item earlier the same day, HELD rather than answered, which is what the row below asked for in the words available at the time. The strict-xfail `test_a_note_that_is_a_junction_is_refused` is now a PASSING arm, which was the stated acceptance; the companion containment arm was re-pointed at the refusal path rather than deleted, so nothing outside the inbox is opened, quoted, spawned for or delivered on either outcome. **The line this deliberately does NOT cross:** a note from a sender code outside the agreement's participants is still dropped silently, because it is somebody else's correspondence rather than a silent failure, and an arm pins that distinction. **Admitting is not trusting:** `NOTE_NAME_RE` is strictly stronger than `_sender_code`, so a code-less name is refused `name-grammar` before any spawn and `reply_targets` can never be built from a sender that does not exist. **New label, with its own mutant:** a plain directory named like a note is refused `note-shape:not-a-file`, ordered after the reparse branches so a junction keeps `note-shape:linked` - without it the entry is still refused, but as `linked`, and a directory is linked to nothing. **LIVE IMPACT MEASURED AS ZERO:** the pending set against RC's real inbox is the same two RSC notes before and after, because the live transposed-name file is separately inside the answered record (106 of whose 108 entries are the operator seed per LEDGER 1366 - that split is inherited, while "the file is in the record" is measured here). The defect was never that one file; it is that the class was invisible. **Body as filed:** `pending_notes` (`tools/inbox_responder.py:174`) filters on `Path.is_file()`, and a directory junction is always a reparse point, so it fails that test. The note therefore never reaches the gate 6 link checks, the cycle terminates `empty / none_pending`, nothing is answered, and the entry sits in the inbox unremarked on EVERY later tick. **Not a containment hole:** a companion PASSING arm pins that nothing outside the inbox is opened, quoted, spawned for or delivered. It is a silent-forever condition rather than a refusal, which is the RSC reassuring-label failure class wearing a different hat - the responder reports "nothing pending" while something is pending. **Pinned by the one remaining strict-xfail,** `tests/test_inbox_responder_runner.py::test_a_note_that_is_a_junction_is_refused`; flip it to passing as the acceptance. **Why it was not fixed in RM-384:** the fix is in `tools/inbox_responder.py`, which `docs/RESPONDER_RUNNER_SPEC.md` section 15 fenced off for that build. That fence expired with the build, so this item may take it with a fresh decision - but `inbox_responder.py` is the SHIPPED validator with its own 50 arms, so any change there is TDD-first with the existing arms re-run, and the `pending_notes` filter must keep rejecting directories that are NOT notes. Consider whether the honest fix is to REFUSE the entry (`refused / note-shape:linked`, answered so it stops re-cycling) rather than to admit it. **A SECOND INSTANCE OF THE SAME CLASS, measured 2026-09-08:** `moon_sync_inbox/from-LL-2026-09-07-2035-correction-our-git-identity-count-is-now-two.md` has its sender and date TRANSPOSED, so `_sender_code` returns `None`, gate 4 drops it, and it too is invisible forever. Any fix here should decide what happens to a `.md` in the inbox that matches NO note shape at all, rather than only to junctions.
- **RM-387 SHIPPED 2026-09-08 (filed the same day, LEDGER 1368, Tier-1) - the delivered reply body now names the file the sender actually sent.** The `# GATE:assemble` call site passes the RAW name, on exactly the footing the stdin envelope has always stood on: assembly is reachable only after gate 6, where the name has passed `NOTE_NAME_RE` and is therefore ASCII, `[A-Za-z0-9._-]` and within `NOTE_NAME_MAX`. **`ROW_NOTE_RE` was NOT widened** - the row wants a bounded, regex-pinned field and the body wants the sender's name, and the answer was that they should never have shared one projection; the row, `safe_name` and both name constants are byte-unchanged, and the only row-side consumers (`metrics_row_ok` plus four arms) were measured before anything moved. Two arms: the delivered file must carry `answering <raw>; cycle ` and NOT `answering <clipped>; cycle ` (the clipped name is a prefix, so a plain substring check passes on the broken body), and a second pins the one behaviour the fix buys - a name carrying `hop` past character 80 now refuses a LATENCY-ONLY cycle the clip used to hide, fail-closed, one grammar wide, delivering normally under A5. **Body as filed:** `result.note` is the `safe_name` projection clipped to 80 chars to satisfy `ROW_NOTE_RE`, and `assemble_body(note_filename=result.note)` puts that clipped name into the reply body the counterparty reads. PRE-EXISTING, not introduced by the cap raise - the old grammar already admitted names up to 109 chars - but raising the topic cap to 160 makes it ROUTINE rather than rare, since the measured topic max across the channel is 108 and the p99 is 102. **Bounded, and the bound matters:** the stdin envelope (`note_filename=name`) and the answered record both carry the RAW name, so nothing re-cycles and the spawned session still sees the true filename; only the human-readable reply header is clipped. **The fix is not obvious and must not be done by reflex:** moving `ROW_NOTE_RE`'s `{1,80}` changes the METRICS ROW grammar, which is a published interface RSC may parse, and that literal carries a mutant needle. Decide whether the row and the body should share one projection at all - the row wants a bounded, regex-pinned field, the body wants the name the sender actually chose. Measure the row-consumer side before widening anything.

- **RM-383 OPEN (filed 2026-09-06, LANE 10 queue cycle 17, found by this cycle's own pre-push suite; Tier-1) - `config/vision_token.txt` is gitignored, so making its absence fatal reddens every worktree and every CI runner while the main tree stays green.** `f8323887e` ("vision token rotation") made `vision_token` raise `RuntimeError: vision_token: no token configured` when neither `RC_VISION_TOKEN` nor `config/vision_token.txt` is present. That file is gitignored at `.gitignore:59`, and **a worktree is a fresh checkout that does not carry gitignored files** - the same class the launcher already guards against at `ops/loop/lane_launcher.py:20` for the `.claude/commands` mirror. **MEASURED, not theorised:** `pytest tests -q -n 8` in `C:/rc-worktrees/rc-lane-queue` gives **131 failed / 20061 passed / 136 skipped / 424 errors**, exit 1, against `10856 passed` exit 0 for DS. The identical failure reproduces in a pristine worktree at `6c6b6a0fd` with no lane changes, so it is inherited by construction; the 233 failures whose summary line does not name `vision_token` are downstream of the same import and were confirmed to fail on that pristine tree too. CI run `34064272894` on `main` shows the `check` job's dual-suite step red for exactly this. **Why it is worth a row rather than a shrug:** it is the `feedback_clean_checkout_probe` failure class - green on the dev box, red everywhere hermetic - it blocks the acceptance signal for every lane that pushes to `main`, and per the queue lane's own contract a red `check` step stops the drain loop. It also costs each lane the same re-measurement to prove the red is not its own. **ACCEPTANCE:** the test suite no longer depends on a gitignored runtime file - either the token accessor fails soft for non-runtime callers, or the tests provide their own token via fixture/monkeypatch/`RC_VISION_TOKEN`, with the hard failure preserved for the real runtime path so the rotation hardening is not simply reverted; a regression test proves collection succeeds with **no** token file and **no** env var present; AND the tree is swept for sibling accessors that make a gitignored path mandatory at import time (sweep the ACCESS pattern, not the string `vision_token` - `feedback_pattern_scoped_sweep_is_not_a_root_cause_sweep`). Note the frozen list before editing: `core/log_setup.py` and friends are out of bounds without adjudication. No `ENGINE_VERSION`, no Share mirror, no live gate.
- **RM-343 (ORIGINAL FILING, superseded above) (filed 2026-09-05, lane 8 cycle 48; LANE 7, Tier-1) - every lane WORKTREE materializes 1884 LF-normalized tracked files with CRLF on disk, and git cannot see it.** Measured this cycle in `C:/rc-worktrees/rc-lane-true-audit`: `data/daemon_slayer/16.15.1/scenarios.json` holds **114231 CRLF pairs**, while the SAME path in the main tree `C:/Riot Commander` holds **0** - so this is a `git worktree add` materialization artifact, not a repo defect and not anything a lane authored. `git status` reports the tree CLEAN, because git normalizes CRLF to LF on read when `.gitattributes` marks the path text, so the condition is INVISIBLE to every git-based check and to the pre-commit gate. The visible consequence is that `tests/test_text_line_endings.py::test_no_normalized_tracked_file_has_crlf_on_disk` FAILS in every lane worktree on 1884 files, worst-first `Share/src/data/daemon_slayer/16.15.1/scenarios.json`, `data/daemon_slayer/{16.13.1,16.14.1,16.15.1,16.10.1}/scenarios.json`. **Why this is worth a row rather than a shrug:** it is an inherited red that reads exactly like a regression, so every lane that runs the full suite in a worktree must spend the same measurement to clear itself, and the standing rule is that you cannot tell your own regression from an inherited one if you never looked. It also means any size or digest taken from those files inside a worktree disagrees with the blob by one byte per line - the `reference_windows_write_text_crlf_byte_count` failure class, arriving from the CHECKOUT rather than from a writer. **ACCEPTANCE:** identify why `git worktree add` produces CRLF here while the main tree checkout does not (compare the effective `core.autocrlf` / `core.eol` and the `.gitattributes` in force at the time each tree was materialized - note `.git/config` is SHARED across worktrees, so a per-worktree `core.autocrlf` override is NOT available and any fix must not mutate the main tree's setting); then a fresh `git worktree add` followed by that single test passes with zero files reported. Do NOT "fix" it by re-materializing 1884 tracked files inside a feature branch - cycle 48 deliberately declined that, because a lane-8 commit carrying 1884 touched paths is a lane-7 commit wearing a lane-8 hat, and it would bury the actual slice. No frozen file, no `ENGINE_VERSION`, no live gate.

- **RM-366 OPEN (filed 2026-09-05, raised by the verifier gate on RM-345; LANE 8, Tier-1, FROZEN-FILE) - the pool's idempotency gate is defeated one frame up: the caller re-sends on the give-up path.** RM-345 shipped `IDEMPOTENT_METHODS` + `is_idempotent()` so `HttpsConnectionPool.request` sends a POST/PATCH exactly ONCE and returns `None` rather than replaying it. The verifier refused to accept that as an end-to-end close, and was right: `lcu/lcu_client.py:191` treats that `None` as "pool unavailable" and falls through to the legacy urlopen path at `:200`, re-sending the identical method and body. So a POST that faults inside `conn.getresponse()` - after its bytes are already on the wire - is STILL transmitted twice. **The measured effect of RM-345 is 3 sends down to 2, not down to 1**, and `core/lcu_pool.py` now says so in a scope note rather than advertising a guarantee it does not deliver. This is live, not latent: `pool_enabled()` defaults ON and the affected writes include ready-check accept (`lcu_client.py:222`), rune-page create (`:609`) and champ-select bench swap (`:387`); lane 8 cycle 39 (RM-297) separately found the rune writer deletes pages by a 3-char prefix test, so a duplicated page is not self-healing. **`lcu/lcu_client.py` is a FROZEN file - this row cannot be executed without explicit operator approval, which is why it is filed rather than fixed.** **ACCEPTANCE:** the give-up return is made distinguishable from "pool disabled" (a sentinel, an exception, or a second return value) so the caller can skip the urlopen retry for a non-idempotent method that may already have been applied, while a genuinely unattempted request still falls through; plus a test driving `LcuClient` end-to-end with a faulting pooled POST and asserting exactly one transmission across BOTH paths. The existing RM-345 tests cannot catch this - they are pool-internal and cannot see the caller. No `ENGINE_VERSION`, no Share mirror, no live gate.

- **RM-312 (original text, filed 2026-08-31, lane 8 cycle 46; LANE 8, Tier-1) - the in-process L3 path degrades to the relay SILENTLY, with no log line.** `dashboard/_lcu_inprocess.py:182-191` wraps the whole `shape_snapshot` assembly in `try: ... except Exception: return None`. The docstring is honest about the contract ("Returns None (never raises) ... so the caller cleanly degrades to the relay `lcu_summary()`"), but there is NO log call on that path, so a fault in the shaping is indistinguishable from "the LCU client is not connected" - the ordinary, expected case. This is not hypothetical: lane 8 cycle 46 measured 19 of 20 probed inputs raising in `lcu/champ_select_shape.py` reachable from wrong-typed LCU fields, and every one of them would have presented as a quiet fallback to the `:8889` hop that RC2 lever L3 exists to REMOVE. The relay path still works, so nothing looks broken; RC just pays the hop forever and no one knows. **ACCEPTANCE:** log the exception once per distinct fault at WARNING with the exception type (never the raw payload - it carries PUUIDs), rate-limited or deduped so a persistent fault cannot flood `logs/`; keep the `None` return and the fail-soft contract exactly as they are. A new test asserts (a) a raising `shape_snapshot` still returns `None`, (b) it emits exactly one log record naming the exception type, and (c) the ordinary not-connected path (`client._port` falsy) logs NOTHING - that third assertion is the one that matters, because a log line on the expected path is worse than none. MUTATION-PROVE the guard: delete the log call and watch the test go red. Use a RECORDING spy (`assertLogs`), never a raising one - an `AssertionError` IS an `Exception` and would be swallowed by the very `except` under test, which is the `reference_raise_based_spy_is_vacuous_under_fail_soft` trap. No `ENGINE_VERSION`, no Share mirror, no frozen file, no live gate.

- **RM-313 OPEN (filed 2026-08-31, lane 8 cycle 46; LANE 8, Tier-1) - the shaper forwards `championId` with whatever type LCU sent.** `lcu/champ_select_shape.py` `_team_picks` computes `cid_locked = p.get("championId", 0) or 0` and emits it verbatim, so a JSON string `"67"` reaches every consumer as a string. Cycle 46 fixed the CELL-ID half of that (both the shaper's local-cell resolution and the Arena member scan now coerce), and deliberately did NOT touch `championId`, because cell ids are used only for internal comparison while `championId` is an EMITTED VALUE that the dashboard, the overlay, the coach prompt and the DS profile chooser all read - changing its type is a consumer-contract change, not a guard. **ACCEPTANCE:** decide the contract first and write it down (recommended: coerce to `int` at the producer with a 0 fallback, matching the `or 0` default already there), then apply it to `championId`, `champion_pick_intent`, `champion_locked`, the `summoners` spell ids and the `bench` list in one pass; a test asserts every emitted id is an `int` for a string-typed session, and a SECOND test asserts a well-formed session's values are unchanged, so the coercion cannot be shipped as a silent renumbering. Grep the JS consumers for strict `===` comparisons against these fields before flipping - a consumer that currently compares `"67" === "67"` successfully will start failing if only one side is coerced. No `ENGINE_VERSION`, no Share mirror, no frozen file, no live gate.

Four latent defects sit behind that dead gate, and they must NOT be described as live: `tft/tft_pbe_engine.py:423` swallows the `cost_tracker` import so the spend kill-switch fails OPEN (its twin was fixed in cycle 30 at `tft/tft_coach_engine.py:783`), `coaches/tft_pbe_coach.py:100` delegates reset to an `if not path.exists()`-guarded seeder so a reset clears nothing, `tft/tft_pbe_engine.py:395` writes no blank artifact, and `:510` swallows `_write_status` with no log at all. **Decide wire-or-delete before fixing any of them** - hardening an unreachable subsystem is inert work (`feedback_verified_claim_vs_measured_downstream`). LANE 7. **(c) Three `tft_live_data.json` keys have NO file reader:** `bench_units`, `shop_units` and `unit_swap` are written by `tft/tft_live_analysis.py:355`/`:356`/`:360` and read back only off in-memory dicts, never off the file. Confirm against a live TFT game before deleting - the writer is live-gated. **(d) Four TFT default-payload sites hand-copy the same literal** (`coaches/tft_coach.py` x1 after this cycle collapsed its two into one, `core/feature_policy.py:102`, `tft/tft_coach_engine.py:749`) when `core/coaching_payload.py:163 TftPayload` already exists as the declared shape authority and `TftPayload().model_dump()` is a drop-in seed; relatedly `tft/tft_coach_engine.py:754` writes `stage`/`round` as INTS against a schema declaring `str`. **Not re-filed here:** the `safe_write` CRLF issue is already RM-287.
- **RM-293 OPEN (filed lane 8 cycle 35) - three findings off the `lcu/snapshot_shape.py` audit (LEDGER 1301), deliberately NOT fixed in-slice so the cycle stayed on one theme.** **(a) `_lookup_summoner_by_id` caches only SUCCESS, never a MISS.** `lcu/snapshot_shape.py:152-157` writes the cache only when the payload is a dict, so a summoner id LCU consistently declines to resolve is re-fetched on EVERY capture tick - `tools/lcu_agent.py:125` `INTERVAL = 1.0`, so that is one wasted LCU GET per second per unresolvable member, for as long as the lobby stands. The enrichment call only fires when `gameName`/`tagLine` are empty, which is exactly the population most likely to keep failing. **Acceptance:** a miss is remembered under its own SHORT ttl (a transient blip must not suppress lookups for the full 600s success TTL); a test driving two consecutive captures against a 404 asserts exactly ONE request reaches the transport, mutation-proved by removing the negative-cache write. LANE 8. **(b) `shape_snapshot` decodes the gameflow phase with a bare `bytes.decode()`** (`lcu/snapshot_shape.py:435`), which is strict UTF-8 and raises `UnicodeDecodeError` on invalid bytes - the ONE remaining raise-out-of-module path after this cycle closed the other three, and it is the same blast radius (the whole snapshot for the tick).
