Its failure mode was a silent row-DROP cascading to a 15-month-old seed served as live.) Sweep A:  (LEDGER 1331: union of 33 candidates probed, 29 RAISED and fixed at the read site, 4 DEGRADED and left, 0 unprobeable; four of the raisers ran at MODULE IMPORT). SWEEP B (numeric coercion, provenance-tiered) REMAINS OPEN - do not merge the two, they have different acceptances. (filed lane 8 cycle 33) - two sibling sweeps off the `core/augment_external_source.py` audit, filed as CANDIDATE POPULATIONS rather than defect counts.** Both came out of the root-cause sibling grep for LEDGER 1299 and neither has been probed, which is stated up front because this same cycle filed a count ("24 snapshot files") that its own verifier refuted as an 18-vs-24 double-count. Treat both numbers as hypotheses (`feedback_filed_counts_are_hypotheses`). **Sweep A, the `UnicodeDecodeError` class - RE-MEASURED 2026-09-04 with `ast` instead of the regex this row itself called the weak link: 167 `read_text`-in-`try` sites scanned across `core/` + `coaches/` + `dashboard/`, 136 genuinely protected, **31 real candidates** (the filed figure was 33). The premise is confirmed empirically, not reasoned: `UnicodeDecodeError` and `json.JSONDecodeError` are SIBLING subclasses of `ValueError`, so `except (json.JSONDecodeError, OSError)` lets a `UnicodeDecodeError` straight through - demonstrated live in the interpreter. The 31 all catch only some combination of `OSError`, `JSONDecodeError`, `FileNotFoundError` and `AttributeError`. Originally filed as 33 candidate sites** across `core/`, `coaches/` and `dashboard/` where a `read_text(...)` appears within ~420 chars of an `except` clause naming neither `UnicodeDecodeError`, `ValueError` nor `Exception`. `UnicodeDecodeError` is a `ValueError`, NOT an `OSError`, so a non-UTF-8 file escapes such a handler. This class has now been fixed at the root FOUR times (`core/polled_json.py` in cycle 24, which also names `core/cost_tracker.py:490` and `coaches/_base_coach.py:502` / `:539` as callers that widened their own handlers instead; and `core/augment_external_source.py:_current_patch` this cycle), so the class is real even though this particular population is unproven. **The measuring regex is the weak link**: it pairs a `read_text` with the NEAREST following `except`, which need not be its handler, so expect false positives. **Acceptance:** for each candidate, write a non-UTF-8 fixture, call the enclosing function, and record RAISED or DEGRADED - the probe is the deliverable, not the count. Fix only the sites that actually raise, at the root, never by widening a caller. **Sweep B, bare numeric coercion of a payload field - ~20 candidate sites** matching `(int|float)\(<name>\.get\(...\) or <number>\)`. `int("1500.0")` and `float("n/a")` both raise `ValueError`, which is how W1 of LEDGER 1299 escaped a handler that caught only the module's own error type. **Severity is NOT uniform and must not be treated as such**: the hits in `core/build_planner/champ_kit_data.py` and `core/archetype_picks.py` read DDragon, which is Riot-authored and stable, whereas the class only bites on a feed whose shape RC does not control. **Acceptance:** classify each site by the PROVENANCE of the dict it reads (RC-internal / Riot-authored / third-party) and harden only the third-party tier; a tolerant `_as_int` / `_as_float` pair now exists in `core/augment_external_source.py` to copy. **Explicitly out of scope for both:** `core/synergy_external_source.py`, verified clean on all three defect classes this cycle and the module the fix pattern was taken FROM.
- **RM-290 OPEN (filed lane 8 cycle 32) - the ITEM_UNDO defect class has TWO more sites, and the one that matters conflates the two undo directions instead of ignoring them.** Found by the sibling sweep for the root cause fixed this cycle in `core/replay_history.py` (LEDGER 1298): Riot does not send `itemId` on an ITEM_UNDO timeline event, it sends `beforeId` (the item held BEFORE the undo) and `afterId` (the item held AFTER it). **Site 1, the real one: `core/event_patterns.py:233` `item_order`.** It does read the right fields, which is why it looked correct and why this needs stating precisely - but it collapses them with `before = e.get("beforeId") or e.get("afterId") or 0` and then pops the most recent matching PURCHASE. That is right for an undone purchase (beforeId set) and **backwards for an undone sale** (beforeId 0, afterId set): the player sold an item and took the sale back, so they KEPT it and the purchase must stand, yet the fallback deletes it from the sequence. Measured over the live `data/rewind_history.db`: **2488 of 28405 ITEM_UNDO events (8.8 percent) carry afterId**, so this is exercised, not theoretical. **Reachable by opening the call path, not by a scan** (`feedback_scan_is_not_a_reachability_probe`): `core/pgr_event_report.py:39` imports the module, and `tools/mine_event_patterns.py:68`, `tools/build_cohort_baseline.py:27` and `tools/build_rank_baselines.py:50` all feed it into `data/event_pattern_rates.json` and the cohort baselines the coach then reads - so a wrong purchase sequence propagates into MINED STATISTICS, where it is invisible per-match. Severity MED for that reason, offline-batch rather than live-request. **Acceptance:** branch on the two ids rather than collapsing them (drop the purchase only when `beforeId` is set; leave the sequence untouched when only `afterId` is); a test per direction, and mutation-test by restoring the `or` fallback and asserting the undone-sale test goes RED. Do NOT copy `core/replay_history._undo_pair` verbatim - that one folds an INVENTORY and legitimately re-appends `afterId`, whereas this is a purchase-ORDER view where the purchase already exists in the list. **Site 2, LOW: `lcu/lcu_postgame_collector.py:611`** does `item_id = _int(ev.get("itemId") or 0)` and writes an ITEM_UNDO row with `item_id` 0 into `{mode}_item_events`. It is filed at LOW and explicitly NOT bundled with site 1 because a grep for readers of that table found the writer only - the undo rows are write-only today, so the fix is worth doing when something reads them and not before; re-probe for a reader rather than inheriting that claim. **A documentation trap that costs the next reader an hour, recorded because it cannot be fixed in source:** the live database's stored schema carries a comment listing `ITEM_PURCHASED / ITEM_SOLD / ITEM_UNDO / ITEM_DESTROYED` directly above the `item_id` column, which is exactly the false claim that made the broken fold look right. **No current source file reproduces it** - `scripts/rewind_scraper.py:317` declares the column bare - so it lives only in `sqlite_master` and correcting it needs a table rebuild of a 1.87 GB file. Not worth a migration on its own; fold it into the next schema change, and until then trust `raw_json`, which is populated for 28405 of 28405 undo rows.

- **RM-289 OPEN (filed lane 8 cycle 31) - `core/moon_proxy.py` holds its instance lock across a 5s network probe. FROZEN FILE: audited and filed, deliberately NOT edited.** Same root cause as the two defects fixed this cycle, found by the same AST transitive call-graph sweep (`with <lock>:` blocks whose body reaches a network call at any depth), which returned exactly THREE sites repo-wide - the two fixed, and this one. `core/moon_proxy.py:48` `is_available()` takes `self._lock`, then calls `self._ping()` at `:52` while still holding it; `_ping` (`:60-71`) issues `urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=CONNECT_TTL)` with `CONNECT_TTL = 5.0` (`:30`), refreshed once per `CHECK_TTL = 30.0` (`:29`). **Live-reachable, verified by opening the call path rather than by a scan** (`feedback_scan_is_not_a_reachability_probe`): `modes/shared_vision.py:254` `USE_RELAY` defaults `True`, `:374-378` imports the `moon_proxy` singleton and calls `extract_vision`, and `extract_vision` gates on `is_available()` on its first line. So while `:8889` is wedged, every vision-extracting coach thread serializes behind a 5s probe every 30s. **Severity LOW and stated as such**, which is why it is filed rather than escalated: the target is `127.0.0.1:8889` (`:21-23` - post-ADR-011 "Moon-PC" is the LOCAL in-process vision server, not the retired Game-PC, so this is not dead 2-PC code), the stall is bounded at 5s rather than the 18s and 120s of the two fixed cases, and the lock is per-instance, not process-wide. Today `:8889` is healthy (`/api/health/all` vision `alive: true`, uptime 89085s), so the cost is unobservable - this is a latent failure-mode row, not an active outage. **Do NOT edit on lane-8 authority.** `core/moon_proxy.py` is on the `CLAUDE.md:40-45` frozen list, so per the frozen-file rule the change needs an ADJUDICATING AGENT THAT DID NOT AUTHOR IT, with the criteria stated up front, plus tests and CI green. **Acceptance:** compute the probe result outside `self._lock` and publish it under the lock (the shape already proven twice this cycle in `core/smoothed_rates_101qq.py` and `core/rank_tier_bench.py`), or serve the last known availability while refreshing; a regression test must probe `self._lock` from a SECOND thread during a stubbed slow `_ping` and assert it is not held - a same-thread probe is vacuous on a reentrant lock, and `self._lock` here is a plain `threading.Lock`, so a same-thread acquire would instead deadlock the test. Mutation-test by restoring the probe inside the lock.

- **RM-253 DONE 2026-08-30 (filed lane 8 cycle 22, CLOSED lane 8 cycle 25; LEDGER 1291) - cycle 20 fixed the raw-exception echo in `vision_server/_relay.py` and left its IDENTICAL SIBLING 130 lines below untouched.** `handle_upload_lcu` was corrected on 2026-08-30 with an explicit block comment at `vision_server/_relay.py:50-56` ("this echoed the raw exception text - offending byte offset and surrounding document context - back to the caller"). `handle_upload_liveclient` still does exactly that at `vision_server/_relay.py:177`: `return {"error": f"bad_json: {e}"}`. Verified by direct read on 2026-08-30, not inherited from a scan. `json.JSONDecodeError.__str__` carries the byte offset and the surrounding document slice, and because this RETURNS rather than raises, it bypasses the redaction `vision_server/_http._err500` applies to raised errors. `vision_server/_frame.py:228-230` already uses the fixed form, so this one line is the outlier of three. This is the `feedback_resolver_fix_is_not_a_consumer_fix` shape at its smallest: the fix and the miss are in ONE file, one commit apart. **Acceptance:** `handle_upload_liveclient` returns a fixed token, logs the cause, and a test asserts the returned dict contains no digits from the parse offset; mutation-test it by restoring the f-string. **CLOSED lane 8 cycle 25, all three acceptance clauses met literally:** the handler returns the fixed `{"error": "bad_json"}`, logs the cause at WARNING, and `test_liveclient_bad_json_leaks_no_parse_position` asserts no digit survives into the returned dict - backed by a positive pin on the exact token as well, because a purely negative assertion also passes for a collapsed or empty message (`feedback_negative_assertion_rules_out_without_pinning_down`). Mutation M1 restored the f-string and killed the guard as specified. The row was found by the mandatory pre-flight recall, not by re-reading the file - which is the whole argument for that step.

- **RM-254 DONE 2026-08-30 (filed lane 8 cycle 22, CLOSED lane 8 cycle 24; LEDGER 1290) - `core/polled_json.py`, the module that IS the repo's atomic-write contract, derives its scratch filename from the destination alone, so concurrent writers share one tmp file.** All three helpers do it: `core/polled_json.py:57` (`atomic_write_json`), `:75` (`atomic_write_bytes`), `:87` (`atomic_write_text`), each `tmp = path.with_suffix(path.suffix + ".tmp")`. Two processes writing `coaching_data.json` therefore both open `coaching_data.json.tmp`; the second truncates the first mid-write and whichever `os.replace` lands second publishes a torn document - which defeats the atomicity the module exists to provide. The module's own docstring concedes the adjacent half at `:115-117` ("cross-process ordering still relies on tmp+rename atomicity") without noticing that a SHARED tmp name gives no such guarantee. Second defect on the same paths: when `_replace_with_retry` exhausts its backoff and re-raises at `:44-45`, the `.tmp` is orphaned with no cleanup on any of the three helpers. **Both were fixed locally in `coaches/sr_user_builds._save` by cycle 22** (per-writer `<name>.<pid>.<token>.tmp`, `try/except BaseException: tmp.unlink()`) - lift that shape here. **This is the highest-leverage row of the four:** it is the helper CLAUDE.md points every new polled-JSON site at, and RM-250 already asks three more writers to adopt it. **Acceptance:** unique tmp name per writer, cleanup on every failure path, plus a two-process (not two-thread) test that the destination is never torn. **CLOSED lane 8 cycle 24.** Both defects fixed at the source: `_scratch_path` gives every writer `<name>.<pid>.<token>.tmp` in the destination's own directory, and `_write_then_replace` removes the scratch file on EVERY failure path (`except BaseException`, so a KeyboardInterrupt mid-write cleans up too). All three public helpers now funnel through it, and they encode to bytes rather than `Path.write_text`, which was silently inflating every indented payload with CRLF. **The acceptance asked for a two-PROCESS test and it was written** (`test_a_separate_process_picks_a_different_scratch_name`, deterministic, RED under a revert to the shared name) **plus a behavioural two-process contention test that is recorded as a SMOKE test, not a guard** - against the same revert it went red on only 1 of 2 attempts, so it is kept for gross regressions and the structural tests carry the weight. Three consumers that mirrored the old shared tmp name were corrected in the same slice, two of which had become silent no-ops: `dashboard/routes_loop_control.py`, `performance_tracker.py` (cycle 23's own cleanup line) and `dashboard/_writers.py`. See RM-261 for the ~96 modules that still hand-roll the same destination-derived scratch name.

- **RM-270 DONE 2026-08-30 (filed lane 8 cycle 25, CLOSED lane 8 cycle 26; LEDGER 1292) - CI on `lane/true-audit` was red on NINE real test failures, all of them in tests LANE 8 ITSELF added, and the class is "a Windows-only behaviour asserted as universal on a Linux runner".** Measured from run **33332566593** (2026-08-30T20:03Z): the pytest step COMPLETED in 2332.51 s (38m52s) and reported `9 failed, 30061 passed, 258 skipped, 18029 subtests passed`. **This is a different finding from the run cycle 24 looked at** - run **33327341297** (18:11Z, 47m06s) emits no `FAILED tests/` lines at all, which is consistent with the timeout that cycle's wrap reported. Both runs are red; only the newer one is diagnosable, and it is not a timeout. The nine, by owning cycle:
  - `tests/test_vision_server_inference_hardening.py::OcrResourceTests` **x5** (cycle 20) - `pytesseract`/`PIL` are absent on the runner, so the module returns `{'error': 'pytesseract/PIL missing'}` and five assertions that expect real OCR behaviour fail (`'error' unexpectedly found`, `3 not greater than 3`). Missing dependency, never skipped.
  - `tests/test_performance_tracker_lane8_cycle23.py::TestAtomicWriteUnderConcurrentReader::test_no_orphaned_tmp_when_the_write_cannot_land` (cycle 23) - `AssertionError: PermissionError not raised`. Its own docstring at `:72` says **"Measured on Python 3.14 / win32"**. On POSIX, `os.replace` over a file another handle has open SUCCEEDS, so not raising is the CORRECT Linux behaviour and the test is asserting a Windows-specific failure mode as if it were universal.
  - `tests/test_loop_control_contention.py::test_a_reader_held_past_the_retry_window_is_503_not_500` (cycle 21) - same root cause: it expects a retryable 503 produced by the Windows sharing violation and gets a clean `200` because the replace simply succeeded.
  - `tests/test_handler_trust_boundary_hygiene.py::test_token_gate_survives_a_lone_surrogate_in_the_env` (cycle 13) - `UnicodeEncodeError: 'utf-8' codec can't encode character '\\ud800'`. Windows stores environment values as UTF-16 and tolerates a lone surrogate; the Linux `os.environ` write encodes to UTF-8 and raises in the TEST SETUP, before the guard under test is ever reached.
  - `tests/test_decision_detector_lane8_cycle12.py::TestWritePendingUnderConcurrentReader::test_record_choice_does_not_double_log_when_the_write_fails` (cycle 12) - the name says it: the write does not fail on Linux, so the double-log path is never exercised and the assertion inverts.
  **Verified 2026-08-30: not ONE of those five files contains `skipif`, `sys.platform` or `win32` as a guard** - the only hit for `win32` in any of them is the cycle-23 docstring quoted above, which records the platform of the measurement without gating on it. **The decision this row needs is bigger than the nine fixes, which is why it is filed rather than patched inside a one-file depth slice:** RC is a Windows-only product (Legion, ADR-011) whose push CI runs on a Linux runner and, since RM-119, gates the whole tree. So either (a) these guards are genuinely Windows-only and must `skipUnless(sys.platform == "win32")`, which means **the concurrency guards lane 8 exists to write would never again be executed by CI** - the `feedback_unrun_gate_is_where_the_bug_hides` trap, and the reason this cannot be waved through as a quick skip-decorator pass; or (b) CI needs a Windows job for them; or (c) they are rewritten to assert the invariant (no torn file, no orphaned tmp, no double log) rather than the Windows-specific mechanism (`PermissionError`), which is the only option that keeps them meaningful on both platforms and is the recommended one. **Acceptance:** a stated decision among (a)/(b)/(c) recorded in an ADR or the row itself, applied to all nine, and `gh run list --branch lane/true-audit` green afterwards. **Do NOT simply add skip decorators to make CI green** - that converts nine executed guards into nine unexecuted ones and hides the next real regression. **CLOSED lane 8 cycle 26. Decision recorded as the acceptance requires: (c) for four of the nine, capability-skip for five, and the split is the substance of the answer.** The four replace-semantics failures plus EIGHT further tests that were passing VACUOUSLY on Linux (the class this row did not count, and two thirds of the real damage) were converted to option (c) verbatim - `tests/_replace_faults.replace_fails` injects the fault at the `os.replace` boundary, so the retry loop, the backoff, the scratch cleanup and the re-raise run on every platform and every original assertion is unchanged. Coverage went UP: the exhaustion branch of `core.polled_json._replace_with_retry` executed on Linux for the first time. The genuinely OS-shaped claim became three platform-gated tests asserting only that a held handle still share-locks. The surrogate-token failure is (c) too - the property was always platform-independent, only the delivery through `os.environ` was not. **The five OCR failures are NOT (c) and are not pretended to be:** `pytesseract` is absent on the runner, which is a capability gap rather than a platform semantic, and no invariant rewrite makes an OCR test meaningful without an OCR engine. They skip on a predicate performing the same imports the production guard performs, in the same order, per-test rather than per-class so the pre-import shape guards keep running on a stack-less box. **This is not the trap the row warned about:** the concurrency guards it was protecting are now executed on BOTH platforms rather than skipped on one, which is the opposite of the feared outcome. A sixth OCR test was found green-but-vacuous and gated with a strengthened assertion. Acceptance measured by one merger-side probe: **112 passed, 6 skipped, 4 deselected, 0 failed**, against 9 failed before, with **`real os.replace share-lock PermissionErrors = 0`**. 112 + 6 + 4 = 122, matching the Windows run exactly. On a real Linux runner that projects to **112 passed / 10 skipped**: the 6 capability gaps plus the 4 win32-gated real-OS tests, which skip there by construction and are deselected here rather than faked (forcing `sys.platform` on Windows breaks pytest's own `tmp_path_factory`, which takes the POSIX branch and calls the absent `os.getuid`) - no test depends on Windows `os.replace` locking any more. That figure was CORRECTED before commit: the verifier found a FOURTH win32-gated test that the first measurement had left selected, and the probe's claim to simulate the POSIX environ rule was unimplemented and was removed. Recurrence is ratcheted by `tests/test_atomic_write_fault_injection_is_portable.py` and by `replace_fails` raising when it never fires.

- **RM-282 OPEN (filed 2026-08-30, lane 8 Headless-True-Audit cycle 28; LANE 8, Tier-1, MEDIUM) - `coaching_data_lock_stats()` has ZERO production callers, so the lock's degradation counters reach a human only through a log file nothing scrapes.** Cycle 28 closed RM-199 by making lock degradation observable, and the WARNING lands correctly in `logs/YYYY-MM-DD.log` (verified: `core/log_setup.py` attaches a DEBUG-level `DailyRotatingFileHandler` to the ROOT logger, `core.*` is not level-suppressed, and a sibling `core.vision_token` INFO record is present on disk today, so the plumbing is proven end to end). **But two measurements bound how much that is worth.** (1) **Nothing in the repo reads log LEVELS.** Every consumer of `logs/` either prunes by mtime (`core/log_setup.py`, `core/log_retention.py`) or matches domain-specific regexes (`tools/lcu_push_watcher.py`, whose own docstring says "grep: no info/warning"); a targeted search for level-scraping returns empty, and `/api/health/all` plus `ops/runtime/health.json` are JSON-only. So a WARNING raises no alarm - its value is forensic, for an operator grepping after a symptom. (2) `coaching_data_lock_stats()`, the structured channel added alongside it, is called by NOTHING outside tests (re-grepped twice by independent agents). **CONSEQUENCE:** cycle 28's rate floor bounds how often the log can speak, so between logged episodes the module's health is carried only by a counter no code reads. That is a narrower version of the RM-199 condition, not its elimination, and it is recorded here rather than being treated as closed by the floor. **ACCEPTANCE:** the counters surface on a POLLED channel - the obvious one is a field on `/api/health/all` (and/or `ops/runtime/health.json`), since both are already polled and both are text-readable per R2. A dashboard consumer is NOT required; reaching `/api/health/all` is enough, because that is what an operator and a health check can both see. Add a test asserting the field moves when `file_lock_failures` moves. **Deliberately scoped OUT of cycle 28:** RM-199 asked for the log and nothing more, and wiring a health field would have pushed a depth slice about a lock into dashboard route code. **Do NOT close this by adding a second log line** - the finding is that no automated reader exists, and another WARNING does not create one.

- **RM-283 OPEN (filed 2026-08-30, lane 8 Headless-True-Audit cycle 29; LANE 4-or-8, Tier-1, MEDIUM) - `web/mc/mc.js` maps EVERY HTTP 503 to the literal "auth not configured on the server", so real 503s from the control plane report a false cause to the operator's phone.** Measured 2026-08-30. `web/mc/mc.js:51-53` in `mcPost` does `if (r.status === 503) { throw new Error("auth not configured on the server"); }` and returns before `r.json()`, so the server's own payload is discarded. That mapping is correct for exactly one producer - `mc/auth.py:48-61` answers 503 when the bearer token is unconfigured - but it is not the only one. `dashboard/routes_loop_control.py` returns 503 for a genuine lane-launch failure (`"error": f"launch failed: {exc}", "released": True`), and any other 503 on that path inherits the same false text. **CONSEQUENCE:** the operator fires a lane from the phone over Tailscale, the launch fails for an unrelated reason, and Mission Control tells them their auth is misconfigured - sending them to fix a setting that is fine while the real error, which the server did send, is thrown away. This is the CLAUDE.md "Error Handling" rule inverted: not a raw error leaking to the UI, but a FRIENDLY message that is actively wrong. **ACCEPTANCE:** a 503 carrying a JSON body renders that body's `error`, and only a 503 that is genuinely the auth-unconfigured case (or one with no JSON body) shows the auth text; add a test driving `mcPost` against both 503 shapes. **Note the coupling that made this visible:** cycle 29 deliberately returned HTTP 200 + `ok:false` for its new in-flight-collision case in `_apply_idempotent` rather than the more natural 503, precisely because a 503 would have surfaced as an auth error; fixing this row removes that constraint, but do NOT flip that response to 503 in the same slice without re-testing the panel. **A SECOND, SMALLER DEFECT IN THE SAME FILE, found by the cycle-29 verifier:** `web/mc/mc.js:363` renders `"INTERRUPT REFUSED - " + d.refused`, i.e. the RAW token, where the lane panel at `:222` correctly prefers `d.detail || d.refused`. So cycle 29's in-flight refusal shows the operator `INTERRUPT REFUSED - in_flight` instead of its actionable `detail` ("the original request is still running - wait for it to finish, do not re-send"). Fix both in one pass: prefer `detail` on the interrupt path too. **Runs the UI fixture ritual** - it is a page-behaviour change in `web/mc/`.

- **RM-284 CLOSED 2026-09-02 (LEDGER 1320) - BOTH halves of the acceptance are met, and the durable half landed as `.gitattributes` rather than as a guard change.** (a) `ROADMAP.md` is under 90 pct: the relocation pass (acc7f25ad) took it 73646 -> 71719 bytes with all 179 RM ids retained, every relocated passage verbatim in `docs/ROADMAP_HISTORY.md` and every fence kept in its stub. (b) `.gitattributes` now carries `*.md text eol=lf`, so on-disk equals blob everywhere - **`ROADMAP.md` measures 71522 on disk AND 71522 as a blob, previously 71719 vs 71522.** 156 of 594 tracked `.md` files were CRLF and are now LF; zero committed `.md` CONTENT changed, because `core.autocrlf=true` was already storing LF blobs - this was a working-tree-only defect. Guarded by the new `tests/test_md_line_endings.py`, written RED first (156 offenders) and green after. **TWO COLLATERAL FIXES THE RULE REQUIRED, neither obvious from the row:** `tools/drift_guard.py:145` byte-compared the `tools/` <-> `.claude/commands/` mirror pair, and `.claude/` is gitignored (`.gitignore:116`) so git can never normalise that side - the rule would have reported every mirrored file as drifted on line endings alone, so the compare is now newline-insensitive (measured after: `tools/done.md` 29507 bytes / 0 CRLF against `.claude/commands/done.md` 29835 / 328 CRLF, raw bytes differ, content identical). And `tools/ds_share_sync.py:957` wrote the TRACKED `Share/MANIFEST.md` with `write_text`, which emits CRLF on Windows and would re-break the guard after every sync; it now writes bytes. Suites: `tests` 20343 passed / 96 skipped / 4750 subtests, `agents/daemon_slayer` 10722 passed / 13530 subtests, drift_guard 0 breaches, `ds_share_sync --check` in sync. **Do NOT re-open to "also normalise `.claude/commands`"** - it is gitignored and local by design; the guard compares content for exactly that reason. ORIGINAL ROW BELOW.** - **RM-284 (original filing, 2026-08-30, lane 8 Headless-True-Audit cycle 29; LANE 7, Tier-0, MEDIUM) - `ROADMAP.md` is 16 bytes under its CI size budget, and the budget is measured against a file whose ON-DISK size depends on the checkout's line endings.** Two measured facts. **(1) The headroom is gone, and `tools/drift_guard.py` HAS ALREADY BEEN REPORTING IT.** The file is **81904 bytes against the 81920 ceiling** enforced by `tests/test_doc_size_budget.py::test_roadmap_md_under_budget`, and `drift_guard.py:119` raises a `doc-budget` finding at `BUDGET_WARN_PCT = 90.0`. **That breach is INHERITED, not new:** measured at cycle 29's parent commit the file was 80139 bytes as a blob and 80355 on a CRLF checkout - **98.1 percent**, already past the warn line - and cycle 29 took it to 100.0 percent. So `drift_guard` has been returning `1 breach(es)` on this branch for some time and the relocation it asks for was already owed before this cycle touched the file. The 90 percent line is why this row's acceptance is ~73 KB rather than merely 'under 81920': clearing the guard needs about **8.2 KB** relocated, not the 1.7 KB the five candidates below supply, so expect to take more rows than that list. Cycle 29 entered at 80139 and spent SIX rounds compressing its own entry from 2929 to ~1550 bytes to fit - effort spent shrinking a summary instead of auditing, and lossy: detail that belonged in the pointer now exists only in `docs/LEDGER.md` 1295. **(2) The subtle half, and the one that will bite: the budget is CRLF-sensitive.** `core.autocrlf=true` on Legion and `.gitattributes` forces `eol=lf` for `*.py` ONLY - there is no `*.md` rule - so a Windows checkout writes `ROADMAP.md` with CRLF and its on-disk size grows by one byte per line (217 today). A tool that rewrites the file with LF endings makes it look ~217 bytes SMALLER than it will be for everyone else, and the guard passes locally while failing on the next Windows checkout. **Cycle 29 hit exactly this and it was caught by an independent verifier, not by the suite:** the file measured 81917 LF-only and green, but 82134 as CRLF - over budget. It was fixed by restoring CRLF on every `.md` this cycle rewrote so the working tree matches a real checkout, then trimming against the TRUE number. Linux CI would have stayed green throughout (`reference_windows_write_text_crlf_byte_count`). **ACCEPTANCE:** (a) `ROADMAP.md` under 90 percent of budget (~73 KB), every relocated body verbatim in `docs/ROADMAP_HISTORY.md` and every do-not-re-litigate fence preserved in its stub - the RM-144 pattern (`ROADMAP.md` carries the stub, history the body); (b) the guard measures a line-ending-independent size, OR `.gitattributes` gains a `*.md text eol=lf` rule so on-disk equals blob everywhere. **(b) is the durable half - without it the next author re-runs this whole trap.** Candidates measured this cycle, all fully closed with no open remainder: RM-127 (532 bytes), RM-129 (562), RM-142 (310), RM-164 (663), RM-171 (428) - about 2.5 KB, roughly 1.7 KB net of stubs. **Deliberately NOT done in cycle 29:** relocating five unrelated shipped rows is tree-shaped doc gardening, lane 7's job, not a depth audit of one file (section 2). **Do NOT close this by raising the budget constant** - the budget exists because this file is auto-loaded context.

- **RM-281 HALF-CLOSED 2026-08-30 by lane 8 cycle 29 - failure (1) is FIXED and this row's stated diagnosis of it was REFUTED.** It is NOT an OS divergence. `dashboard/_idempotency.py` keeps its replay table in a process-global `OrderedDict` (900 s TTL) and the suite had no global isolation for it; `tests/test_session_intents.py:43` and `tests/test_loop_control_contention.py:125` share the literal key `a1b2c3d4-0000-4000-8000-000000000001`, so when xdist lands both files on one worker the second file's `halt_save` POST REPLAYS the first file's stored answer, short-circuits before the side effect, and `os.replace` is never called - hence `0 matching attempts`. **The row's own first hypothesis was half right:** no write ever happened and the context-exit assertion did mask the reason, but the POST did not fail on Linux - it succeeded as a replay, returning HTTP 200 with `replayed: true`. **It reproduces on WINDOWS in 1.8 s** with `pytest tests/test_session_intents.py tests/test_loop_control_contention.py -q` in one process (1 failed / 54 passed), which is what refutes the OS framing - the platform was never the variable, test ORDER was. Fixed structurally by an autouse fixture in `tests/conftest.py`, not by a `skipif` (the row's explicit prohibition is honoured - the guard still runs on Linux). A cross-file sweep found 3 colliding keys across 4 test files, all now covered. **Failure (2), the `test_home_week_pool_rows` Playwright timeout alongside unfetched git-LFS pointers, is UNTOUCHED and this row stays OPEN for it.** Original body follows.

- **RM-281 (original filing, 2026-08-30, lane 8 Headless-True-Audit cycle 28; LANE 8, Tier-1, HIGH - this is the branch's CI gate) - `lane/true-audit` CI is RED on two tests, and the first one is this lane's own cycle-26 harness correctly refusing to pass vacuously.** Measured from run **33340966410** (completed `failure` in 46m37s, started 2026-08-30 23:07:24Z at branch HEAD `0589d171`, so it predates the cycle-28 slice entirely - cycle 28 added no failure). **(1) `tests/test_loop_control_contention.py::test_halt_save_raises_STOP_while_a_reader_holds_it`** fails with `replace_fails(...) never fired: os.replace was not called with that destination (0 matching attempts). That message is cycle 26's own `tests/_replace_faults.replace_fails` guard doing exactly its job - it raises when the injected fault never fires, which is the whole reason it was written - so this is a REAL signal, not harness noise. **It passes on Windows: `pytest tests/test_loop_control_contention.py -q` gives 13 passed on Legion, so this is an OS divergence, the same class cycle 26 closed, with a residual instance cycle 26 did not reach.** **HYPOTHESIS TO TEST FIRST, and do not skip to a fix:** `0 matching attempts` means the STOP write never reached `os.replace` at that destination AT ALL on Linux - not that it reached it and the fault missed. The most likely cause is therefore that the `queue_intent halt_save` POST fails earlier on Linux and no write ever happens, with the harness assertion firing at context exit and MASKING the real failure (the `status == 200` assert is at `:128`, after the `with` block closes at `:127`). Same run also logged `FileNotFoundError` for `web/data/ddragon/16.16.1/img/item/3153.png` and `3091.png` plus `BrokenPipeError`, and several `ADR-014 disk pin NOT enforced` warnings naming **unfetched git-LFS pointers** (`run git lfs pull`). Establish whether the Playwright timeout is caused by the missing asset / LFS state before treating it as a UI regression - these may be one root cause, and a missing PNG that the page waits on would produce exactly this timeout. **ACCEPTANCE:** run 33340966410's two failures are green on a Linux runner with the cause NAMED for each, not skipped and not deselected; and state explicitly whether the LFS pointers are a runner-config gap or a repo gap. **PROCESS NOTE, the reason this row exists at all:** cycle 26 recorded that five consecutive lane-8 cycles each declared done over an inherited red they had each contributed to. Cycle 28 is NOT doing that - it is reporting the branch as red, naming both failures, proving neither is its own, and filing them rather than absorbing them into a depth slice about a lock.

- **RM-277 OPEN (filed 2026-08-30, lane 8 Headless-True-Audit cycle 28; LANE 8 + FROZEN-ADJUDICATED, Tier-1, HIGH) - the shared `coaching_data_lock` covers 4 writers of root `coaching_data.json`; the `app/` package writes it with NO lock at all, and every file involved is FROZEN.** Measured 2026-08-30 by the cycle-28 audit and independently re-probed by the merger: `grep -rn coaching_data_lock app/` returns **ZERO hits**, while `app/__init__.py:270-273` `_write_data()` writes `DATA_FILE` (root `coaching_data.json`) through `_atomic_write_json`, and `app/__init__.py:241-253` `_init_data_file()` performs a full read-modify-write (reads `pregame` at `:245`, writes at `:253`). Callers, all verified: `app/_game_lifecycle.py:131` (game start), `app/_game_lifecycle.py:244` (game end), `app/_overlay_manager.py:45` (`switch_mode`, hotkey / mode transitions). **The window is seconds, not microseconds:** `_write_data` writes `self.data`, a whole-file copy refreshed by the 500 ms poller at `app/__init__.py:256-267`, so the read half of its R-M-W can be arbitrarily stale. **FAILURE SCENARIO:** the SR coach enters at `coach_integration/_coach.py:678`, reads `coaching_data.json` into `current`; the game-lifecycle thread runs `app._write_data()` at `_game_lifecycle.py:244` on game end, blanking `action` / `immediate` / `next` / `log`; the coach then replaces the file at `:715` and resurrects the cleared text, so the dashboard shows live coaching after the game ended. That is precisely the lost update the lock module's docstring claims is eliminated. **This is the single highest-severity finding of the cycle-28 audit and it was deliberately NOT fixed there:** `app/__init__.py`, `app/_game_lifecycle.py` and `app/_overlay_manager.py` are ALL on the CLAUDE.md frozen list, so an edit needs an adjudicating agent that did not author the change, with criteria stated up front. **ACCEPTANCE:** the three `app._write_data()` call sites and `_init_data_file()` acquire `core.coaching_data_lock.coaching_data_lock()` around their read-modify-write, with a red-first test that interleaves an `app._write_data()` against a `_coach._write_fields()` on one file and asserts neither update is lost. **State the adjudication criteria BEFORE seeing the diff.** Note `app/__init__.py` carries the separate hard rule that `SCRIPT_DIR` MUST stay `Path(__file__).parent.parent`. **DO NOT re-file the reentrancy question** - LEDGER 404 settled that this lock is deliberately non-reentrant, so an `app` acquirer must be a top-level acquirer and must not sit inside another holder.

- **RM-278 OPEN (filed 2026-08-30, lane 8 Headless-True-Audit cycle 28; LANE 8, Tier-1, HIGH) - two lock-holding writers can atomically CLOBBER root `coaching_data.json` with `{}`, and holding the lock makes it worse rather than better.** `dashboard/_context.py:50-63` `read_json` is documented to "guarantee dict return" and returns `{}` on **any** exception as well as on non-dict content, logging only at DEBUG. Two callers then write that value straight back: `dashboard/routes_state.py:637-639` (`/api/command "refresh"`) does `d = read_json(...)` then `atomic_write_json(..., d)`, and `dashboard/_writers.py` `set_pregame` does the same with one field set. **TRIGGER IS MEASURED, NOT HYPOTHETICAL:** the read can hit the Windows WinError 5 / ENOENT window against a concurrent replace - `dashboard/_writers.py` records that window as MEASURED on this machine and lane 8 cycle 19 fixed the write half of it. A transient read failure therefore replaces the whole file with `{}` or with `{"pregame": ...}`, destroying `pregame`, `log`, `mode` and every coaching field. Because the write is atomic AND holds `coaching_data_lock`, no other writer can interleave to repair it - the lock guarantees the clobber lands cleanly. **THE ASYMMETRY IS THE PROOF:** `coach_integration/_coach.py:685-687` already has exactly the right guard for this (`if not isinstance(current, dict)` -> warn + `self._default_data()`), so the correct pattern exists in the tree and these two sites simply lack it. **ACCEPTANCE:** neither site writes a file it failed to read. A red-first test that forces `read_json` to return `{}` while the on-disk file is non-empty, then asserts the on-disk file is UNCHANGED and a WARNING was logged. **Do not fix it by making the sites check `if d:`** - a legitimately empty file on first run must still be writable, so the fix has to distinguish "absent or empty" from "unreadable", which is a contract question about `read_json` itself and is why cycle 28 filed this rather than patching two call sites. Consider a new checked reader returning a `(dict, ok)` pair and leaving `read_json` alone for its many other callers.

- **RM-279 OPEN (filed 2026-08-30, lane 8 Headless-True-Audit cycle 28; LANE 7, Tier-0/1, LOW) - `core/aftergame_summary.py` is unreachable code that carries a repo-wide ASCII hard-rule violation and a shared-scratch-name writer.** Three measured facts, all 2026-08-30. (1) **ZERO production importers** - `grep -rn "aftergame_summary" --include=*.py .` outside tests returns only its own docstring, the stale reference in `core/coaching_data_lock.py` that cycle 28 removed, and three prose comments in `core/live_benchmark_band.py`; there is no dynamic import and no `.bat` / `.ps1` / `.json` / scheduled-task reference. It is reachable ONLY through its own `if __name__ == "__main__"` block with an explicit `--write` flag. (2) **It violates the 7-bit-ASCII hard rule**: `core/aftergame_summary.py:407` contains 6 non-ASCII bytes, U+2713 and U+26A0, inside an f-string that builds `out["action"]`. `tools/precommit_gate.py` was widened on 2026-07-28 to flag every non-ASCII character, but it only inspects STAGED lines, so a file nobody edits keeps its violation indefinitely - that gate-scope gap is the reusable half of this row. (3) It hand-rolls a destination-derived scratch name (`tmp = target.with_suffix(".json.tmp")`) instead of using `core/polled_json.py`, which is the RM-261 class, and it writes `data/coaching_data.json` while the real SR file is root `coaching_data.json` (`app/__init__.py:42`) - so even its target is wrong. **ACCEPTANCE:** lane 7 decides delete-vs-revive against a prove-it-dead bar (this row supplies the evidence but the decision is lane 7's, not lane 8's); if it stays, the ASCII violation and the scratch name are fixed and it is pointed at the correct file. **DO NOT treat the ASCII fix alone as closing this row** - shipping a glyph fix on dead code while leaving it dead is the worst of both.

- **RM-280 OPEN (filed 2026-08-30, lane 8 Headless-True-Audit cycle 28; LANE 8, Tier-1, MEDIUM) - `_LOCK_TIMEOUT_S` bounds the file-lock POLL, not the acquire, so an unbounded `open()` still runs inside the process-wide lock.** Measured against portalocker 3.2.0 during the cycle-28 audit and left deliberately unfixed there. `portalocker.utils.Lock.acquire` calls `self._get_fh()` - which performs the actual `open(str(_LOCK_FILE), "a+b")` - **before** entering its `_timeout_generator` loop, so the 2.0 s `timeout` covers only the retry polling. The `open()` itself is unbounded, and `core/coaching_data_lock.py` executes it while holding the module-global `_LOCK`, on exactly the antivirus / OneDrive-hostile path the module adopted portalocker to survive. Cycle 28 removed the per-entry `mkdir` from that critical section (memoized, and re-armed on any failure) and bounded the WAITER with `_TL_TIMEOUT_S`, but the HOLDER's section is still not bounded, which is why `_TL_TIMEOUT_S` had to be set as high as 30 s. **SECOND HALF, do not drop it:** on Windows portalocker's default `LOCKER` is `MsvcrtLocker`, whose `__init__` constructs `Win32Locker` and imports **pywin32**; pywin32 is present on Legion today (verified), but if it ever goes missing the ImportError is wrapped to `LockException` inside `portalocker`, caught by this module's handler, and the cross-process lock then silently never works again - permanently, not transiently. Cycle 28's logging makes that visible for the first time, but it does not make it recoverable. The module docstring's claim that portalocker "wraps the stdlib msvcrt/fcntl primitives" is therefore only partly true on Windows. **ACCEPTANCE:** the holder's critical section is bounded - either open the lockfile handle BEFORE taking `_LOCK`, or hold a single long-lived handle for the process rather than constructing, opening, locking, unlocking and closing a fresh `portalocker.Lock` on every acquire (the current design does all five per entry, at coach poll cadence). Add a test that pins whichever invariant is chosen. **Re-measure `portalocker`'s internals first** - two of the behaviours this row depends on (`DEFAULT_FAIL_WHEN_LOCKED is False`, and `AlreadyLocked` being a `LockException` subclass) are version-dependent and are pinned only by `requirements.txt`, so verify them against the installed version before building.

- **RM-272 OPEN (filed 2026-08-30, lane 8 Headless-True-Audit cycle 26; LANE 8, Tier-1) - a Legion box that loses `pytesseract` degrades OCR SILENTLY and indistinguishably from idle.** `vision_server/_inference.py:351-352` returns `{"error": "pytesseract/PIL missing"}` with no `_record` call and no log line at all. Every other early-return failure path in `handle_ocr` records a failure (`:337` body error, `:341` bad_crops, `:346` too_many_crops), so the stats ring cannot distinguish "the OCR stack is not installed" from "no OCR calls happened". **This row exists because the fix was PROPOSED and correctly REFUSED during cycle 26, and the reasoning is the useful part.** Do not simply add `_record("ocr", 0, ok=False)` there: (1) the premise that it is an outlier is inaccurate - `:343-344` (`{"error": "no crops"}`) also returns unrecorded, so the ImportError path is consistent with its real neighbour; (2) recording a failure there would make the `assertGreater(_errors(...))` assertions in `tests/test_vision_server_inference_hardening.py` pass on a stack-less runner FOR THE WRONG REASON, fenced only by a skip decorator that a later edit could remove - green while proving nothing is strictly worse than red; (3) a missing stack is a static machine-configuration fact, not a per-call event, so recording it per call floods the 80-entry `_log_ring` and 60-entry `_latency_ring` with a permanent condition and displaces genuine transient OCR errors. **Suggested shape:** a `log.warning` emitted once behind a module-level latch, plus an `ocr_stack_ok` boolean in `get_stats()` - a capability field, not an error counter. **Acceptance:** losing pytesseract on Legion produces exactly one WARNING in `logs/` and a false `ocr_stack_ok` on `/stats`, the per-call error counters are unchanged, and the OCR tests still SKIP (not pass) on a stack-less box - assert that last clause explicitly, since it is the one the naive fix breaks.

- **RM-276 OPEN (filed 2026-08-30, lane 8 Headless-True-Audit cycle 27; LANE 7, Tier-1) - `scripts/wakeup_prune.py` cannot see the NEWEST sessions in `WAKEUP_NOTES.md`, so the auto-loaded head of that file grows without bound while the pruner reports success.** Cycle 25 observed the symptom ("3 session(s), nothing to do" against FIVE headers) and guessed the cause was "the coarse block split"; this cycle measured the exact mechanism and it is worse than the symptom suggested. `SEP = "\n---\n\n"` (`scripts/wakeup_prune.py:33`) occurs **3** times in the file while `SESSION_RE` matches **7** headings. `split_sessions` (`:100`) does `text.split(SEP)` and treats `parts[0]` as the pinned header, scanning it for sessions **never** - and `_split_on_interior_headings` (`:71`), which exists precisely to recover glued blocks, is applied only to `parts[1:]`. Measured 2026-08-30: `parts[0]` contains **4** session headings (cycles 27, 26, 25, 24) and parts 1-3 contain one each (cycles 23, 22, 21). Recent cycles separate blocks with a blank line rather than `---`, which is why every new session lands inside `parts[0]`. **The consequence is an inversion, not just an under-count:** the pruner archives from the OLD, `---`-separated tail it can see, and the NEWEST sessions are structurally unprunable, so the "newest 3 verbatim" contract silently under-enforces in the one direction that costs auto-loaded context every turn. `WAKEUP_NOTES.md` is 49907 bytes at 7 sessions. **ACCEPTANCE:** `split_sessions` finds all 7 sessions in the file as it stands today (assert the count against a fixture built from the real file, not a synthetic two-session sample - a synthetic sample with `---` separators passes today and proves nothing), `--keep 3` actually relocates the surplus, and the true header is separated from the sessions glued behind it by splitting `parts[0]` at its first `SESSION_RE` match rather than by assuming a separator. **Do NOT "fix" this by normalizing every block to `---` separators without also fixing the parser** - that leaves the next cycle that forgets the separator silently invisible again, which is exactly how this reached 4 hidden sessions. Verify `scripts/wakeup_prune.py --check` too; it shares `split_sessions` and therefore shares the blindness.

- **RM-273 OPEN (filed 2026-08-30, lane 8 Headless-True-Audit cycle 27; LANE 7, Tier-0) - `requirements.lock` is UTF-16LE, so it is INVISIBLE to every grep/ripgrep dependency audit in this repo, and it has already produced one false finding.** Measured 2026-08-30: the file begins with the BOM `ff fe`, does NOT decode as UTF-8 at all, and carries `certifi==2026.2.25` on line 12 of 91 - yet `grep -c certifi requirements.lock` returns **0**. RM-255 was filed partly on that empty grep and asserted "certifi appears in NEITHER `requirements.txt` NOR `requirements.lock`"; the second half was false, and the pattern was the reason (`feedback_empty_grep_is_a_claim_about_the_pattern`). Almost certainly a PowerShell 5.1 `pip freeze > requirements.lock` redirect, which writes UTF-16LE. **Nothing installs it** - CI installs `requirements.txt` (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:106`, `:281`, re-derived 2026-09-06 after a workflow-level `env:` block shifted them from `:71`/`:240`), and as of 2026-09-06 there are now ZERO in-tree references: the single one was a comment in `benchmarks/conftest.py`, and that tree was deleted with CodSpeed (docs/OPERATIONS.md "Why CodSpeed was dropped"). The conclusion is unchanged and slightly stronger - re-encoding is behaviour-free. **ACCEPTANCE:** re-encode to UTF-8 without a BOM, `grep -c certifi requirements.lock` returns 1, contents byte-equal after decode, and a guard test asserts the file decodes as UTF-8 so it cannot silently revert. **Check for siblings before closing:** any other tracked `.lock`/`.txt`/`.csv` written by a PowerShell redirect has the same blindness.

- **RM-274 OPEN (filed 2026-08-30, lane 8 cycle 27; LANE 8, Tier-1) - `HttpClient._hosts` grows without bound in a process that runs for days.** `lib/http/client.py` `_host_state()` creates one `_HostState` per hostname and NOTHING ever evicts one. Harmless in a short script; `RC-Phase3-Supervisor` is a live long-running task (confirmed Running 2026-08-30), and `lib/scrapers/_base.py:87` forwards any path starting with `http` verbatim, so the hostname set is not statically bounded by the code. Each entry holds two locks plus four fields. **ACCEPTANCE:** a bounded map with an eviction policy that NEVER discards a host whose breaker is open or whose `probe_in_flight` is set - evicting those silently resets protection for the exact host that earned it - plus a test that drives the cap and proves an open breaker survives eviction pressure. Deliberately NOT fixed in cycle 27: the policy is a real decision and the slice was already large.

- **RM-275 OPEN (filed 2026-08-30, lane 8 cycle 27; LANE 8, Tier-1) - `request()` reads the response body with an unbounded `resp.read()`, so a hostile or malfunctioning third party can exhaust memory.** `lib/http/client.py` reads the whole body into `bytes` with no cap, on the module that is the chokepoint for every outbound third-party fetch (DDragon, the icon CDN, the aggregator D / aggregator B scrapers). This is the SAME class as RM-247, which filed the missing size / decompression-bomb guard on the `:8889` vision frame path - one control was added there and its sibling here was never checked. **ACCEPTANCE:** a `MAX_RESPONSE_BYTES` module constant, a streaming read that stops at the cap and raises `HttpError` naming the limit, a generous default (DDragon champion JSON is the largest legitimate payload - MEASURE it, do not guess), and a test that a body one byte over the cap raises while one byte under succeeds. Note the fix must not read the whole body first and then check its length, which is the bug wearing a guard.

- **RM-266 OPEN (filed 2026-08-30, lane 8 Headless-True-Audit cycle 25; LANE 8, Tier-1) - `vision_server/_http.py:143` hand-rolls the lookup that `lcu_get_result` exists to perform, and the real function has ZERO callers.** Verified by grep 2026-08-30: `lcu_get_result` is referenced nowhere in production, is NOT re-exported by `vision_server/__init__.py` (which does re-export `lcu_queue_command`, `lcu_record_result` and `lcu_drain_pending`), and the `/lcu-cmd-result` GET instead inlines `with _lcu_cmd_lock: rec = _lcu_cmd_results.get(rid)`. Two copies of one lookup against shared locked state, free to drift - and cycle 25 has already hardened only ONE of them (the uncalled one) to stop handing out the stored dict. The inline copy is currently safe only incidentally, because `self._j` serialises the row rather than mutating it. **Acceptance:** one lookup, not two - either `_http.py` calls `lcu_get_result`, or the function is deleted as dead code and the deletion is justified in the commit. Do not simply harden the second copy; that preserves the drift.

- **RM-267 OPEN (filed 2026-08-30, lane 8 cycle 25; LANE 7-or-8, Tier-1) - `/stats` is the only UN-AUTHENTICATED GET on the :8889 vision server, where every sibling endpoint calls `self._auth()` first.** Verified by direct read: `vision_server/_http.py:48-52` serves `get_stats()` with no auth call, while `/latest-frame`, `/latest-liveclient`, `/latest-lcu` and `/lcu-cmd-pending` all gate. Severity is bounded and should be stated honestly rather than inflated: the server binds `127.0.0.1` by default (`vision_server/__init__.py:68`, RM-150) and this was CONFIRMED LIVE on 2026-08-30 (`Get-NetTCPConnection -LocalPort 8889` shows `LISTEN 127.0.0.1:8889`), and `get_stats()` exposes no key material - `api_key_ok` is a BOOLEAN from `api_key_present()`, not the key. What does leak to any local process is per-kind call/error/latency counters, the configured vision and coach model ids, uptime, and a 30-entry request log. **PROBE BEFORE FIXING:** `grep -rn "8889/stats"` returns nothing in the repo, so nothing polls it programmatically - but the vision server serves its own HTML status page at `/`, and gating `/stats` may break that page's fetch. Check the served HTML first. **Acceptance:** either `/stats` gates like its siblings and the status page is proven still to work, or a comment at `:48` records the deliberate exemption and why.

- **RM-268 OPEN (filed 2026-08-30, lane 8 cycle 25; LANE 8, Tier-1) - the `/lcu-cmd` dispatch feeds an unvalidated body straight into the command queue, the one shape-guard hole cycle 25 could not close from inside `_relay.py`.** `vision_server/_http.py:213` is `"lcu-cmd": lambda b: {"id": lcu_queue_command(json.loads(b or '{}'))}`. A body of `[1,2]`, `"x"` or `5` decodes fine and is queued as the `cmd` payload; the RC-LCUAgent then drains it and calls `.get` on a non-dict. `lcu_queue_command`'s own signature already annotates `cmd: dict`, so the type is documented and unenforced. Cycle 25 closed exactly this class on both UPLOAD paths (`handle_upload_liveclient`, `handle_upload_lcu`) and could not close it here: the fix has to return a caller-visible error, which changes that lambda's contract from "always 200 with an id" to a 400 path, and it belongs at the dispatch site rather than inside the queue primitive. Note the neighbouring `lcu-cmd-done` lambda at `:214-216` also parses the same body TWICE. **Acceptance:** a non-object `/lcu-cmd` body returns a 400-shaped fixed token and NOTHING is queued; a test asserts the queue length is unchanged; mutation-test by removing the guard.

- **RM-269 OPEN (filed 2026-08-30, lane 8 cycle 25; LANE 8, Tier-1) - a BACKWARD wall-clock step makes a stale liveclient snapshot read as perfectly fresh, in three modules at once.** This is the `core/liveclient_cache.py` cycle-7 defect class ("reported STALE relay data as PERFECTLY FRESH") recurring one layer up, at a different site. Every consumer ages the snapshot by subtracting a STORED wall-clock `ts` from a live `time.time()`: `vision_server/_relay.py:200` (`age = time.time() - float(snap.get("ts") or 0.0)`, gating the 1-PC self-heal on `age > _SELF_READ_STALE_S`), `game_reader/poller.py:103` (`age = _time.time() - wrap.get("ts", 0)` against `RELAY_MAX_AGE_S`) and `game_reader/poller.py:133` (against `LCU_RELAY_MAX_AGE`). After an NTP correction that steps the clock backward, every one of those ages goes NEGATIVE, no threshold trips, and arbitrarily old data is served as current until wall time catches up. **Cycle 25 deliberately fixed only the adjacent half and left this open on purpose:** the self-read THROTTLE was a pure interval with no external contract, so it moved to `time.monotonic()` in-slice; `ts` itself cannot follow, because it is published across a process boundary and compared against each consumer's own `time.time()`. **Acceptance:** a cross-module decision recorded once - either carry a monotonic companion alongside `ts` in the relay envelope and age against that, or clamp negative ages to "stale" at all three sites. A fix at one site only is not acceptance; that is what made this a three-site problem.

- **RM-255 OPEN (filed 2026-08-30, lane 8 cycle 22; LANE 8, Tier-1) - `lib/http/client.py`'s host blocklist FAILS OPEN, silently, on both of its failure paths, and the module has zero tests.** Verified by direct read 2026-08-30: `lib/http/client.py:112-117` logs "blocklist missing ... allowing all" and empties the set on `FileNotFoundError`; `:120-127` does the same on `OSError` / `JSONDecodeError` / `UnicodeDecodeError`. Because `_maybe_reload_blocklist` re-runs this on every `is_blocked()` call, a single mid-write `stat` of a half-written `lib/http/blocklist.json` latches the fail-open for that request. This is the chokepoint for EVERY third-party fetch in the tree (DDragon, the aggregator D / aggregator B scrapers, the icon CDN, the CDragon mirror), so a control that silently allows all is the whole control. Secondary, independently confirmed: `certifi` appears in NEITHER `requirements.txt` NOR `requirements.lock`, so the `_SSL_CONTEXT` built from it at `:45-49` is `None` in the shipped environment and `:236-237` never passes a context - the documented python-embed CA-store fix is INERT. `grep -rn "lib\.http" tests/` returns nothing. **Acceptance:** the blocklist fails CLOSED (or refuses to start) when its file is missing or unparseable; a test per failure path; and either pin `certifi` or delete the dead context plumbing - do not leave a security control whose absence is invisible.

- **RM-256 OPEN (filed 2026-08-30, lane 8 cycle 22; LANE 7 or 8, Tier-1) - `agents/agent2_backend/ws_server.py` binds ALL interfaces with no authentication on either route, and has zero tests.** Verified by direct read 2026-08-30: `agents/agent2_backend/ws_server.py:46` `DEFAULT_HOST = "0.0.0.0"` and `agents/supervisor.py:296` constructs it as `WSServer(host="0.0.0.0", port=WS_PORT)`. `_router` at `:175` dispatches on PATH ONLY - the module contains no token, no auth check, nothing. Contrast `vision_server/_http.py:34-39`, where `_auth()` gates every non-public route, and the RM-150 comment recording the deliberate loopback narrowing of that sibling service for exactly this reason. Anything reaching the interface can open `/ingest` and inject a frame that is stamped and rebroadcast to every dashboard subscriber, or open `/push` and read the live game-state stream. `grep -rln "ws_server\|WSServer" tests/` returns NOTHING. **Before fixing, establish REACHABILITY, do not assume it** (`feedback_scan_is_not_a_reachability_probe`): confirm whether the supervisor that constructs it actually runs on Legion today, and whether the port is firewalled - a 0.0.0.0 bind on a process nobody starts is a different severity from a live one. **Acceptance:** loopback default plus a shared-secret gate matching the vision-server pattern, a first test module for the file, and the reachability finding recorded either way.

- **RM-258 OPEN (filed 2026-08-30, lane 8 Headless-True-Audit cycle 23; LANE 8, Tier-1) - the bare-`os.replace` exposure is a REPO-WIDE class of roughly 45 runtime cross-process writers, and RM-250 names only three of them.** Cycle 23 fixed the instance in `performance_tracker.py:34`; the census taken in the same pass is the real finding. **First, a grep trap that makes any re-count wrong:** `grep -rn "os.replace"` finds 87 sites but MISSES roughly 100 more written as `Path.replace` (`tmp.replace(target)`) - which is the spelling CLAUDE.md's own atomic-write rule literally prescribes. `tft/placement_aggregator.py:115` is invisible to the `os.replace` grep and is reached from `save_tft_rating` itself (`performance_tracker.py:331`). Search BOTH spellings or the count is meaningless. Bare writers whose target is polled by another process, verified by direct read 2026-08-30: `coach_integration/_coach.py:120` and `:715` (the per-mode `data/<mode>_coaching_data.json` that the `:8888` dashboard and every overlay poll - the highest-traffic pair in the tree), `tft/tft_coach_engine.py:667`/`:786`, `tft/tft_pbe_engine.py:497`/`:509`, `tft/tft_live_analysis.py:185`/`:454`, `coaches/arena_coach.py:477`, `tft/placement_aggregator.py:115`, `core/vision_tracker.py:396`, `core/coaching_timestamps.py:73`, `core/safe_coach_output.py:56`, `core/aftergame_summary.py:434`, `core/post_game_score.py:265`, `core/feature_policy.py:437`, `core/auto_accept_pref.py:66`, `core/archetype_picks.py:554`, `dashboard/routes_lobby_aux.py:99`, `coaches/sr_user_builds.py:451` (note `:530` in the SAME file IS hardened - the restore path was missed), plus the vision-calibration group (`core/vision_profiles.py:291`/`:335`/`:341`, `core/vision_region_atlas.py:135`, `core/vision_atlas_precompute.py:337`) and the separate-process agents (`tools/hotkey_listener.py:192`/`:206`, `tools/lcu_agent.py:1374`/`:1425`). **Two that deserve singling out:** `core/hot_reload.py:130`/`:204` writes `restart_trigger.txt`, which `ops/rc_supervisor.py` polls from a DIFFERENT PROCESS; and `ops/rc_supervisor.py:1605` clears that same trigger with a bare replace while that very file defines a retry helper at `:77` and does not use it at this site. **The structural point, which is why this is one row and not 45:** the repo carries THREE separate hardened implementations (`core/polled_json.py:39`, `coaches/_base_coach.py:92-103`, `ops/rc_transactional_deploy.py:79`) and nothing forces any writer to use one - so each new polled writer starts bare by default. **Acceptance:** a guard test that walks the tree for BOTH spellings and fails on a bare replace targeting a known-polled path (an explicit allowlist for one-shot generators, which the ~55 sites under `tools/*extract*`, `scripts/`, `ops/audit/`, `Share/src/tools/` and `lib/` legitimately are), then convert the runtime writers above onto the shared helper. Land RM-254 FIRST - it fixes the shared-tmp and orphaned-tmp defects inside `core/polled_json` itself, and converting 45 callers onto a helper with a known tmp-collision bug would propagate it. Note the retry buys the TRANSIENT window only: a test whose reader holds the handle for the whole attempt exercises the exhaustion path, not the retry, and will read as a failing fix (same warning as RM-250).

- **RM-259 OPEN (filed 2026-08-30, lane 8 cycle 23; LANE 8, Tier-1, FROZEN-FILE - needs an adjudicating agent) - a failed rating write is reported to the user as a SUCCESS, and then silently enriches the next post-game summary from a PRIOR game.** Verified by direct read 2026-08-30. `performance_tracker.save_rating` catches its own write failure at `:361-362`, logs `_log.warning("Rating save failed ...")`, and FALLS THROUGH - so the MatchDB row at `:386` is still written and the function returns normally. `app/_game_lifecycle.py:236` then logs `"Performance rating saved"` unconditionally, which is false on that path. Two consequences beyond the wrong log line: (a) split-brain - the DB row is correct while `data/ratings/last_<mode>.json` keeps the previous game's content; (b) worse, the stale file keeps its stale MTIME, so `performance_tracker._latest_rating_file:120-132` (mtime-max across the five per-mode files) still selects it, and `agents/supervisor.py:799-805` copies `rating / label / stats / notes / mode_category` out of it into THIS game's post-game summary with no staleness signal - possibly from a different MODE. **`app/_game_lifecycle.py` is FROZEN (CLAUDE.md:40-45), so this needs an adjudicating agent that did not author the change, with criteria stated up front.** The signalling fix cannot simply change `save_rating`'s return type: `app/_game_lifecycle.py:201` unpacks `g, n = save_rating(...)` while `:230` discards the result, so a third return value or an out-of-band flag is required. **Acceptance:** the success log line reflects the actual write outcome, and `_latest_rating_file` (or its consumer) refuses to enrich a summary from a rating file whose content predates the match it is summarising. Cycle 23 measured the historical corpus CLEAN on this - every mode's rating file content matches its newest `match_history.db` row - so there is no backfill owed today, only the guard.

- **RM-260 OPEN (filed 2026-08-30, lane 8 cycle 23; LANE 7, Tier-0/1) - the experimental-builder hook that reads the Anthropic API key is DORMANT, and the one thing keeping the key out of `logs/` is a `%s`.** Verified 2026-08-30 by grep across `*.py` and `*.js`: `coaches/experimental_builder.mark_active()` (`:343`) has ZERO callers repo-wide, so `data/experimental_active.json` is never written, `consume_active()` (`:402`) returns `None`, and the block at `performance_tracker.py:400-421` short-circuits before the key file is ever opened. `performance_tracker.py:401` is the module's only importer. This is consistent with `dashboard/routes_loadout.py:282-290`, which records the experimental chooser row being removed in item 213. So the API-key read at `performance_tracker.py:406` is currently an unreachable dead path - which is also why cycle 23 did NOT delete it: **prove it dead before removing it** (`feedback_scan_is_not_a_reachability_probe`), and note the path DOES wake up if a stale marker file survives on disk from an older build. **Second half, independent of the first:** cycle 23 measured that no anthropic exception can carry the key out of `record_result` today (`_call_haiku` catches everything at `:236`, and the SDK's `str`/`repr`/traceback surfaces are all clean under `%s` formatting), but an adversarial probe found ONE shape that does leak - a NON-ASCII key file raises `UnicodeEncodeError`, and `repr()` of that exception embeds the entire header value including the key. `experimental_builder.py:237` uses `%s` today and is therefore safe; switching that line to `%r`, or to `logger.exception` / `exc_info=True`, would put the key into `logs/YYYY-MM-DD.log`. **Acceptance:** decide remove-vs-rewire for the dormant hook (if removing, drop the key read with it), and either way add a guard test pinning `%s` formatting at that log site so the fragility cannot be reintroduced silently. Related observation, not part of this row: `dashboard/builders.py:338-343` tails the last 40 lines of `logs/YYYY-MM-DD.log` into `_build_diagnostics()["log_tail"]`, rendered by `web/js/panels/dev.js:234`, so anything reaching the log reaches that panel.

- **RM-261 OPEN (filed 2026-08-30, lane 8 Headless-True-Audit cycle 24; LANE 7 for the sweep / LANE 8 for the runtime subset, Tier-1) - 96 modules hand-roll a destination-derived scratch name, which is the SAME defect RM-254 just fixed inside the contract module.** Measured 2026-08-30 by regex over every tracked `.py` outside `tests/`, `Share/`, `_archive/`: 96 modules build their scratch file as `path.with_suffix(path.suffix + ".tmp")`, `with_suffix(".json.tmp")` or `str(dest) + ".tmp"`. Fixing `core/polled_json.py` does NOT reach any of them. **The overwhelming majority are one-shot generators and are NOT the exposure** - roughly 55 sites under `tools/*extract*`, `scripts/`, `ops/audit/`, `Share/src/tools/` and `lib/` run once, alone, by hand. **The exposed subset is the one to convert, and it is small.** Verified by direct read: `tools/lcu_agent.py:1372` is a FOURTH writer of `data/force_scan.json` (after `core/hotkeys.py:103`, `dashboard/_writers.py:68` and `coaches/arena_coach.py`, all three of which cycle 24 converted) and it runs as its OWN PROCESS (the `RC-LCUAgent` ONLOGON task), which is exactly the case no in-process lock can serialize - it also uses a bare `tmp.replace(p)` with no WinError 5 retry, and its own comment at `:1367-1368` records that it deliberately does not import `core.polled_json`, so this is a considered change and not a drive-by. Also in the runtime subset: `core/vision_tracker.py:393`, `core/aftergame_summary.py:431`, `core/post_game_score.py:262`, `core/auto_accept_pref.py:64`, `core/coach_trace.py:141`, `core/replay_narrative_shadow.py:105`, `core/rofl_archive.py:810`/`:820`/`:836`, `coach_integration/_coach.py:118` and **`:713`, which hand-rolls a `data_file.with_suffix(".tmp")` write of `coaching_data.json` itself and is therefore a THIRD writer of the most-polled file in RC** (surfaced by the cycle-24 verifier, which correctly caught that the slice understated that destination's writer count), `tft/tft_live_analysis.py:183`, `ops/rc_incident_log.py:52`, `ops/rc_self_monitor.py:72`, `ops/rc_transactional_deploy.py:131`/`:146`, and the whole `ops/loop/` group (`adjudicator.py:38`, `intents.py:111`, `lanes.py:419`, `loop_controller.py:126`, `steer.py:99`), which is multi-process by construction. **Two are FROZEN and need an adjudicating agent that did not author the change:** `ops/rc_supervisor.py:87`/`:1603` and `ops/rc_dev_runtime.py:62`. **This row is the natural partner of RM-258** - that one is about the missing RETRY, this one about the shared SCRATCH NAME, and they are the same set of writers, so convert each site once and satisfy both. **Acceptance:** a guard test that fails on a destination-derived scratch name in any runtime module (explicit allowlist for the one-shot generators, same shape RM-258 proposes), plus the exposed subset above converted onto `core.polled_json`. Do NOT sweep all 96 in one commit - that is a lane-7-shaped change and this row is filed as one deliberately.

- **RM-262 OPEN (filed 2026-08-30, lane 8 cycle 24; LANE 4 or LANE 7, Tier-0 mechanically but behaviour-affecting) - four U+2192 arrows sit inside a LIVE Haiku prompt string in `coaches/arena_coach.py:159-160`, against the repo-wide ASCII hard rule.** Verified pre-existing, not introduced by cycle 24: `git show HEAD:coaches/arena_coach.py` carries 4 and the post-slice file carries 4. The lines are `1. Does it combo with your kit? (e.g. dash -> Sudden Impact; CC -> Glacial Augment)` and `2. Does it scale with your items? (AD items -> Conqueror; AP -> Luden's)`, currently written with U+2192. The same block at `:157` also carries U+2550 box-drawing characters, which are outside the banned set but equally non-ASCII. **Why this was filed rather than fixed in cycle 24:** these bytes are part of the prompt text sent to the model, so rewriting them is a change to coaching OUTPUT, not a formatting cleanup, and it belongs with someone who can eyeball the resulting coach text. The 2026-05-18 retroactive purge explicitly did not sweep every surface, so this is drift, not a regression. **Acceptance:** arrows replaced with `->`, the box-drawing run replaced with ASCII, and one live-or-fixture coach render eyeballed to confirm the augment framing still reads correctly.

- **RM-263 OPEN (filed 2026-08-30, lane 8 cycle 24; LANE 8, Tier-1) - the atomic writers are atomic against a concurrent READER but not against a CRASH: there is no fsync anywhere in `core/polled_json.py`.** `_write_then_replace` does `tmp.write_bytes(data)` then `os.replace`, with no `flush`/`os.fsync` on the scratch file and no directory sync. On NTFS the rename is ordered but the DATA is not guaranteed durable, so a power loss or hard kill between the write and the flush can leave a correctly-named file with zero or partial content - the reader then sees a valid-looking but empty polled file rather than the old content. This is a DIFFERENT failure from the one the module already defends (a concurrent reader mid-write), and the docstring's "readers see either the old content or the new" is true for the reader case and overclaims for the crash case. **Scope honestly:** RC is killed with `taskkill /F` routinely (it is the prescribed restart path in CLAUDE.md), so the crash window is exercised in normal operation, not only on power loss. Not fixed in cycle 24 because an fsync per write is a real cost on the highest-traffic polled files (`coaching_data.json` is rewritten every coach tick) and the tradeoff deserves a measurement rather than a reflex. **Acceptance:** measure the per-write cost of `flush` + `os.fsync` on the live coaching-data path, then either adopt it (with the docstring corrected to claim crash-durability) or document the limit explicitly in the module docstring so the next reader does not over-trust it.
- **RM-264 OPEN (filed 2026-08-30, lane 8 cycle 24; LANE 8, Tier-1) - `PolledJsonFile`, the wrapper `core/polled_json.py` advertises as its migration target, has ZERO production instantiations.** Measured 2026-08-30 by grep for `PolledJsonFile(` across every tracked `.py`: outside `tests/test_polled_json_lane8_cycle24.py` (which cycle 24 wrote) NOTHING constructs it - not one module, and not one pre-existing test. The three module-level helpers (`atomic_write_json`, `atomic_write_text`, `atomic_write_bytes`, `read_json_dict`) carry all real traffic. The module docstring at `:13` presents the class as one of three headline features and `:15-16` names the whole module a "migration target", so this is an unadopted API, not accidental dead code. **It was deliberately NOT deleted in cycle 24** - `feedback_nonexistent_path_may_be_deliberate` and the prove-it-dead bar both apply, and a documented helper awaiting adopters is a different thing from scrap. **Severity qualifier this puts on cycle 24's own work:** the W3 half of that slice (the shallow `dict(default)` in `PolledJsonFile.__init__`, which leaked the caller's nested mutable) was a REAL defect fixed with a real test, but its exposure is LATENT, not live, precisely because nothing constructs the class. The W2 half in `read_json_dict` IS live, via many callers. Recording the split rather than letting the fix read as more load-bearing than it is (`feedback_verified_claim_vs_measured_downstream`). **Second, smaller defect in the same class, also latent for the same reason:** the docstring claimed the lock "serializes read-modify-write inside this process", but the lock is per-INSTANCE, so two instances built for one path do not serialize against each other. Cycle 24 corrected the wording and recorded the reachability inline rather than silently fixing a claim nobody could currently hit. **Acceptance:** decide ADOPT or REMOVE. If adopt, convert at least the repeated read-modify-write sites onto it (`core/cost_tracker.py:280-308` and `:431-440` are the obvious candidates - both do read_json_dict -> mutate -> atomic_write_json by hand) and make the per-path locking real, since a per-instance lock plus a module-level accessor is the shape that actually delivers the docstring's promise. If remove, delete the class and its tests and drop the "migration target" wording from the module docstring so the next reader is not pointed at something that no longer exists.

- **RM-234 OPEN (filed 2026-08-30, lane 8 Headless-True-Audit cycle 17; LANE 8, Tier-1) - `game_reader/snapshot_normalizer.py` pays TWO `/activeplayerrunes` GETs per tick for fields NO production code reads.** Two independent audit passes measured the same per-tick census: `activeplayerrunes: 2`, `playermainrunes: 1 per enemy`, `activeplayerabilities: 1`. The second `/activeplayerrunes` comes from `_read_my_runes()`, called a few lines after `_read_my_runes_structured()`. Cycle 17 corrected the in-file comment that claimed "the snapshot pays a single /activeplayerrunes GET, not two" but did NOT dedupe, because that changes live HTTP behaviour and deserves its own slice. **Two consequences.** (a) On a 404 the failure counter increments TWICE per tick for one endpoint, so `get_liveclient_subresource_failures()` overstates that endpoint 2x - in the one number whose block comment says it "never under-reports". (b) Each GET is `timeout=2` (`game_reader/poller.py:279`) and synchronous on the poll thread. **AND the justification is refuted:** the block comment claimed these fields "feed live coach prompts (coaches/aram_coach.py:350, 352)"; those lines are ARAM fountain-rule PROSE, and a repo-wide grep finds NO production consumer of `my_runes` / `runes_full` / `stat_shards` at all. **Acceptance:** either wire the rune fields into a real consumer, or delete both GETs and their WARNING machinery; then assert the per-tick GET census in a test. Decide (delete vs wire) FIRST - do not dedupe a call nobody needs.

- **RM-235 OPEN (filed 2026-08-30, lane 8 cycle 17; LANE 8, Tier-1) - the Live Client subresource failure counters are process-global with NO production reader and NO production reset.** `reset_liveclient_subresource_failures()` advertised itself as a "test / new-game hook" (docstring corrected by cycle 17); measured, its ONLY callers are in `tests/test_silent_except_liveclient_subresource.py`. So the counters and the 60s warn throttle survive every game boundary in a process that runs for days. **Two real effects:** the WARNING text "N failure(s) so far this session" means the PROCESS, not the game, so it cannot answer "is THIS game degraded"; and a warn that fired shortly before a new game starts silences the first part of that game. `get_liveclient_subresource_failures()` likewise has no production consumer - the observability the silent-except A4 block comment was written to provide is surfaced on no operator surface at all (`/api/health/all`, `/metrics`, nothing). **Acceptance:** call the reset from the game-start path alongside `reset_reader_state` (`core/sr_aram_worker.py`, invoked from `app/_game_lifecycle.py`), expose the counters on the health payload, and pin both with tests. NOTE `app/_game_lifecycle.py` is FROZEN - the reset belongs in the worker, not the frozen lifecycle module.

- **RM-236 OPEN (filed 2026-08-30, lane 8 cycle 17; LANE 8, Tier-1) - six MORE `game_reader/snapshot_normalizer.py` defects found by the cycle-17 audit and deliberately NOT fixed in that slice, four of which are wrong on REAL Riot data in EVERY game.** Cycle 17 fixed the two CRITICALs (turret side attribution, the fog-respawn dead flag) plus the wire-numeric class; these six are separable and each needs its own evidence. **(a) `quest_boots_owned` is wrong in BOTH directions on 16.15.1** - the `ADVANCED_BOOTS` set contains NONE of the eight tier-3 boots actually shipping (Swiftmarch, Armored Advance, Chainlaced Crushers, Crimson Lucidity, Forever Forward, Gluttonous Greaves, Gunmetal Greaves, Spellslingers Shoes, Synchronized Souls), lists a "boots of speed" that matches nothing (the tier-1 item is named `Boots`), and falls back to a `len(combat_items) >= 3` proxy that returns True with no boots at all; "bf sword" also never matches the real "B. F. Sword". `coach_integration/_sr_prompt.py` injects a literal build instruction off this field, so it produces actively harmful advice. HIGH. **(b) The dragon soul note sums BOTH teams' kills** - `dragon_kills.append(et)` ignores `KillerName`, so a 2-1 split reads as `[SOUL - 3 kills]` and a genuine 3-0 soul point stays silent if the enemy also took one.
