- **(from R198) Overlay TYPOGRAPHY instances logged FUTURE rather than bumped blind.** The 5-phase audit enumerated sub-`--fs-xs` type on the Arena-reachable overlay surface: `web/css/panels/build_module.css:125` `.bm-pip` at 9px, and four inline `style.cssText` twins in `web/js/panels/active_match.js` (9px pips at :2342 / :2433 / :2472, 10px caption at :2438). Raising a 9px pip on a 44px item icon is a LAYOUT change, not a token substitution, so it needs a visual pass rather than a find-replace. The related lower-severity class is the JS-side token bypass: `web/js/lib/overlay_tooltip.js:67,78` and `web/js/lib/overlay_item_radial.js:65,74` hardcode `13px` where `--fs-ov-chip` already resolves to 13 - inert today, drifts the moment the overlay scale is re-tuned. **RM-132 SHIPPED 2026-08-01 (LEDGER 1157), acceptance met - but the token reference it creates is INERT, and that finding is worth more than the row.** The 4 literals now read `var(--fs-ov-chip,13px)`; `grep font-size:13px` returns 0 across both lib files and the render is byte-identical. **The 13px fallback is the only thing that ever applies, in EVERY shell.** `--fs-ov-chip` is declared on `body[data-shell="overlay"]` (`web/css/overlay.css:33`, deliberately never `:root`), but both widgets mount to `document.documentElement` (`overlay_tooltip.js:52`, `overlay_item_radial.js:118`), making them SIBLINGS of `<body>` - and custom properties inherit downward only. So the var never resolves, not even in the overlay shell, and the reference cannot track the token until the mount point or the token scope changes. Caught by the adversarial verifier AFTER the implementer had already reasoned about scope and still got it half wrong (the first write-up claimed it failed only on the plain `:8888` dashboard). Filed as **RM-139**. _(original filing kept below)_ - now well-formed.** VERIFIED still real 2026-08-01: the 4 `font-size:13px` cssText/style literals sit at `overlay_tooltip.js:67,78` + `overlay_item_radial.js:65,74`, and `--fs-ov-chip: 13px` is defined at `web/css/overlay.css:33`. Replace the 4 font-size literals with `var(--fs-ov-chip)`. ACCEPTANCE: grep for `font-size:13px` returns 0 across the two lib files, and the overlay renders byte-identical (both sides equal 13 today). No ENGINE bump, no Share. The overlay sub-floor tokens themselves (`--fs-ov-chip` 13 / `--fs-ov-sigil` 12 / `--fs-ov-head` 11 / `--fs-ov-call` 14) are the documented item-184 exception and are NOT a defect - do not re-flag them.

- **(from R202 / LEDGER 1076) Six resident processes still run whatever code they started with.** The defect class is a long-lived python process that imports its source ONCE and never notices a change, so a shipped fix stays inert until somebody restarts it - the loop controller burned three cycles to this on 2026-07-27 and now self-reloads (`ops/loop/loop_controller.py` `CODE_FILES` + `cycle_top_code_guard`). 39 candidates were dispositioned (15 ALREADY-COVERED by `restart_trigger.txt` / `_Phase3Watcher` / the DS `/health` engine_version check, 17 OUT-OF-SCOPE incl. FROZEN `ops/rc_supervisor.py` as the root of the restart tree and 4 periodic one-shot watchdogs that are immune by construction). These six are GENUINE-GAP, ordered by measured blast radius: (a) `tools/ds_matchdb_mcp_server.py:722` (`RC-DS-MatchDB-MCP`) - was LIVE-STALE when measured, pid 1940 up since 07-25 12:37 running `core/build_order.py` from before its 07-25 16:02 edit and `core/daemon_slayer_client.py` from before its 07-27 01:17 edit; (b) `vision_server/__init__.py:91` - pid 10788 up since 07-25 running 07-05 code, and it SURVIVES a `main.py` restart because `dashboard/server.py:207-219` self-heals on PORT LIVENESS only (`connect_ex` on 8889), so port-open is not code-current; (c) `tools/lcu_agent.py:1575`, (d) `tools/liveclient_relay.py:118` (low radius - RC self-heals `:2999` in-process), (e) `tools/hotkey_listener.py:399`, (f) `tools/live_flip_watcher.py:243` (its STOP file is a stop, not a reload). `ops/rc_supervisor.py` contains ZERO references to any of the six, and `core/hot_reload.py` is a near-miss: it watches `ops/**` but its only action is writing `restart_trigger.txt`, which restarts `main.py` alone. The reference implementations to copy are `_Phase3Watcher` (`ops/rc_supervisor.py:379-386` + `:574`, declared import chain + mtime vs process start + grace + restart budget) for a supervised task, or the loop controller's own digest-plus-`os.execv` for a process that must restart itself. Each needs its own restart owner and its own live-behaviour check, which is why they were not bundled into the loop-infra commit. Also missing and cheap: `dashboard/routes_loop_monitor.py` carries no pid or process-start field, so nothing surfaces controller staleness to the dashboard.

- **(DEFER - operator-gated ARCHITECTURE question; explicitly NOT queueable to a headless cycle) Layered `CLAUDE.md`: a root under ~200 lines plus per-module `CLAUDE.md` files** _(from RM-121 item 4, the `random.txt` part-1 ingest, 2026-07-28)._ REAL PROPOSAL, and its premise is one RC already agrees with: the root `CLAUDE.md` is auto-loaded EVERY turn and is CI size-budgeted under 60KB precisely because per-turn context is a managed resource - that budget is WHY the per-item ledger was relocated to `docs/LEDGER.md`. But the file is far over 200 lines today and splitting it changes what every session bootstraps from, so this is an architecture call with blast radius on every future session, not a cleanup task. Sub-questions to settle BEFORE anyone builds it: which rules are genuinely module-local versus repo-global (the no-em-dash rule and the frozen-file list are global by construction); whether a per-module file would be auto-loaded or read-on-demand (auto-load only MOVES the budget, it does not cut it); and how a split interacts with the "Settled - do not re-litigate" block, whose entire value is that it is unavoidable. Needs an operator decision first, then its own session.
- **(DEFER - a one-rule `CLAUDE.md` addition, operator territory) Forbid authoring source files via raw shell redirection (`echo` / `cat <<EOF`)** _(from RM-121 item 4, the `random.txt` part-1 ingest, 2026-07-28)._ PARTIALLY HAVE - the two existing rules were written for exactly this failure mode: `CLAUDE.md` already mandates `git commit -F <tmpfile>` for commit messages (a double-quoted here-string or a piped string carries BOM + ANSI-mangle risk) and atomic `tmp.write_text(...); tmp.replace(target)` for data writes. What does NOT exist is a blanket rule against authoring SOURCE files through shell redirection, which is the same encoding hazard one step earlier - the 2026-05-18 boot-script incident (PowerShell 5.1 `ParseFile` ANSI-decoding a no-BOM `.ps1` into a cascading parse failure) is the standing precedent. Filed rather than done because `CLAUDE.md` is CI size-budgeted under 60KB and rule changes are the operator's call; fold it into the SAME rule edit as the `ast-grep` / LSP item under "Platform / observability" so the budget is spent once.
- **Mission Control log rotation** _(from the S10 whole-branch final review, 2026-07-31; non-blocking)._ `mission_control.py:27` (`_setup_logging`) computes its date stamp ONCE at process start via `_dt.datetime.now().strftime("%Y-%m-%d")` and hands that single filename to `logging.FileHandler` for the whole process lifetime, so a process alive across a day boundary keeps appending to the day-1 file instead of rolling to a new one. The dashboard page polls Mission Control every 5s, and long uptime is the explicit point of the S10 scheduled task (`RestartCount=3`/`RestartInterval=1min`, ONLOGON self-healing), so a 30-day-alive process writes roughly 1.6 MB/day of access-log lines into one ever-growing file with no cap. Fix = a date-aware or `TimedRotatingFileHandler`-based logger. Not built here - doc-only per the S10 final review.
- **Mission Control secondary-bind thread death goes undetected** _(from the S10 whole-branch final review, 2026-07-31; non-blocking)._ `mc/server.py:86-91` serves every `BIND_ADDRESSES` entry past the first (the tailnet `100.70.22.55` address) on a background `daemon=True` thread running `srv.serve_forever()`; only the primary loopback bind (`servers[0]`, line 93) runs on the main thread. If the tailnet thread dies AFTER a successful start, `main()` never notices - it never joins that thread - so the process stays alive serving loopback alone and still exits 0 whenever it eventually does stop. `RC-MissionControl`'s `MultipleInstances=IgnoreNew` means the scheduled-task watchdog will not restart a still-running process either, so nothing catches it from that side. Practical consequence: remote tailnet reachability (the explicit S10 "reachable by IP" requirement) disappears silently while the process and its loopback bind both look healthy. Fix = a monitored/joined thread or a periodic both-binds self-check. Not built here - doc-only per the S10 final review.
- **Mission Control S10 residual minors - six, all adjudicated non-blocking, recorded so they are not rediscovered** _(2026-07-31; the S10 execution ledger they came from has been deleted, so this line is the surviving record)._ Ordered by how likely each is to actually deserve fixing. **(a) HIGHEST VALUE: raw exception strings can reach `:8895`.** `dashboard/_errors.send_error` returns `{"error": str(exc)[:200]}`, and the last-resort `except` blocks in `dashboard/routes_loop_status.py:383` and `routes_loop_control.py:483` call it - both modules are imported by `mc/routes.py`, so the new control plane inherits the behaviour. It collides with the CLAUDE.md rule "never surface raw API error strings in any user-facing dashboard panel", though the final review graded it weaker than the ledger did: the normal paths return curated envelopes, and what leaks here is filesystem paths on an internal control API rather than Claude API errors in a coach panel. NOT introduced by S10 - `:8888` does exactly this today - and the fix touches a module shared with the dashboard, which is why it was out of every task's scope. **RM-134 SHIPPED 2026-08-01 (LEDGER 1157) - `dashboard/_errors.send_error` scrubs, and BOTH surfaces are covered.** It no longer serialises `str(exc)[:200]`; it renders `GENERIC_ERROR` ("internal error - see logs", the wording `builders_last_match.py` already uses) or a caller-supplied `public_msg`, and logs `type(exc).__name__` + the raw cause to `rc.web_dashboard`. Signature is back-compatible, so all 21 call sites across 7 route modules were fixed by the one edit. TDD: the leak was PROVEN first end-to-end through the MC route table (`mc/routes.py:16-22` splices `routes_loop_status` + `routes_loop_control`, re-verified independently), then fixed. `tests/test_dashboard_error_scrub_rm134.py`, 8 tests, mutation-checked against the old body outside the repo: **7 of 8 kill it**; the survivor is the `is`-identity importer check, which passes by construction and is a wiring assertion, not a leak assertion. _(original filing kept below)_ - now well-formed.** VERIFIED full chain intact this run: `dashboard/_errors.py:5` returns `{"error": str(exc)[:200]}` raw; the last-resort `except Exception as exc` guards at `dashboard/routes_loop_status.py:383` + `dashboard/routes_loop_control.py:483` call `send_error(h, exc)`; `mc/routes.py:16` imports both route modules and splices their route tables (`:19-22`), so `:8895` serves the leaky handlers. **ACCEPTANCE:** a test forces an exception through an MC (`:8895`) loop-status / loop-control route and asserts the response `error` field carries no filesystem path, module name, or raw `str(exc)` - i.e. `send_error` scrubs to a generic degraded-mode message before serialization, logging the raw error to `logs/` per the CLAUDE.md Error Handling rule. Touches `dashboard/_errors.py` (shared with `:8888`, which has the same leak today), so verify BOTH surfaces. No ENGINE bump, no Share. **(b)** `mc/auth.py` `resolve_token()` catches `OSError`/`IndexError` but not `UnicodeDecodeError`, so a token file containing invalid UTF-8 propagates instead of degrading to `None`; verified fail-CLOSED (it surfaces as a 500 through `do_POST`'s outer handler), not fail-open. **(c)** `web/mc/mc.js` `_mcCapturePreserve()` snapshots textarea values synchronously at tick time but restores them after the `/api/loop-status` fetch resolves, so characters typed inside that window are overwritten by the snapshot - REPRODUCED with a held-open fetch (typed `abcd`, got `abc`), bounded by localhost fetch latency and at most once per ~30s of continuous editing. **(d)** the two "undefined custom property" scans in `tests/test_mission_control_panel.py` still enumerate definitions only under `web/css/`, not `web/mc/mc.css` itself; verified harmless today (mc.css uses 21 tokens and defines all 21, and every one is also mirrored under `web/css/`), but a future MC-only token would false-fail. **(e)** `dashboard/_dispatch.py:26` `from typing import Callable` is now dead - its only two use sites moved to `dashboard/_matchers.py` - and stays lint-green because `ruff.toml:27` disables `F401` repo-wide. **(f)** `web/js/panels/dev.js:129` carries a comment citing `/api/loop-control` as a live sibling pattern; that endpoint is 404 on `:8888` since S10.

That substitution is arguably STRONGER than eyeballing for STRUCTURE, TYPOGRAPHY, ASCII and contrast-HIERARCHY - contrast was recomputed from the stylesheet's own hex values at 17.23:1 - but it cannot catch a paint-only rendering glitch, and `web/mc/mc.css` is newly-authored CSS rather than proven reuse. - **RM-139 (NEW 2026-08-01, LANE 4 UIUX or 7, Tier-1, MED) - the four overlay sub-floor type tokens are UNREACHABLE from the two widgets that mount outside `<body>`** _(surfaced by the RM-132 verifier pass; measured, not inferred)._ `--fs-ov-chip: 13px` / `--fs-ov-sigil: 12px` / `--fs-ov-head: 11px` / `--fs-ov-call: 14px` are declared on `body[data-shell="overlay"]` at `web/css/overlay.css:22-37`, with the file's own comment stating the scoping is deliberate ("overlay-scoped tokens (never :root)") because the global `tokens.css` >=16px floor is relaxed only inside the overlay. But `overlay_tooltip.js:52` and `overlay_item_radial.js:118` both `document.documentElement.appendChild(...)`, so those two widgets are SIBLINGS of `<body>` and custom properties inherit DOWNWARD only. **Consequence:** any `var(--fs-ov-*)` in those two files resolves to nothing in EVERY shell, overlay included - it silently falls back or, without a fallback, inherits. RM-132 shipped `var(--fs-ov-chip,13px)` there, which renders correctly but can never track the token, so re-tuning the overlay scale still misses those widgets. This is the `feedback_guard_on_nondefault_call_path_is_untested` / inert-seam shape: settable, grep-green, arithmetically dead. **Three candidate fixes, none free - pick with the operator:** (a) mount both widgets under `<body>` instead of `documentElement` (smallest diff, but they were put on the root for stacking/positioning reasons that must be re-checked against the overlay z-order and `position:fixed` containing block); (b) add a `:root:has(body[data-shell="overlay"])` block mirroring ONLY the four type tokens, keeping the palette body-scoped (respects the doctrine, needs `:has()` which Legion's Chrome has); (c) read the token in JS via `getComputedStyle(document.body)` and set the px inline (most code, least elegant). **ACCEPTANCE:** whichever route, prove the var actually RESOLVES - a computed-style read on a live overlay widget, not a grep - and add a regression test that fails if the widgets move back outside the token's scope. Check the other three tokens for the same bypass while in there. Owes the 5-phase UI-audit ritual if the mount point moves.

Either way it must FAIL when an rc-shell test fails - prove it by breaking one deliberately and restoring. **Watch two traps:** the runner needs Node on the CI image (the docs-guards job deliberately installs a minimal dep set, so do not silently add weight to it), and a harness that skips when `node` is missing would reproduce exactly the self-excusing shape RM-212's own guard was written to avoid - assert, do not skip. **DO NOT re-measure** the zero-hits grep or the 329/0 figure (both measured 2026-09-04). Neither live-gated nor operator-gated.
- **RM-212 SHIPPED 2026-09-04 (LEDGER 1329; body below retained as the historical record; filed 2026-08-15, lane-research refill; LANE 7, Tier-1) - the rc-shell Node test runner enumerates its 16 test files BY NAME and nothing guards that list against the directory, so the next test file added runs NOWHERE with every signal green.** `rc-shell/package.json:9` `"test"` is a single `node --test <16 explicit paths>` string. **Measured this run: 16 named, 16 `*.test.js` on disk, set difference EMPTY in both directions** - the trap is real but currently unrealised, which is exactly why it is worth guarding now rather than after it bites. **Also measured: NO test anywhere reads that scripts block.** A grep for `package.json` across `tests/*.py` returns exactly one hit, `tests/test_rename_amberstone_guard.py:46`, which lists the path for a rename check and never opens `scripts`. The trap is already recorded as institutional knowledge (`reference_rc_shell_test_script_enumerates_files`, "nothing globs the directory") and has never been converted into a guard. **ACCEPTANCE:** a guard asserting set equality between `rc-shell/test/*.test.js` on disk and the paths named in `rc-shell/package.json` `scripts.test`. Red-then-green in BOTH directions: delete one name from the script -> RED; add a `.test.js` file that is not named -> RED. It must ASSERT rather than skip when `rc-shell/` is absent - see RM-214 for what a self-excusing guard costs. **DO NOT REDO:** do not re-measure the 16/16 equality (measured 2026-08-15, clean). Do not convert the script to a directory glob as the fix - `rc-shell/package.json:12` pins `"node": ">=18"` and directory-mode `node --test` semantics differ across that range; a guard is the cheap correct fix and changing the runner invocation is a different row. Neither live-gated nor operator-gated.
- **RM-214 SHIPPED 2026-09-04 (LEDGER 1330; body below retained as the historical record; filed 2026-08-15, lane-research refill; LANE 8, Tier-1) - a path-disclosure guard on the vision server DISARMS ITSELF whenever the file it probes for exists, and the server under test creates that file in its own CWD - the exact `skipTest` shape LEDGER 1179 already declared wrong for a sibling test in this same subsystem.** `tests/test_vision_server_bind_rm150.py:206-215` `test_monitor_404_does_not_list_filesystem_paths` does `if status == 200: self.skipTest("moon_monitor.html is present on this box")`, and the assertions it thereby skips are the redaction ones - no "searched", no `Path.home()`, no "Desktop" in the body. `vision_server/_http.py:241-243` writes `moon_monitor.html` into `Path.cwd()` on any PUT of that name ("Write monitor files to both cwd AND sync inbox for discoverability"), on top of the `SYNC_DIR` write at `:244` - so ONE authenticated PUT permanently disarms the guard for that working directory. **The precedent is explicit and recent:** LEDGER 1179 (2026-08-03) fixed `tests/test_vision_server_http_hardening.py::test_absolute_path_injection_is_refused` in this same subsystem and recorded the rule in its own text - "with the requirement ASSERTED rather than `skipTest`ed so a machine that breaks the assumption fails loudly instead of quietly passing - `reference_guard_can_be_green_while_self_excusing`". This test is the unswept sibling. **HONEST SCOPE, so nobody over-reads this: there is NO live leak and NO auth hole.** `do_PUT` gates on `self._auth()` (`vision_server/_http.py:228`) and `fname` is already `Path(...).name`-sanitised (`:240`); the 404 body at `:81` is `{"error": "moon_monitor.html not found"}` with no path in it, and the `searched %s` text at `:79` is a LOG line, not a response body; and `moon_monitor.html` is absent from both this worktree and `C:\Riot Commander`, so the test is currently RUNNING rather than skipping. The defect is that a normal production action turns the guard off. **ACCEPTANCE:** the test controls its own working directory (a tmp dir) so the 404 branch is always exercised, and the presence of `moon_monitor.html` is asserted-absent rather than skipped on. Red-then-green: put a path string into the 404 body at `vision_server/_http.py:81` and the test must go RED regardless of whether `moon_monitor.html` exists anywhere - today, creating that file in the CWD makes it pass unconditionally. **DO NOT REDO:** do not re-file this as an auth or traversal bug (both refuted above by direct read). Do not re-open `test_absolute_path_injection_is_refused`, closed by LEDGER 1179. Do not delete the `Path.cwd()` write as a drive-by - it is a stated discoverability behaviour and removing it is a separate decision, not required to close this row. `vision_server/_http.py` is not on the frozen list. Neither live-gated nor operator-gated.

## Speculative

- **`.rofl` Layer-1 container spike (optional, the only keep-able half).** Full `.rofl` packet-parse is out of scope as a shipping feature (Layer-2 per-packet field obfuscation rotates every patch = recurring binary RE; CLAUDE.md Settled). The patch-stable **Layer-1** spike - parse header/metadata + Blowfish-decrypt/decompress chunks, ~1.5-2 days - is keep-able IF offline self-replay chunk inspection is ever wanted. **ESTIMATE REVISED 2026-07-19: the METADATA half is already done and cost ~10 lines, not days.** Measured against two archived replays: a `.rofl` ends with a plain unencrypted JSON blob (`magic RIOT\x02\x00`; find `{"gameLength"`, then `JSONDecoder().raw_decode` - a plain `json.loads` raises "Extra data" on the trailing bytes), carrying `statsJson` = 10 player dicts at **367 engine-named fields each** (`CHAMPIONS_KILLED`, `GOLD_EARNED`, `ITEM0..ITEM6`, `TOTAL_DAMAGE_DEALT_TO_CHAMPIONS`, `KEYSTONE_ID`, ...; `NAME` measured empty). **No client, no matching patch, no third-party tool** - stat extraction has NO patch gate, unlike playback, which is hard-locked (one patch back already reports `state: "incompatible"`). Only the Blowfish CHUNK half still costs real time. Load-bearing consequence: the KIWI/Mayhem replay absent from `rewind_history.db` (Match-V5 403s on event modes) still yields full end-of-game stats incl. event item ids, so an archived replay is a permanent stats record for exactly the modes Match-V5 refuses. Feeds `core/rofl_archive.py` + `tools/rofl_archiver.py`. Memory: `reference_rofl_layer1_stats_extraction`. Steps 3-6 (Layer-2 decode -> cast/windup events -> validate vs wiki offsets -> wire to DS) stay out of scope. The s220 S5 Replay page stays Match-V5-timeline-driven. Sources: maknee League-Data-Scraping blog, fraxiinus/roflxd, robertabcd/lol-ob ROFL Container Notes.
- **PyInstaller packaging**: `riot-commander.spec` exists as opt-in starter. Not prioritized.

Also note the MAIN FOCUS lever is on-my-way PING COUNT, which is trivially gameable and is a proxy for coordinated play rather than a cause of it - a textbook Goodhart target that stops meaning anything the moment a user is told to raise it. **RC's existing posture is the stronger one and must not regress to match theirs:** RM-32 measured wr_followed 0.5857 vs unfollowed 0.5109 (delta_shrunk +0.0677) and shipped as INSTRUMENTATION, NOT A VERDICT, because the effect could not be separated from noise; any RC build here keeps that framing, shows n, and never renders a causal win-percent promise.

### RM-310 SHIPPED 2026-09-06 (lane 8) - core/rofl_archive.py sidecar writes use a bare os.replace (LANE 8, Tier-1)

**Closed by the lane 8 deep audit of `core/rofl_archive.py`.** All three writers
now delegate: `_atomic_write_json` and `_atomic_write_bytes` call
`core/polled_json.py`'s public `atomic_write_json` / `atomic_write_bytes`, and
`_atomic_copy` keeps `shutil.copy2` (so a 13 MB replay is not pulled through
memory) while taking its scratch name from `_scratch_path` and its rename from
`_replace_with_retry`. The retry and the naming are therefore single-sourced
rather than re-rolled, which is what let them diverge in the first place.

**The row was RIGHT about the cause and INCOMPLETE about the damage.** Alongside
the bare replace, the same three writers derived the scratch name from the
destination alone (`target.with_suffix(target.suffix + ".tmp")`), so every
writer of a given `index.json` opened one shared `index.json.tmp` - the defect
`core/polled_json.py` fixed for ITSELF in lane 8 cycle 24 and never propagated
here. `download_replays` has nine non-test callers including two scheduled
tasks, so two of them sweeping one archive_dir was a live interleave, not a
hypothetical. Guarded by `test_index_write_retries_a_windows_permission_error`
(a real PermissionError injected at `os.replace`) and
`test_writers_never_use_the_shared_deterministic_scratch_name` (structural, the
shape polled_json's own cycle-24 guards use because a contention test is
probabilistic). Both mutation-proven.

**Sibling population NOT fixed here** (lane 8 audits one file): the same
deterministic-scratch shape is live at `agents/agent1_lead/scheduler.py:186`,
`agents/agent2_backend/pipeline/orchestrator.py:82`,
`agents/agent2_backend/smb_push.py:169`,
`agents/agent4_coach_mentor/{cold_streak_detector.py:65,insight_detector.py:62,ui_applier.py:92}`,
`agents/_supervisor_ephemeral.py:106`, `coaches/experimental_builder.py:121,353`,
`coaches/_base_coach.py:94` (FROZEN) and `coach_integration/_coach.py:118,713`.
The last of those is already filed as RM-261; the rest need a row of their own,
and severity varies by whether the file is genuinely multi-writer - do not sweep
them on the strength of the grep alone.

_(original filing kept below)_

### RM-310 - core/rofl_archive.py sidecar writes use a bare os.replace (LANE 8, Tier-1)

Filed 2026-08-31 by lane 8 cycle 45 off the `core/rofl_stats_backfill.py` audit
(LEDGER 1311). NOT fixed in-slice: it is a different file, and lane 8's unit of work
is one file plus the direct callers whose contract changed. `rofl_archive` is neither.

**Finding.** `core/rofl_archive.py` `_atomic_write_json` (and two siblings) finish with a
bare `tmp.replace(target)` at `:812`, `:823` and `:839`. On Windows `os.replace` raises
`PermissionError` (WinError 5) when any reader holds the target open - a poller reading
the file for a few milliseconds is enough. `core/polled_json.py:41` `_replace_with_retry`
already solves this with bounded backoff (~275 ms worst case, then re-raise) and its own
comment cites `reference_os_replace_winerror5`; the rofl writer predates or missed it.

**Severity is bounded and stated honestly.** Sidecars are read on demand by the backfill,
not polled continuously by an overlay, so the collision window is far narrower than for
`data/*.json`. This is a latent robustness gap, NOT a measured live failure: no WinError-5
has been observed on this path. Cycle 45 hardened the READER against a torn/short file,
which is the consequence; this row is the cause.

**Acceptance.** The three sites route through `core.polled_json._replace_with_retry` (or
`atomic_write_json`), plus a regression test that holds the target open and asserts the
write still lands rather than raising. Mutation-test it: remove the retry and confirm RED.

- **RM-379 OPEN (filed 2026-09-06, LANE 10 queue cycle 12, found by the RM-359 sibling sweep; LANE 7 or LANE 8, Tier-1) - three `scripts/` DDragon asset downloaders interpolate a wire-supplied `image.full` straight into a filesystem path, and one of them runs as a REGISTERED SCHEDULED TASK.** RM-359 fixed the "a validator exists and one call site skips it" shape in `lib/icons/downloader.py` and its twin `tools/ddragon_mirror_refresh.py`. This row is the adjacent-but-DISTINCT shape the same sweep turned up: modules that define **no validator at all**. (a) **`scripts/data_pipeline.py` is the live one.** `:507-510` and `:516-519` repeat the exact `Path(tree.get("icon","")).name` anti-pattern RM-359 just removed, and `:550-552` (champions) plus `:568-570` (summoner spells) are WORSE - they do not flatten at all: `icon_file = info.get("image",{}).get("full", ...)` then `dest = champ_dir / icon_file`, and `_download_icon` (`:160`) -> `_write_bytes` (`:140-143`) writes `dest` directly, so an `image.full` of `../../../evil.png` lands outside `data/icons/champions`. The bundles are CDN-downloaded by `cmd_ddragon` in the same file (`:83-95`). **Reachability VERIFIED LIVE on this box, and this corrects the sweep that found it:** the sweeping agent reported "no `RC-PatchRefresh` installer exists in this tree", but `Get-ScheduledTask` shows **`RC-PatchRefresh` registered and Ready**, executing `pythonw.exe "C:\Riot Commander\scripts\data_pipeline.py" all`, and `cmd_all` at `:768-769` calls `cmd_runes()` and `cmd_icons()`. So this is scheduled production code, not an operator-run script - re-probe the task list, never infer reachability from the absence of an installer. (b) `scripts/cache_ddragon_assets.py:54-56` and `:61-63` - `img = (info.get("image") or {}).get("full") or f"{iid}.png"` then `dst = dst_root / "img" / "item" / img`, with `fetch` (`:31`) doing `dest.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)` first. (c) `scripts/audit_ddragon_items.py:115`, `:127`, `:188` - weakest of the family, two hops from the wire and behind a `--download` flag; include it only for completeness. **ACCEPTANCE:** every one of those sites routes its wire-supplied name through a validator with the semantics of `lib/icons/downloader.py:_safe_basename` / `_safe_relpath`, and each touched module gets a test asserting a hostile `image.full` (`.`, `..`, `../../x.png`, a backslash form) is rejected AND that a legitimate multi-segment rune path still downloads - the second half is load-bearing, because applying `_safe_basename` to a rune path silently reduces the downloader to a no-op and a count of 0 reads as an empty payload (RM-359 pinned exactly that). Sweep the WRITE CALL, not the string `data_pipeline` (`feedback_pattern_scoped_sweep_is_not_a_root_cause_sweep`). **Not already closed:** RM-372 (`BACKLOG.md:205`) names `scripts/cache_ddragon_assets.py` for its **version** string only; the `img` filename half is not in that row. Co-locate the two - they are one visit. No `ENGINE_VERSION`, no Share mirror, no live gate.

These are the siblings of that exact shape, each re-verified by hand this cycle rather than taken from the sweeping agent. **(a) The highest-value one, because it is a REACHABILITY claim and not just a number:** `config/CONFIG_AUTHORITY.md:24`, `:87` and `:135` all state that `config/feature_flags.json` is **"Read ONLY by: `core/feature_policy.py`"**. `core/config_validator.py` also reads and validates it - `:472` `_FEATURE_FLAGS_REL = "config/feature_flags.json"`, `:489`, `:551`. - **RM-381 OPEN (filed 2026-09-06, LANE 10 queue cycle 14, the measured RESIDUE of RM-364; LANE 8, Tier-1) - seven Anthropic prompt builders still interpolate wire or model text with no sanitisation, and they are now pinned by a guard rather than remembered.** RM-364 sanitised the five RANKED exposures at prompt assembly and shipped a census guard (`tests/test_rm364_prompt_sanitizer_population.py`) that partitions every production module carrying an Anthropic egress call into three buckets. This row IS the `_RESIDUAL_RM381` bucket, and the guard's `test_residual_bucket_members_do_not_silently_become_sanitized` goes RED the moment any member is fixed, which forces the fixer to move it into `_SANITIZED` with a reason instead of letting the list decay into a stale accusation. **The members, each with the reason it sits below the ranked set:** `coaches/brawl_coach.py` (champion name lands in the SYSTEM block at `:365`, not just the user turn); `coaches/replay_coach.py` (Match-V5 rows; the existing caps bound LENGTH only, never content); `coaches/experimental_builder.py` (prior Haiku output re-fed to Haiku); `coaches/aram_team_analyzer.py`; `tft/tft_coach_engine.py` and `tft/tft_pbe_engine.py` (LiveClient-derived state fields); `agents/agent7_context/warm_session.py`. **GRANULARITY, stated so the RM-364 buckets are not read as stronger than they are:** membership of `_SANITIZED` is FILE-level and means only that the fields RM-364 ranked are cleaned at assembly in that file. `_run_coach` in BOTH `coaches/aram_coach.py` and `coaches/arena_coach.py` is a second, lower-ranked builder in an already-`_SANITIZED` file and is NOT covered - it is part of this residue, and the file-level guard cannot see it. **THREE REACHABILITY PROBES, run THIS cycle rather than inherited, and they corroborate census 2 against census 1** (RM-364 recorded both unaveraged and ordered a probe rather than an average): (a) `coaches/experimental_builder.py` - `mark_active` (`:347`) is the sole writer of the `data/experimental_active.json` gate marker and has **ZERO callers** anywhere outside its own docstring at `:42` and its own failure log at `:362`; the marker is **absent from disk** on this box. (c) `coaches/aram_team_analyzer.py:201` has no production caller in `dashboard/`, `web/`, `app/`, `modes/`, `mc/` or `agents/` and is test-only today. **What those probes do NOT settle, named so the next visit does not overclaim:** the caller chain of `experimental_builder._call_haiku` (`:200`) was not traced to a live entry point, so "the marker is never written" is measured while "the builder is unreachable" is not - `feedback_scan_is_not_a_reachability_probe`. **ACCEPTANCE:** each member is either (i) sanitised at prompt ASSEMBLY - never at `messages.create`, because RM-364 measured that `core.moon_proxy.get_coaching` is the PRIMARY egress for the TFT builders and the SDK call only the fallback, so an API-call-side guard sits on a dead path - with a red-first test asserting an injection string in the named field does not reach the prompt with raw newlines or an unmarked role token, and the member MOVED from `_RESIDUAL_RM381` into `_SANITIZED`; or (ii) DELETED, if the reachability probe above is completed and returns dead - which for two of them is the likelier honest answer, and removing a dead Haiku call site is worth more than hardening it. The two `_run_coach` builders need the guard granularity lifted from file-level to builder-level, or they will stay invisible to it. **Do NOT "fix" the three entries RM-364's census REFUTED** - `modes/shared_vision.py:400` and `tft/tft_vision_reader.py` send one image block plus an RC-authored class constant (the untrusted input there is PIXELS, which `prompt_sanitize` cannot address; re-scope as image-injection or drop), and `core/moon_proxy.py` is a transport that never calls `messages.create`, is FROZEN, and carries a prompt a builder already assembled. **Threat model, inherited from RM-364's second census and worth restating because it sharpens the whole row:** no summoner name, riot ID, chat or queue name reaches any prompt - those are read as identity-match keys and discarded - so the genuinely unconstrained bytes are VISION/OCR MODEL OUTPUT.
